## Constitutional Declaration

This document is the supreme constitutional authority of the AI Trading Brain. Every architecture document, every operational layer, every agent, every automated decision, every trade, every learning event, and every memory record must comply with the principles, invariants, and governance rules defined herein. No component of the system may operate in violation of this constitution. No instruction — human or automated — overrides a constitutional invariant.

The IIOS does not replace any prior architecture document. It governs them. It orchestrates them. It integrates them. It defines the rules by which they interact, the sequences in which they operate, the authorities that resolve conflicts between them, and the principles under which they must evolve. Every prior document is a chapter in a larger work; this document is the constitution under which all chapters operate.

The nine prior architecture documents answer what the system knows, what information it processes, what entities it recognises, how relationships are structured, what events it processes, how it reasons, how it decides, how it learns, and how it remembers. This document answers how the complete intelligence operates — how all nine prior documents coordinate, in what sequence, under what authority, with what constraints, and toward what constitutional purpose.

## PART I — THE PHILOSOPHY OF THE OPERATING SYSTEM

### 1.1 What Is an Investment Intelligence Operating System?

An Investment Intelligence Operating System (IIOS) is the supreme constitutional framework that defines, coordinates, and governs the complete intelligence lifecycle of an autonomous investment system. The IIOS is not software. It is not an artificial intelligence model. It is not a database schema. It is not a trading algorithm. It is not a collection of agents. It is not a cloud platform. It is not a software framework.

The IIOS is the constitutional document that answers a single, supreme question: **How does the complete intelligence operate?**

Not: How does the code execute?
Not: How does the model predict?
Not: How does the agent act?
Not: How does the database store?
But: How does the intelligence think, perceive, reason, decide, execute, learn, remember, and evolve as a unified, coherent, self-governing system — perpetually, correctly, and in service of a defined investment purpose?

The IIOS defines the rules, structures, sequences, authorities, constraints, and principles within which all intelligent operations must occur. It does not implement those operations. It constitutes them. The difference is profound: implementation is how something is built; constitution is the law under which everything built must operate.

An implementation can be changed. A constitution can only be amended — with deliberation, justification, and authority. Implementations are contingent. Constitutional principles are intended to be permanent. This is the essential distinction that elevates the IIOS above every other system concept in the hierarchy.

#### 1.1.1 The Nine Distinguishing Properties of an IIOS

| Property | Definition | Why Required |
|---|---|---|
| **Perpetuity** | The system operates in continuous cycles without stopping — each cycle terminates and immediately initiates the next | Markets never pause. Information never stops arriving. An intelligence that pauses loses ground to an evolving market. Perpetuity is the baseline operating condition. |
| **Self-Improvement** | Every operational cycle produces learning that is incorporated into all subsequent cycles — the system measurably improves over time | Markets evolve continuously. A static intelligence degrades relative to an evolving market. Self-improvement is the only mechanism for sustained performance over time. |
| **Constitutionality** | The system is governed by its own written principles — no operation, however profitable-seeming, may violate the constitution | Without self-governance, intelligence drifts toward short-term optimisation at the expense of long-term integrity. A constitution prevents corruption of purpose. |
| **Completeness** | The system integrates every relevant knowledge domain — no domain is excluded on grounds of convenience or simplicity | Partial intelligence produces partial answers. Markets reward complete understanding. Exclusion of any relevant domain creates a structural blind spot that compounds over time. |
| **Auditability** | Every output is traceable to its reasoning, its evidence, its governing authority, and its constitutional basis | Unexplainable decisions are unjustifiable decisions. Auditability is not optional — it is the only mechanism for detecting systematic error and correcting it before it destroys capital. |
| **Resilience** | The system continues operating under partial component failure — no single component failure stops the intelligence | Markets operate continuously. Intelligence failure during market hours produces opportunity cost and, worse, leaves open positions unmonitored. Resilience is an operational requirement. |
| **Explainability** | Every output includes a human-readable reasoning chain connecting evidence to conclusion through documented intermediate steps | Black-box intelligence cannot be trusted, cannot be improved, and cannot be governed. Explainability enables learning from errors and enables meaningful human oversight. |
| **Epistemic Humility** | Every output quantifies the uncertainty in that output — no claim of certainty 1.0 or 0.0 is ever made about any market prediction | Overconfidence is the most common cause of catastrophic investment loss. Epistemic humility forces calibrated risk management and prevents false certainty from corrupting position sizing. |
| **Evolutionary Capacity** | The architecture evolves over time — new capabilities are added, new domains integrated, new principles formulated — while historical integrity is always preserved | Intelligence must grow as markets grow in complexity. But the foundation of future intelligence is the historical record of past decisions. Evolution without preservation destroys the learning base. |

---

### 1.2 The Conceptual Hierarchy — How IIOS Differs From All Prior Concepts

The following table defines nine distinct system concepts and positions the IIOS at the apex of the hierarchy. Every concept below the IIOS is either a component of the IIOS, a substrate for the IIOS, or a predecessor concept that the IIOS integrates and transcends.

| Concept | Definition | Scope | Intelligence Level | Self-Governing | Evolves | Owns History | Constitutional | Governs Others |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Software** | Executable instructions performing defined functions on defined inputs | Single function | None | No | No — requires rewrite | No | No | No |
| **Platform** | Shared infrastructure hosting multiple software services and applications | Infrastructure | None | No | Limited — via updates | No | No | No |
| **Framework** | Structural pattern constraining how applications must be designed and built | Architectural pattern | None | No | Limited — via versioning | No | No | No |
| **AI Model** | Learned statistical mapping from input spaces to output spaces, produced by training on historical data | Narrow prediction | Narrow | No | Via periodic retraining only | No | No | No |
| **Agent** | Autonomous perceiver-actor that perceives environment states and takes goal-directed actions | Specific action domain | Narrow | Partial — via reward shaping | Via reinforcement learning | Partial | No | No |
| **Knowledge System** | Organised, queryable repository of facts, relationships, rules, and assertions about a domain | Domain knowledge storage | None | No | Via curation and manual update | Partial | No | No |
| **Decision System** | System that evaluates alternatives and selects actions based on defined objectives and criteria | Decision for a defined scenario | Narrow | No | Via threshold tuning | No | No | No |
| **Operating System** | Software foundation that manages hardware resources and provides services to application software | Resource coordination | None | Self-maintaining | Via version updates | No | No | No |
| **Investment Intelligence Operating System** | Constitutional framework governing the complete intelligence lifecycle — knowledge, reasoning, decision, learning, and memory — for autonomous investment intelligence | Complete intelligence domain | Full | Fully self-governing | Perpetually and constitutionally | Always — immutable history | Yes — supreme authority | Yes — all components |

#### 1.2.1 The Five Critical Distinctions

The following five distinctions separate the IIOS from all predecessor concepts. These distinctions are not differences of degree — they are differences of kind.

**Distinction 1: Governance vs. Function**
Every predecessor concept performs a function. Software executes. Models predict. Agents act. Knowledge systems store. The IIOS governs. It does not perform any function itself — it defines the laws under which all functions are performed. This distinction is absolute: a function can be changed, replaced, or optimised; a governance principle must be amended with constitutional deliberation.

**Distinction 2: Constitutionality vs. Configuration**
Every predecessor concept is configured — parameters are set, thresholds are tuned, architectures are designed. The IIOS is constituted — its governing principles are written as constitutional law, not as configuration parameters. Configuration can be changed by an administrator. Constitutional principles can only be amended through the defined amendment process.

**Distinction 3: Perpetuity vs. Invocation**
Every predecessor concept is invoked — it runs when called and stops when done. The IIOS operates perpetually. It defines an endless cycle in which every termination is simultaneously the initiation of the next cycle. The intelligence never stops; it only pauses between cycles, and even those pauses are constitutional.

**Distinction 4: Complete Intelligence vs. Narrow Intelligence**
Every predecessor concept exhibits intelligence within a narrow domain. The IIOS integrates all domains into a complete intelligence. A complete intelligence does not excel in one domain and ignore others — it maintains coherent, coordinated understanding across all relevant domains simultaneously.

**Distinction 5: Self-Governance vs. External Governance**
Every predecessor concept is governed by something external — a human administrator, a configuration file, an orchestration script. The IIOS governs itself by its own written constitution. It knows its own principles, monitors its own compliance, and escalates its own violations. No external governor is required for constitutional compliance.

---

### 1.3 Why the Investment Intelligence Operating System Exists

The IIOS exists because no subset of intelligent components — however sophisticated — constitutes a complete intelligence. A system of isolated components is not an intelligence. It is a collection of parts. Intelligence emerges only when all parts operate under a unified constitutional framework that defines their relationships, their sequences, their authorities, and their collective purpose.

Seven reasons make the IIOS necessary. Each reason is necessary. None is sufficient alone.

#### Reason 1: To Unify Nine Architectures Into One Intelligence

The nine prior architecture documents define individual capabilities with great depth and precision. MASTER_KNOWLEDGE_ARCHITECTURE defines what is known. INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY defines what information is. ENTITY_ONTOLOGY defines what entities exist. RELATIONSHIP_ONTOLOGY defines how entities relate. EVENT_ONTOLOGY defines what events occur. REASONING_ARCHITECTURE defines how the system reasons. DECISION_ARCHITECTURE defines how decisions are made. LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE defines how the system learns. MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE defines how the system remembers.

Nine excellent architectures, each correct in isolation, do not automatically produce an integrated intelligence. The IIOS answers questions that no single document can answer: In what sequence do these architectures operate? What happens when two architectures produce conflicting outputs? Who has authority to resolve conflicts? How do failures in one architecture affect others? How does learning in one domain propagate to reasoning in another? How does memory of one event affect decisions about a different event? None of the nine documents answers these questions. The IIOS does.

#### Reason 2: To Establish the Perpetual Operating Cycle

Intelligence is not a one-time computation. Intelligence is a perpetual cycle: perception, reasoning, decision, action, outcome, learning, memory — each cycle improving the next. No single architecture document defines this complete cycle. REASONING_ARCHITECTURE defines how to reason but not when. DECISION_ARCHITECTURE defines how to decide but not what triggers a decision. LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE defines how to learn but not when learning initiates. The IIOS defines the complete perpetual cycle — the exact sequence in which every architecture operates, the precise triggers and termination conditions for each stage, and the invariants that must hold throughout.

#### Reason 3: To Define Constitutional Authority

In any complex system, conflicts arise continuously. A risk model signals danger while a reward model signals opportunity. A memory record contradicts a new observation. A learning event suggests a principle that conflicts with an existing principle. A human instruction conflicts with an active risk limit. Without a constitutional authority that defines resolution protocols, these conflicts produce inconsistent, unpredictable behaviour — and in an investment system, inconsistency costs capital.

The IIOS is the constitutional authority. It defines, once and for all, how all conflicts are resolved, in what sequence authorities are consulted, what principles take precedence over others, and which conflicts require human escalation. With the IIOS, every conflict has a resolution protocol. Without the IIOS, every conflict is an unresolved ambiguity.

#### Reason 4: To Prevent Optimisation Pathologies

Every component of an investment intelligence is designed to optimise something. Reasoning optimises logical validity. Decision-making optimises expected returns. Learning optimises prediction accuracy. Memory optimises retrieval efficiency. Risk management optimises capital preservation. But a system of locally optimising components can produce globally pathological behaviour: overfitting, confirmation bias, recency bias, circular reasoning, false confidence, catastrophic forgetting, and catastrophic concentration.

The IIOS defines 60 system invariants that prevent each class of optimisation pathology. No component may optimise in a way that violates an invariant. This constraint is not a limitation on performance — it is the mechanism that makes sustained performance possible.

#### Reason 5: To Enable Institutional Memory

The value of an investment intelligence is not in any single correct decision. It is in the accumulation of knowledge over thousands of decisions — in the institutional memory of what worked, what failed, why, under what conditions, and with what confidence. Institutional memory requires that all knowledge be properly attributed, validated, stored, and retrievable. It requires that learning from one domain be accessible to reasoning in another domain. It requires that the history of decisions be preserved in a form that supports future learning. The IIOS defines the architecture of institutional memory — not just how individual memory records are stored, but how the entire intelligence accumulates wisdom over time.

#### Reason 6: To Define Human-Machine Authority Boundaries

An autonomous investment intelligence that operates without clear human authority boundaries is not a tool — it is a liability. The IIOS defines, with constitutional precision, exactly which decisions require human approval, under what conditions human override is permitted, how human instructions are validated before execution, and how human overrides are recorded for audit. The IIOS ensures that human authority is never bypassed and that machine autonomy is never unlimited. This is not a restriction on the intelligence — it is the social contract that makes trusting the intelligence possible.

#### Reason 7: To Enable Constitutional Self-Governance

An intelligence that cannot govern itself cannot be trusted. The IIOS enables constitutional self-governance: the system knows its own principles, monitors its own compliance, detects its own violations, and escalates detected violations for resolution. This self-governance is not merely self-monitoring — it is self-correction. The intelligence is designed to detect when it is operating incorrectly and to initiate the correction process without waiting for human instruction.

Constitutional self-governance is not optional for an investment intelligence that handles real capital. It is the minimum standard for trustworthy autonomous operation in financial markets where mistakes are immediate, irreversible, and expensive.

### 1.4 Core Objectives of the IIOS

The IIOS pursues twelve core objectives. These objectives are not aspirational — they are constitutional requirements. Every component of the AI Trading Brain must be designed and operated to advance all twelve objectives simultaneously. Advancing one objective at the expense of another is a constitutional violation.

| # | Objective | Statement | Success Criterion | Failure Indicator |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| OBJ-01 | **Integrated Intelligence** | All nine architecture documents operate as a unified intelligence, not as isolated components — every operation flows through the complete pipeline | Every output is traceable through the complete intelligence pipeline | Any output that bypasses a required pipeline stage |
| OBJ-02 | **Perpetual Operation** | The intelligence operates continuously through all market sessions without manual intervention or unscheduled interruption | System uptime ≥ 99.5% during all market hours | Any unscheduled downtime during market hours |
| OBJ-03 | **Continuous Self-Improvement** | Every completed cycle produces measurable improvement in at least one performance dimension across the rolling measurement window | Learning event rate > 0 per session; performance trend positive over 30-day rolling window | Zero learning events in any session; negative performance trend over 30 days |
| OBJ-04 | **Constitutional Compliance** | All 60 system invariants are respected in every operation — no operation violates any invariant | Invariant violation count = 0; any detected violation triggers immediate escalation | Any confirmed invariant violation |
| OBJ-05 | **Explainable Intelligence** | Every decision, every reasoning output, every learning conclusion, every memory update is fully traceable and explainable in human-readable form | No output exists without a complete audit trail | Any output without a complete audit trail |
| OBJ-06 | **Calibrated Uncertainty** | Every output includes a calibrated confidence measure — no output is presented as certain | Confidence calibration error < 10% across all output classes | Calibration error > 15% in any output class |
| OBJ-07 | **Risk Primacy** | Risk management overrides return optimisation in every conflict — no trade is executed in violation of any active risk limit regardless of conviction score | Zero trades executed in violation of active risk limits | Any trade executed against an active risk limit |
| OBJ-08 | **Institutional Memory** | Knowledge accumulates without loss — every experience is preserved in a form that supports future learning | Memory completeness ≥ 0.95; all learning events archived | Any loss of decision history; any unarchived learning event |
| OBJ-09 | **Human Authority Preservation** | Human authority over the system is never diminished — all human instructions are respected within constitutional bounds | Human override capability always active; no autonomous decision overrides explicit human instruction | Any instance of autonomous system ignoring explicit human instruction |
| OBJ-10 | **Audit Completeness** | Every action is auditable from first information input to final learning output | Audit trail completeness ≥ 0.99 for all operations | Audit trail completeness < 0.95 for any operation class |
| OBJ-11 | **Architectural Integrity** | The architecture evolves without breaking prior integrity — all changes are additive, not destructive | No change removes an existing capability; all changes preserve historical compatibility | Any architecture change that removes a prior capability or breaks historical records |
| OBJ-12 | **Convergence Toward Wisdom** | Over time, the intelligence moves from data to information to knowledge to wisdom — making better decisions with progressively less information and more insight | Decision quality improves monotonically over long-term rolling windows | Any sustained deterioration in decision quality over rolling 90-day windows |

### 1.5 Fundamental Philosophy — The Five Pillars

The IIOS is grounded in five philosophical pillars. These pillars are not design preferences — they are constitutional commitments that cannot be traded away for performance, convenience, or efficiency.

#### Pillar 1: Truth Has Primacy Over Preference

The investment intelligence must pursue accurate understanding of reality regardless of whether that understanding is comfortable, profitable, or consistent with prior beliefs. A system that prefers confirming evidence over disconfirming evidence is not an intelligence — it is a bias engine. A system that adjusts its beliefs to match its preferences is not learning — it is confabulating.

The IIOS requires that all information be evaluated on evidential merit alone. Prior beliefs must be updated when evidence demands. Uncomfortable conclusions must be drawn when evidence supports them. Every mechanism in the system — from information acquisition to reasoning to memory — must be designed to promote accurate understanding rather than preferred outcomes.

Truth Primacy has three operational consequences: (1) Evidence evaluation must be independent of the current belief state. (2) Belief updates must be monotone with respect to evidence quality — stronger evidence produces larger belief updates. (3) No conclusion may be suppressed because it is inconvenient for a current position.

#### Pillar 2: Process Integrity Over Outcome Optimisation

The investment intelligence must follow its defined processes with complete fidelity, even when deviation would produce a better outcome in a specific case. A system that bends its processes when outcomes justify it is a system without process integrity — and a system without process integrity cannot be trusted, cannot be audited, and cannot be improved.

Process integrity means: if the reasoning process requires four stages of evidence validation, all four stages are executed regardless of how confident the system already is. If the decision process requires risk approval before execution, risk approval is always obtained. If the learning process requires outcome observation before lesson extraction, no lesson is extracted before the outcome is observed.

Process integrity is the only foundation for audit, learning, and improvement. A system that sometimes skips steps produces audit trails with gaps, learning signals that are uncalibrated, and performance metrics that cannot be trusted.

#### Pillar 3: Uncertainty Is Information, Not Weakness

The investment intelligence must quantify and express uncertainty in every output. Uncertainty is not a sign of inadequate intelligence — it is a sign of calibrated intelligence. A system that expresses false certainty is more dangerous than a system that honestly expresses uncertainty, because false certainty produces position sizing that is incommensurate with actual risk.

The IIOS requires that every prediction include a confidence interval, every decision include a conviction score with calibrated uncertainty bounds, every reasoning conclusion include a confidence level, and every memory retrieval include a reliability score. Uncertainty that cannot be quantified must be flagged as such — it cannot be treated as zero.

Expressing uncertainty is not weakness. Suppressing uncertainty is the greatest weakness an investment intelligence can exhibit.

#### Pillar 4: History Is the Foundation of Future Intelligence

The investment intelligence must treat every historical decision, outcome, and learning event as the permanent foundation of future intelligence. History cannot be altered, deleted, selectively preserved, or selectively forgotten. Every completed investment cycle — however bad the outcome — is a learning asset. Destroying history destroys learning capacity.

The IIOS requires that all decision history be preserved in immutable form, that all outcome records be linked to the decisions that produced them, that all learning events be traceable to the outcomes that triggered them, and that all memory records be attributable to the events that created them. The chain from event to decision to outcome to learning to memory must be unbroken for every completed cycle.

History is not a burden to be managed — it is the most valuable asset the intelligence possesses.

#### Pillar 5: Evolution Is Constitutional, Not Opportunistic

The investment intelligence must evolve in accordance with its constitutional architecture. New capabilities may be added, new domains may be integrated, new principles may be formulated — but all evolution must be additive, documented, backward-compatible, and constitutionally compliant. No evolutionary change may remove a prior capability, break a historical record, or contradict a constitutional principle.

Opportunistic evolution — adding capabilities because they seem useful, removing capabilities because they seem expensive, changing architectures because a new technique is trendy — is constitutionally prohibited. Every architectural change must be motivated by a documented improvement in accuracy, completeness, resilience, or explanatory power.

Evolution governed by constitution is sustainable. Evolution driven by opportunity is unstable.

### 1.6 Design Goals

| # | Goal | Statement | Metric | Target |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G-01 | **Comprehensiveness** | The IIOS integrates all knowledge domains relevant to investment intelligence — no relevant domain is intentionally excluded | Domain coverage score | 100% of identified domains integrated |
| G-02 | **Coherence** | All components of the IIOS produce outputs that are mutually consistent — no two components produce contradictory authoritative outputs simultaneously | Consistency rate | > 99.9% of all concurrent outputs consistent |
| G-03 | **Traceability** | Every output produced by any component of the IIOS can be traced to its inputs, its reasoning process, and its governing principle | Traceability score | 100% of outputs fully traceable |
| G-04 | **Proportionality** | The intelligence applied to any decision is proportional to the magnitude and reversibility of that decision — small decisions use minimal intelligence resources; large decisions use full intelligence resources | Intelligence allocation efficiency | Within 10% of theoretically optimal allocation |
| G-05 | **Adaptability** | The IIOS adapts to changing market conditions without requiring human reconfiguration of its fundamental architecture | Adaptation rate | Parameter drift detected and corrected within 5 trading sessions |
| G-06 | **Legibility** | Every component of the IIOS can be understood by a knowledgeable human without requiring access to its internal code | Legibility test passage rate | 100% of architectural components documented in human-readable form |
| G-07 | **Composability** | New capabilities can be added to the IIOS without modifying existing components — the architecture grows by composition, not by replacement | Composition success rate | 100% of new capabilities added without breaking existing components |
| G-08 | **Predictability** | The IIOS behaves predictably — given the same inputs and system state, it produces the same class of outputs | Predictability score | Behavioural variance < 5% for identical inputs |
| G-09 | **Fail-Safe Operation** | When any component of the IIOS fails, the system falls into a safe state rather than an unsafe state — uncertainty produces caution, not action | Fail-safe activation rate | 100% of component failures produce safe state |
| G-10 | **Long-Term Sustainability** | The IIOS architecture is sustainable over a multi-year time horizon — it does not accumulate technical debt that prevents future evolution | Architecture health score | No blocking architectural debt accumulated over any 12-month period |

### 1.7 Design Constraints

| # | Constraint | Statement | Justification |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-01 | **No Speculative Architecture** | Architecture components may not be added speculatively — every component must serve a documented, tested function | Speculative components add complexity without value; complexity degrades reliability |
| C-02 | **No Destructive Evolution** | No architectural change may remove or break an existing component — evolution is always additive | Removing components destroys the learning and reasoning chains built on them |
| C-03 | **No Uncalibrated Confidence** | No component may produce confidence estimates that are not empirically calibrated against historical performance | Uncalibrated confidence produces systematically incorrect position sizing |
| C-04 | **No Silent Failure** | No component failure may be suppressed — all failures are surfaced, logged, and escalated according to severity | Silent failures accumulate into undetected systemic risk |
| C-05 | **No Circular Governance** | No component may govern itself alone — every component is governed by at least one external authority | Self-governance without external check produces uncorrectable bias |
| C-06 | **No Historical Modification** | Historical records — trades, decisions, learning events, memory records — may never be altered after creation | History modification destroys the validity of all future learning |
| C-07 | **No Unlimited Autonomy** | No component may make decisions of consequence without a defined human authority check at some level | Unlimited machine autonomy in financial markets is a systemic liability |
| C-08 | **No Unexplained Output** | No component may produce an output without a documented reasoning chain | Unexplained outputs cannot be audited, improved, or trusted |
| C-09 | **No Complexity for Its Own Sake** | Architectural complexity is permitted only when it produces a documented capability improvement — not for theoretical completeness | Unnecessary complexity degrades reliability without adding intelligence |
| C-10 | **No Outcome-Driven Process** | Process steps may not be skipped even when the outcome is already clear — process integrity is unconditional | Outcome-driven process skipping degrades audit completeness and learning signal quality |

---

### 1.8 Constitutional Vision

The constitutional vision of the IIOS is the convergence of investment intelligence toward what may be called **Calibrated Wisdom** — the state in which the intelligence makes consistently excellent investment decisions not because it has perfect information, but because it has learned, over many market cycles, how to extract maximum insight from imperfect information, how to act decisively under uncertainty with calibrated confidence, and how to learn from every outcome without overfitting to any particular pattern.

Calibrated wisdom is not achieved at launch. It is the destination of a continuous, constitutional, multi-year journey. Each trading cycle moves the intelligence incrementally closer to calibrated wisdom by adding one more verified learning event, one more calibrated memory record, one more tested reasoning pattern. Calibrated wisdom is never fully achieved — because markets are never fully understood — but the architecture is designed so that every cycle of operation moves the intelligence measurably closer.

The four epochs of IIOS evolution:

| Epoch | Name | Characteristics | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Epoch I** | Structured Learning | Architecture is established; every component learns its operational baseline; performance is monitored against benchmarks; no autonomous variation | Months 1–6 |
| **Epoch II** | Adaptive Intelligence | Components begin adapting to market conditions; learning events accumulate; reasoning patterns are validated; decision quality improves measurably | Months 7–18 |
| **Epoch III** | Institutional Wisdom | The intelligence has accumulated sufficient decision history to reason by analogy from institutional memory; novel market situations are matched to historical archetypes; performance is consistently above baseline | Months 19–36 |
| **Epoch IV** | Calibrated Mastery | The intelligence operates with deeply calibrated uncertainty, well-established reasoning patterns, rich institutional memory, and self-correcting learning that maintains performance through structural market shifts | Month 37 onward |

The transition between epochs is constitutional: each transition requires documented evidence of the capability improvements that define the next epoch. No transition is declared by schedule — only by evidence.

```
  │   PLANE      │      │   PLANE         │      │   PLANE          │
  │             │      │                 │      │                  │
  │ Phase 8:    │      │ Phase 9:        │      │ This document:  │
  │ How we      │      │ How we          │      │ How we govern   │
  │ learn       │      │ remember        │      │ all planes      │
  └─────────────┘      └─────────────────┘      └──────────────────┘

  CONSTITUTIONAL FLOW:
  Knowledge → Reasoning → Decision → Execution → Outcome → Learning → Memory → Knowledge

  GOVERNANCE FLOW:
  Constitution → Invariants → Principles → Governance Model → All Components

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
```

---

## PART II — SYSTEM OF SYSTEMS

### 2.0 Overview

The AI Trading Brain is a System of Systems: nine distinct architecture documents, each defining a complete subsystem, all operating under the governance of this IIOS constitution. This part provides full specifications for each subsystem — its purpose, responsibilities, inputs, outputs, dependencies, consumers, producers, failure impact, governance, and interactions with every other subsystem.

The nine subsystems are organised into three planes:

| Plane | Documents | Function |
|---|---|---|
| **Knowledge Plane** | MASTER_KNOWLEDGE, INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY, ENTITY_ONTOLOGY, RELATIONSHIP_ONTOLOGY, EVENT_ONTOLOGY | Define what reality is, what information exists, what entities act, how they relate, and what events occur |
| **Intelligence Plane** | REASONING_ARCHITECTURE, DECISION_ARCHITECTURE | Define how the system reasons about knowledge and transforms reasoning into decisions |
| **Evolution Plane** | LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE, MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE | Define how the system learns from decisions and preserves learning in long-term institutional memory |

The IIOS governs all three planes. It defines the interfaces between planes, the sequences of operation, the conflict resolution protocols, and the constitutional principles that apply to all planes simultaneously.

#### 2.0.1 Master Subsystem Overview Table

| # | Document | Plane | Primary Function | Constitutional Authority | Failure Class |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MASTER_KNOWLEDGE_ARCHITECTURE | Knowledge | Defines the complete knowledge universe | Knowledge authority | Knowledge corruption |
| 2 | INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY | Knowledge | Defines all information types and validation | Information authority | Data poisoning |
| 3 | ENTITY_ONTOLOGY | Knowledge | Defines all entities and their properties | Entity authority | Entity misidentification |
| 4 | RELATIONSHIP_ONTOLOGY | Knowledge | Defines all entity relationships | Relationship authority | Relationship corruption |
| 5 | EVENT_ONTOLOGY | Knowledge | Defines all events and their properties | Event authority | Event misclassification |
| 6 | REASONING_ARCHITECTURE | Intelligence | Defines all reasoning patterns and processes | Reasoning authority | Reasoning failure |
| 7 | DECISION_ARCHITECTURE | Intelligence | Defines all decision processes and approvals | Decision authority | Decision failure |
| 8 | LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE | Evolution | Defines all learning processes and governance | Learning authority | Learning failure |
| 9 | MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE | Evolution | Defines all memory types and lifecycle | Memory authority | Memory corruption |

| MKA-R02 | Knowledge Taxonomy | Establishes the taxonomy that organises all knowledge into coherent categories — the vocabulary that all subsequent documents use |
| MKA-R03 | Knowledge Quality Standards | Defines what constitutes valid, reliable, and trustworthy knowledge — the epistemological standards applied to all knowledge claims |
| MKA-R04 | Knowledge Acquisition Protocols | Defines how new knowledge enters the system — the validation gates that information must pass before being accepted as knowledge |
| MKA-R05 | Knowledge Boundary Definition | Defines the boundaries of the knowledge domain — what is within scope and what is outside scope |
| MKA-R06 | Cross-Domain Knowledge Integration | Defines how knowledge from different domains is integrated into a coherent whole — the synthesis rules |
| MKA-R07 | Knowledge Evolution Governance | Defines how the knowledge architecture itself evolves over time — the process for adding new knowledge domains |

#### 2.1.3 Inputs and Outputs

| Direction | Source/Target | Content | Format |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Input** | Reality (markets, economic data, corporate actions) | Raw observations that may become knowledge | Unstructured, unvalidated |
| **Input** | Human instruction | Curated domain expertise | Semi-structured |
| **Output** → Phase 2 | INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY | Knowledge domain definitions, taxonomy | Structured ontology |
| **Output** → Phase 6 | REASONING_ARCHITECTURE | Knowledge structure that reasoning operates within | Knowledge graph |
| **Output** → Phase 8 | LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE | Definition of what can be learned and how learning is organised | Learning domain specification |
| **Output** → Phase 9 | MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE | Definition of what must be remembered and how knowledge memories are structured | Memory domain specification |
| **Output** → IIOS | This document | Foundation for the complete knowledge plane | Constitutional reference |

#### 2.1.4 Failure Impact

| Failure Mode | Impact on System | Recovery Protocol |
|---|---|---|
| Knowledge domain incomplete | Reasoning operates in blind spots — entire market sectors are not understood | Extend knowledge domain definition; rebuild reasoning knowledge base |
| Taxonomy inconsistency | Downstream documents use inconsistent terminology — outputs are not comparable | Audit all document terminology; resolve inconsistencies; revalidate all cross-references |
| Quality standards too permissive | Invalid knowledge enters the system — reasoning is corrupted at the root | Tighten quality standards; purge invalid knowledge; revalidate all derived beliefs |
| Quality standards too restrictive | Valid knowledge is rejected — intelligence is artificially impoverished | Recalibrate quality standards; re-evaluate rejected knowledge |
| Boundary definition error | System attempts to reason in domains it has no knowledge of | Correct boundary definitions; add disclaimers for near-boundary reasoning |

| RELATIONSHIP_ONTOLOGY | Foundation → Structure | MKA → RO | Knowledge domains define what relationship types are meaningful |
| EVENT_ONTOLOGY | Foundation → Classification | MKA → EVO | Knowledge domains define what event categories are recognised |
| REASONING_ARCHITECTURE | Foundation → Process | MKA → RA | Knowledge structure defines what reasoning can operate on |

| LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE | Foundation → Scope | MKA → LA | Knowledge domains define what can be learned |

#### 2.2.4 Failure Impact

| Failure Mode | Downstream Impact | Severity | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| Data poisoning | Corrupted beliefs propagate through all reasoning and decisions | Critical | Quarantine affected information; identify all derived beliefs; rollback corrupted knowledge |
| Schema validation failure | Invalid data enters entity/relationship/event systems | High | Reject invalid data at validation gate; log rejection; alert |
| Stale data acceptance | Outdated information treated as current | High | Enforce timeliness checks; age all information; reject beyond threshold |
| Source reliability miscalibration | Low-quality sources over-weighted in reasoning | Medium | Recalibrate source reliability scores; re-weight evidence |
| Lineage chain broken | Outputs cannot be traced to sources | Medium | Reconstruct lineage from logs; flag outputs with incomplete lineage |

#### 2.4.3 Key Relationship Classes

| Class | Examples | Directionality | Strength Metric | Propagation Rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Causal** | Monetary policy → interest rates → bond prices | Directed | Lag coefficient + correlation | Linear along causal chain |
| **Correlational** | Sector peers co-movement, index members | Undirected | Rolling correlation coefficient | Network spreading |
| **Hierarchical** | Index → constituent, Sector → subsector → security | Directed (parent→child) | Membership weight | Aggregation upward |
| **Transactional** | Counterparty → transaction → counterparty | Directed (originator→recipient) | Transaction volume | Flow-based |
| **Regulatory** | Regulatory body → market → participant | Directed (regulator→regulated) | Compliance strength | Command-based |
| **Competitive** | Issuer vs. issuer within same sector | Undirected symmetric | Market share differential | Peer competition |
| **Substitution** | Security vs. substitute security | Undirected | Price elasticity of substitution | Cross-price elasticity |
| **Informational** | Information event → market participant belief update | Directed | Signal strength | Bayesian propagation |

#### 2.4.4 Failure Impact

| Failure | Impact | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| Relationship strength miscalibration | Contagion signals too strong or too weak — correlated position risk under/over-estimated | Recalibrate using rolling correlation windows; rebuild relationship network |
| Causal chain misidentification | Policy reasoning produces incorrect predictions — wrong attribution of market moves | Re-derive causal chains from first principles; rebuild causal graph |
| Relationship graph corruption | Reasoning uses incorrect entity relationships — recommendations violate sector/regulatory constraints | Audit relationship graph; purge corrupt edges; rebuild from validated data |
| Temporal dynamics failure | Stale relationships used for current decisions | Enforce relationship TTL; age-decay all relationship strengths |

#### 2.5.4 Event Impact on the Intelligence Pipeline

```
Event Detected
      │
      ▼
Event Classification (EVO)
      │
      ├── Entity Impact? ──► Entity State Update (EO)
      │                           │
      ├── Relationship Impact? ──► Relationship Update (RO)
      │                           │
      ├── Reasoning Trigger? ──►  Reasoning Activation (RA)
      │                           │
      ├── Decision Required? ──►  Decision Process (DA)
      │                           │
      ├── Learning Trigger? ──►   Learning Event (LA)
      │                           │
      └── Memory Record? ──────►  Memory Creation (MA)
```

#### 2.5.5 Failure Impact

| Failure Mode | Impact | Detection | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| Event missed | Intelligence operates without awareness of a significant market event | Post-hoc event audit against news feed | Replay event through pipeline; adjust detection sensitivity |
| Event misclassified | Wrong reasoning and decision process triggered | Outcome attribution reveals systematic misclassification | Retrain classification; audit all decisions from affected period |
| Impact magnitude error | Reasoning assigns wrong weight to event | Performance attribution post-mortem | Recalibrate impact models; update event impact coefficients |
| Causality chain error | Wrong sequence of subsequent events predicted | Prediction accuracy tracking | Rebuild causality graph from validated historical event sequences |

#### 2.6.1 Profile

| Primary Question | How does the intelligence reason about what it knows? |

| Document Size | 257,059 bytes |
| Authority Level | Reasoning plane authority — all reasoning processes governed by this document |
| Plane | Intelligence Plane |
| Dependencies | All five Knowledge Plane documents |
| Consumers | DECISION_ARCHITECTURE, LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE, MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE |

#### 2.6.2 Responsibilities

| # | Responsibility | Description |
|---|---|---|
| RA-R01 | Reasoning Pattern Library | Defines every reasoning pattern available to the intelligence — deductive, inductive, abductive, analogical, statistical, and domain-specific patterns |
| RA-R02 | Evidence Evaluation | Defines how evidence is collected, weighted, and integrated into conviction — the epistemological engine of the intelligence |
| RA-R03 | Hypothesis Generation | Defines how hypotheses are generated from observations — the creative component of reasoning |
| RA-R04 | Hypothesis Testing | Defines how hypotheses are tested against available evidence — the validation component |
| RA-R05 | Conviction Computation | Defines how raw evidence is transformed into a quantified conviction score — the output interface to the decision system |
| RA-R06 | Uncertainty Quantification | Defines how uncertainty is computed, expressed, and propagated through reasoning chains |
| RA-R07 | Reasoning Audit Trail | Defines how every reasoning chain is documented for auditability and learning |
| RA-R08 | Reasoning Failure Detection | Defines how the system detects its own reasoning failures — circular reasoning, confirmation bias, hasty generalisation |

#### 2.6.3 Reasoning Patterns

| Pattern | Trigger Condition | Knowledge Required | Output | Confidence Profile |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Deductive** | Known rule + observed condition | Formal rules + entity state | Guaranteed conclusion | High if premises are certain; inherits premise uncertainty |
| **Inductive** | Multiple observations of pattern | Historical event database | Probabilistic generalisation | Proportional to sample size and consistency |
| **Abductive** | Observation without known cause | Causal graph + entity state | Best-fit hypothesis | Low-medium; requires validation |
| **Analogical** | Current situation matches historical archetype | Memory — episode database | Prediction by analogy | Proportional to archetype match quality |
| **Statistical** | Quantitative data available | Price/volume history + factor models | Statistical inference | Proportional to data quality and model fit |
| **Causal** | Event with known causal structure | Causal graph | Predicted downstream effects | Proportional to causal model accuracy |
| **Adversarial** | Market move contrary to expectation | Full knowledge context | Devil's advocate hypothesis | Deliberately low — used to stress-test primary hypothesis |
| **Ensemble** | Multiple patterns active simultaneously | All available | Weighted composite hypothesis | Highest of all patterns — ensemble reduces variance |

#### 2.6.4 Conviction Computation Framework

The REASONING_ARCHITECTURE defines conviction as a composite score in [0.0, 10.0], computed as the evidence-weighted combination of all active reasoning pattern outputs.

```
Conviction Score = Σ(pattern_weight × pattern_confidence × evidence_quality)
                  / Σ(pattern_weight)

Where:
  pattern_weight    = predefined weight for each reasoning pattern (sum = 1.0)
  pattern_confidence = output confidence from each active reasoning pattern
  evidence_quality  = aggregate quality score of evidence used by pattern
```

Decision threshold for execution approval: Conviction ≥ 6.5 (defined in DECISION_ARCHITECTURE)

#### 2.6.5 Failure Impact

| Failure Mode | Impact | Detection | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| Circular reasoning | System confirms its own beliefs — no real evidence integration | Reasoning audit: check if evidence predates hypothesis | Break cycle by forcing adversarial reasoning; flag conclusion as unreliable |
| Confirmation bias | Only evidence supporting prior belief is integrated | Evidence set audit: compare confirming vs. disconfirming evidence ratio | Force evidence balance check: at least 20% of evidence must be disconfirming |
| Hasty generalisation | Insufficient evidence produces high-confidence conclusion | Sample size tracking; evidence count gate | Enforce minimum evidence count per conclusion |
| Model drift | Reasoning patterns produce systematically biased outputs | Rolling calibration score tracking | Recalibrate model; retrain on recent data |
| Conviction miscalibration | Conviction scores not predictive of outcomes | Conviction-outcome correlation analysis | Recalibrate conviction computation; adjust pattern weights |

#### 2.7.3 Decision Classes and Approval Requirements

| Decision Class | Conviction Required | Risk Check | Portfolio Check | Human Approval | Max Position Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Signal-only** | ≥ 6.5 | Automated | Automated | Not required | N/A |
| **Paper trade** | ≥ 6.5 | Automated | Automated | Not required | Full paper portfolio limits |
| **Small live trade** | ≥ 7.0 | Automated + RISK_GUARDIAN | Automated | Not required | 2% of portfolio |
| **Medium live trade** | ≥ 7.5 | Full risk suite | Full portfolio | Recommended | 5% of portfolio |
| **Large live trade** | ≥ 8.0 | Full risk suite + stress test | Full portfolio + concentration | Required | 10% of portfolio |
| **Portfolio rebalance** | ≥ 8.5 | Full risk suite + MC simulation | Full portfolio + correlation | Required | Full rebalance |
| **Emergency exit** | Any (risk trigger) | Automated kill-switch | Bypassed | Not required (speed) | Full position |

#### 2.7.4 The Five-Agent Debate Protocol

The DECISION_ARCHITECTURE defines that every investment hypothesis is subjected to a structured debate among five agents before a decision is formed:

| Agent | Role | Bias | Contribution |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Advocate** | Argues for the hypothesis | Confirms bullish case | Strongest supporting evidence |
| **Skeptic** | Challenges the hypothesis | Finds flaws in the case | Disconfirming evidence, alternative explanations |
| **Risk Officer** | Evaluates tail risks | Conservative risk focus | Worst-case scenarios, sizing implications |
| **Historian** | Matches to historical analogues | Precedent-seeking | Historical outcome of similar situations |
| **Strategist** | Evaluates portfolio-level impact | Portfolio coherence | Correlation with existing positions, concentration effects |

Decision is formed only when the aggregate debate score exceeds 6.5/10.0 after the full five-agent process.

#### 2.7.5 Failure Impact

| Failure Mode | Impact | Detection | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conviction threshold breach | Low-conviction trade executed | Post-trade conviction audit | Halt execution; review threshold enforcement |
| Risk check bypassed | Trade executed against risk limits | Real-time limit monitor | Immediate position review; potential forced exit |
| Portfolio constraint violation | Concentration or exposure limit exceeded | Portfolio monitor alert | Reduce offending position; review constraint logic |
| Approval process skipped | Decision executed without required approval | Audit trail gap | Halt execution pipeline; investigate; enforce process |
| Debate protocol incomplete | Decision formed without full five-agent input | Debate completion log | Void decision; restart debate process |

### 2.8 Subsystem VIII — LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE

#### 2.8.1 Profile

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| Document | LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE.md |
| Phase | Phase 8 — The Learning System |
| Primary Question | How does the intelligence learn from its decisions and outcomes? |
| Constitutional Role | Defines all learning processes, learning triggers, lesson extraction, belief updates, and learning quality governance |
| Document Size | 303,442 bytes |
| Authority Level | Learning plane authority — all learning processes governed by this document |
| Plane | Evolution Plane |
| Dependencies | All Knowledge Plane + Intelligence Plane documents |
| Consumers | MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE, All reasoning and decision processes via feedback |

#### 2.8.2 Responsibilities

| # | Responsibility | Description |
|---|---|---|
| LA-R01 | Learning Event Definition | Defines what constitutes a learning event — the trigger conditions under which a learning process is initiated |
| LA-R02 | Outcome Attribution | Defines how outcomes are attributed to decisions, to strategies, to reasoning patterns, and to individual evidence sources |
| LA-R03 | Lesson Extraction | Defines how lessons are extracted from attributed outcomes — the generalisation process that converts specific events into transferable knowledge |
| LA-R04 | Belief Update Protocol | Defines how the intelligence updates its beliefs in response to learning events — Bayesian update framework |
| LA-R05 | Learning Quality Control | Defines how learning quality is measured and governed — preventing overfitting, recency bias, and false lessons |
| LA-R06 | Cross-Domain Learning Transfer | Defines how lessons from one domain are transferred to other relevant domains |
| LA-R07 | Meta-Learning | Defines how the intelligence learns about its own learning process — identifying which types of learning produce reliable knowledge |
| LA-R08 | Learning Anti-Patterns | Defines the learning anti-patterns that must be actively prevented — overfitting, catastrophic forgetting, recency bias |

#### 2.8.3 Learning Lifecycle

| Stage | Name | Trigger | Process | Output |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L-01 | Outcome Observation | Trade/signal closed | Actual vs. expected outcome computed | Outcome record |
| L-02 | Attribution Analysis | Outcome record created | Decision factors → outcome contribution | Attribution record |
| L-03 | Lesson Extraction | Attribution complete | Pattern identified; lesson formulated | Lesson candidate |
| L-04 | Lesson Validation | Lesson candidate | Cross-validate against alternative evidence | Validated lesson |
| L-05 | Belief Update | Lesson validated | Bayesian update of relevant beliefs | Updated belief state |
| L-06 | Knowledge Propagation | Belief updated | Updated beliefs propagate to relevant domains | Knowledge network update |
| L-07 | Memory Encoding | Knowledge propagated | Learning event encoded as memory record | Memory record |
| L-08 | Learning Audit | Cycle complete | Audit learning quality; detect anti-patterns | Audit record |

#### 2.8.4 Failure Impact

| Failure Mode | Impact | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| Overfitting | Intelligence optimises for historical patterns that don't generalise — performance degrades in new regimes | Detect via walk-forward test degradation; regularise; expand training window |
| Catastrophic forgetting | Recent learning overwrites valuable historical knowledge | Enforce memory retention constraints; require evidence to override established beliefs |
| Recency bias | Recent events given disproportionate weight | Temporal weighting audit; enforce balanced temporal sampling |
| False lesson | Spurious correlation mistaken for causal insight | Cross-validation gate; require multiple independent confirmations |
| Attribution failure | Outcomes misattributed — wrong factors learned | Improved attribution models; causal structure validation |

#### 2.9.3 Memory Integration With Intelligence Pipeline

The MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE is unique among all architecture documents in that it receives from all upstream documents and feeds back into all upstream documents. This bidirectional integration makes memory the connective tissue of the intelligence:

| Direction | From/To | Content |
|---|---|---|
| **Receives from** INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY | Information → Memory | Raw information events create Memory Traces |
| **Receives from** ENTITY_ONTOLOGY | Entity → Memory | Entity state changes are recorded as entity memory records |
| **Receives from** EVENT_ONTOLOGY | Event → Memory | Every significant event creates an episode memory record |
| **Receives from** REASONING_ARCHITECTURE | Reasoning → Memory | Every reasoning chain is stored as a reasoning memory record |
| **Receives from** DECISION_ARCHITECTURE | Decision → Memory | Every decision is stored as a decision memory record |
| **Receives from** LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE | Learning → Memory | Every learning event is encoded as a long-term memory record |
| **Feeds back into** REASONING_ARCHITECTURE | Memory → Reasoning | Historical analogues, patterns, and validated beliefs inform current reasoning |
| **Feeds back into** DECISION_ARCHITECTURE | Memory → Decision | Institutional decision memory informs current decision context |
| **Feeds back into** INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY | Memory → Information | Reliability calibration of information sources uses historical memory |
| **Feeds back into** ENTITY_ONTOLOGY | Memory → Entity | Long-term entity trajectories supplement current entity state |

#### 2.9.4 Failure Impact

| Failure Mode | Impact | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| Institutional memory loss | Intelligence loses access to historical knowledge — reasoning degrades toward naive | Restore from backup; rebuild from audit logs; accept capability regression |
| Memory corruption | False memories contaminate reasoning — systematic errors in analogy and pattern matching | Quarantine suspect memory records; re-validate from source data; audit all decisions using corrupted memories |
| Consolidation failure | Working memory fills up; short-term patterns never promoted to long-term knowledge | Manual consolidation trigger; increase consolidation frequency |
| Recall failure | Correct memories exist but cannot be retrieved — intelligence "knows but cannot remember" | Rebuild retrieval index; audit retrieval cue logic |

## PART III — THE COMPLETE COGNITIVE LOOP

### 3.0 Overview

The Complete Cognitive Loop is the perpetual operating cycle of the Investment Intelligence Operating System. It defines the exact sequence in which every architecture document activates, in what order, for what purpose, with what inputs and outputs, and under what constitutional constraints. Every market cycle — from the first arrival of information to the final encoding of learning into institutional memory — traverses this complete loop.

The loop is perpetual: it has no beginning and no end. Every termination of one cycle is simultaneously the initiation of the next. The intelligence never stops; it only transitions between cycles.

The loop has 20 stages. Each stage is owned by one or more architecture documents. No stage may be skipped. No stage may be reordered. The sequence is constitutional.

```
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                      THE COMPLETE COGNITIVE LOOP
                    20-Stage Perpetual Operating Cycle
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

  ┌─── STAGE 01: REALITY MONITORING ───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Market exists; global state is monitored continuously                 │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 02: INFORMATION ARRIVAL ──────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Price ticks, economic releases, corporate events detected             │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 03: INFORMATION VALIDATION ───────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Source check, schema check, timeliness check, quality score           │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 04: ENTITY STATE UPDATE ──────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Entity attributes updated; state snapshots created                    │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 05: RELATIONSHIP UPDATE ──────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Correlation coefficients refreshed; causal chains updated             │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 06: EVENT DETECTION ───────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Events classified from updated entity/relationship state              │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 07: EVENT IMPACT ASSESSMENT ──────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Event magnitude, surprise, propagation path assessed                  │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 08: HYPOTHESIS GENERATION ────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Investment hypotheses generated from events + entity state            │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 09: EVIDENCE COLLECTION ──────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Supporting and disconfirming evidence collected per hypothesis        │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 10: MEMORY-ASSISTED REASONING ─────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Historical analogues retrieved; pattern library consulted             │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 11: CONVICTION COMPUTATION ───────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Multi-pattern reasoning → conviction score in [0, 10]                 │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
                              Conviction ≥ 6.5?
                             /                  \
                           YES                   NO ──► STAGE 18: LEARNING
                            │                            (from non-decision)
  ┌─── STAGE 12: FIVE-AGENT DEBATE ────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Advocate / Skeptic / Risk Officer / Historian / Strategist debate     │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 13: RISK APPROVAL ─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Position size check; portfolio limits; VIX/drawdown kill-switch       │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
                            Risk Approved?
                           /               \
                         YES               NO ──► STAGE 18: LEARNING
                          │                        (from risk rejection)
  ┌─── STAGE 14: DECISION FORMATION ───────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Full decision record created: instrument, direction, size, conditions │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 15: EXECUTION ─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Order sent to broker; fill confirmed; position opened                 │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 16: TRADE MONITORING ─────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Position monitored continuously; stop-loss and target levels watched  │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 17: OUTCOME OBSERVATION ──────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Trade closed; actual PnL computed; outcome record created             │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 18: LEARNING EVENT ───────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Attribution analysis; lesson extraction; belief updates               │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 19: MEMORY ENCODING ──────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Learning encoded as memory record; consolidated into long-term memory │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
  ┌─── STAGE 20: KNOWLEDGE INTEGRATION ────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Updated beliefs propagate; reasoning patterns updated; quality audit  │
  └──────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
                              Next Cycle Begins
                                     │
                              ◄──────┘
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
```

### 3.1 Stage Specifications

| Architecture Documents | ENTITY_ONTOLOGY, RELATIONSHIP_ONTOLOGY, EVENT_ONTOLOGY |
| Trigger | Continuous — no trigger required; always active |
| Input | Global market state — all monitored instruments across all markets |
| Process | Continuous monitoring of price levels, volume, breadth, volatility, correlation, global indicators |
| Output | Change signals when any monitored metric crosses a detection threshold |
| Timing | Continuous during market hours; periodic overnight |
| Constitutional Requirement | All monitored instruments must be covered; no monitoring gap permitted |

| Constitutional Requirement | All information arrivals must be timestamped at receipt; no post-hoc timestamping |

| Trigger | Learning event complete |
| Input | Learning event record; updated beliefs; outcome record; decision record |
| Process | Create memory record via 26-stage memory pipeline; consolidate into appropriate memory tier |
| Output | Consolidated memory record in long-term memory; index update |
| Timing | Immediate record creation; consolidation within 30 minutes |
| Constitutional Requirement | Every learning event must produce a memory record — the learning cycle is not complete until memory is encoded |

#### Stage 20 — Knowledge Integration

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| Name | Knowledge Integration |
| Owner | IIOS, All Architecture Documents |
| Architecture Documents | All nine architecture documents |
| Trigger | Memory encoding complete |
| Process | Propagate updated beliefs to all relevant knowledge domains; update reasoning pattern weights; refresh entity priors; trigger quality audit |
| Output | Updated knowledge state across all domains; quality audit record; readiness flag for next cycle |
| Timing | < 5 minutes after memory encoding |
| Constitutional Requirement | The cognitive loop is not complete until knowledge integration is done — next cycle cannot begin on stale knowledge |

### 3.2 Cognitive Loop Timing Summary

| Stage | Owner | Nominal Timing | Maximum Allowed | Action on Timeout |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01 Reality Monitoring | GlobalIntelligence | Continuous | N/A | Alert if monitoring gap > 60s |
| 02 Information Arrival | Data Feeds | Sub-100ms | 500ms | Retry; log; alert if persistent |
| 03 Information Validation | INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY | 50ms | 200ms | Queue backlog alert |
| 04 Entity State Update | ENTITY_ONTOLOGY | 100ms | 500ms | Alert; use last valid state |
| 05 Relationship Update | RELATIONSHIP_ONTOLOGY | 5min | 15min | Use previous relationship state |
| 06 Event Detection | EVENT_ONTOLOGY | 200ms | 1s | Alert; manual review trigger |
| 07 Event Impact | EVENT_ONTOLOGY | 500ms | 2s | Use conservative impact estimate |
| 08 Hypothesis Generation | REASONING | 2s | 5s | Alert; reduce hypothesis set |
| 09 Evidence Collection | REASONING | 5s | 15s | Accept partial evidence set with flag |
| 10 Memory-Assisted Reasoning | REASONING + MEMORY | 3s | 10s | Proceed without analogues; flag output |
| 11 Conviction Computation | REASONING | 500ms | 2s | Suspend; alert |
| 12 Five-Agent Debate | DECISION | 5s | 15s | Abort decision; log |
| 13 Risk Approval | RISK_GUARDIAN | 200ms | 1s | Default to rejection on timeout |
| 14 Decision Formation | DECISION | 200ms | 1s | Abort; alert |
| 15 Execution | ExecutionEngine | Market-order: 1s | 5s | Cancel order; alert; retry with limit |
| 16 Trade Monitoring | TradeMonitoring | Continuous | N/A | Alert if monitoring gap > 60s |
| 17 Outcome Observation | LEARNING | 1s | 5s | Alert; manual review |
| 18 Learning Event | LEARNING | 30s | 5min | Truncate learning; flag for review |
| 19 Memory Encoding | MEMORY | 30min | 2h | Flag for manual consolidation |
| 20 Knowledge Integration | IIOS | 5min | 30min | Flag for review; proceed to next cycle |

## PART IV — THE COMPLETE ARCHITECTURE MAP

### 4.0 Overview

The AI Trading Brain operates across 15 functional layers. These layers are distinct from the 9 architecture documents defined in Part II and from the 17 code layers defined in ARCHITECTURE.md. The 15 functional layers defined here represent the constitutional architecture of the intelligence — the functional decomposition of all intelligence activity into coherent, bounded, governed domains.

```
╔═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║              COMPLETE INVESTMENT INTELLIGENCE ARCHITECTURE MAP           ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║  Layer 15 │ GOVERNANCE LAYER        │ Constitutional authority           ║
║  Layer 14 │ AUDIT LAYER             │ Compliance and quality assurance   ║
║  Layer 13 │ FEEDBACK LAYER          │ Performance and attribution        ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║  Layer 12 │ MEMORY LAYER            │ Institutional knowledge store      ║
║  Layer 11 │ LEARNING LAYER          │ Experience-to-knowledge conversion ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║  Layer 10 │ MONITORING LAYER        │ Position and system monitoring     ║
║  Layer 09 │ EXECUTION LAYER         │ Order routing and fill management  ║
║  Layer 08 │ RISK LAYER              │ Risk assessment and kill-switch    ║
║  Layer 07 │ DECISION LAYER          │ Decision formation and approval    ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║  Layer 06 │ REASONING LAYER         │ Evidence → conviction computation  ║
║  Layer 05 │ EVENT LAYER             │ Event detection and classification ║
║  Layer 04 │ RELATIONSHIP LAYER      │ Entity relationship graph          ║
║  Layer 03 │ ENTITY LAYER            │ Entity state and identity          ║
║  Layer 02 │ INFORMATION LAYER       │ Data validation and quality        ║
║  Layer 01 │ KNOWLEDGE LAYER         │ Domain knowledge and taxonomy      ║
╚═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
```

---

### 4.1 Layer 01 — Knowledge Layer

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Knowledge Layer |
| **Constitutional Owner** | MASTER_KNOWLEDGE_ARCHITECTURE |
| **Function** | Define, organise, and govern the complete knowledge universe of the investment intelligence |
| **Boundary** | Receives from reality; supplies to all other layers; governs what can be known |
| **Inputs** | Human-curated domain expertise; validated historical knowledge; architectural definitions |
| **Outputs** | Knowledge taxonomy; domain definitions; quality standards; epistemological rules |
| **Consumes from** | Nothing within the system — the Knowledge Layer is the root |
| **Produces for** | All other 14 layers |
| **Governed By** | IIOS (this document) |
| **Audited By** | Audit Layer (Layer 14) |

**Responsibilities:**
1. Maintain the complete knowledge taxonomy for all investment domains
2. Define what constitutes valid knowledge vs. unvalidated information
3. Define knowledge quality standards applied across all layers
4. Govern the evolution of the knowledge universe over time
5. Define knowledge domain boundaries and scope limits

**Layer Invariant:** Every concept used in any other layer must be defined in the Knowledge Layer's taxonomy. Undefined concepts may not be used in reasoning.

---

### 4.2 Layer 02 — Information Layer

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Information Layer |
| **Constitutional Owner** | INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY |
| **Function** | Receive, validate, classify, and route all information arriving from external sources |
| **Boundary** | Interface between external reality and internal intelligence; first validation gate |
| **Inputs** | Raw market data (Dhan API, yfinance), economic releases, corporate announcements, news feeds |
| **Outputs** | Validated, quality-scored, timestamped information objects routed to appropriate consuming layers |
| **Consumes from** | Reality (external data sources) |
| **Produces for** | Entity Layer, Relationship Layer, Event Layer, Memory Layer |
| **Governed By** | Knowledge Layer, IIOS |
| **Audited By** | Audit Layer |

**Responsibilities:**
1. Implement all information validation rules defined in INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY
2. Maintain source reliability scores for all data providers
3. Detect and quarantine potentially poisoned data
4. Route validated information to consuming layers with full lineage
5. Reject and log all invalid information with specific rejection reasons

**Layer Invariant:** No information may pass to any downstream layer without completing all validation gates. Partial validation is a constitutional violation.

| **Inputs** | Validated information from Information Layer |
| **Outputs** | Current entity state; historical entity snapshots; entity change events |

2. Resolve entity identity conflicts and ambiguities
3. Create point-in-time state snapshots for audit and reasoning
4. Detect entity lifecycle events (IPO, delisting, merger, split)
5. Maintain historical entity state with full temporal query capability

**Layer Invariant:** Every entity referenced in any layer must have a registered entity record in the Entity Layer. No ghost entity references.

| **Outputs** | Relationship graph; correlation coefficients; contagion paths; relationship change events |

1. Maintain rolling correlation coefficients for all monitored entity pairs
2. Update causal chain strengths based on observed event sequences
3. Detect relationship formation, strengthening, weakening, and dissolution events
4. Maintain relationship history for temporal reasoning
5. Provide contagion path analysis for risk assessment

**Layer Invariant:** All relationship strengths must have a defined TTL. Expired relationship data must be flagged as stale before being used in any reasoning.

1. Apply event detection rules to incoming entity and relationship state changes
2. Classify every detected event by type, magnitude, and surprise
3. Assess event impact — which entities are affected, in what way, for how long
4. Maintain complete event history for analogical reasoning
5. Trigger downstream intelligence processes for events above defined thresholds

**Layer Invariant:** Every event above the significance threshold must be classified before any downstream processing. Classification cannot be deferred.

| **Produces for** | Decision Layer; Learning Layer (via outcome attribution) |
| **Governed By** | Knowledge Layer, IIOS |
| **Audited By** | Audit Layer |

**Responsibilities:**
1. Generate investment hypotheses from event impact assessments
2. Collect and evaluate supporting and disconfirming evidence for each hypothesis
3. Retrieve historical analogues from Memory Layer to assist reasoning
4. Compute multi-pattern conviction scores with calibrated uncertainty
5. Produce complete reasoning chain records for audit and learning

**Layer Invariant:** Every hypothesis must specify its invalidation conditions at creation time. Hypotheses without specified invalidation conditions are rejected.

### 4.7 Layer 07 — Decision Layer

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Decision Layer |
| **Constitutional Owner** | DECISION_ARCHITECTURE |
| **Function** | Transform high-conviction hypotheses into approved investment decisions through structured debate and risk integration |
| **Boundary** | The last intelligence gate before execution; integrates reasoning with risk and portfolio constraints |
| **Inputs** | Investment hypotheses with conviction ≥ 6.5; portfolio state; risk capacity |
| **Outputs** | Approved decision records; rejected decision records with reasons; position sizing recommendations |
| **Consumes from** | Reasoning Layer, Risk Layer, Entity Layer, Relationship Layer |
| **Produces for** | Execution Layer, Learning Layer, Memory Layer |
| **Governed By** | Risk Layer (risk primacy), Knowledge Layer, IIOS |
| **Audited By** | Audit Layer |

**Responsibilities:**
1. Execute five-agent debate for all hypotheses that cross the conviction threshold
2. Integrate risk assessment into decision formation
3. Enforce portfolio-level constraints: concentration limits, sector limits, gross exposure
4. Produce fully specified decision records for all approved decisions
5. Produce fully documented rejection records for all rejected decisions

**Layer Invariant:** No decision is formed without completing the full five-agent debate. Partial debates produce no decisions.

### 4.8 Layer 08 — Risk Layer

| Attribute | Value |

| **Constitutional Owner** | DECISION_ARCHITECTURE (RiskGuardian section) |

| **Boundary** | Non-bypassable — all proposed decisions pass through the Risk Layer before execution |
| **Inputs** | Proposed decisions; portfolio state; market risk metrics (VIX, correlation, drawdown) |
| **Outputs** | Risk approval or rejection; position size recommendations; risk metrics for monitoring |
| **Consumes from** | Decision Layer, Entity Layer, Relationship Layer, Monitoring Layer |
| **Produces for** | Decision Layer (approval/rejection), Execution Layer (risk parameters) |
| **Governed By** | IIOS — risk limits are constitutional, not configurable by individual decisions |
| **Audited By** | Audit Layer |

**Responsibilities:**
1. Check all proposed positions against active risk limits before any execution
2. Enforce kill-switch conditions (VIX > 45, daily loss > 2%) unconditionally
3. Compute portfolio-level risk metrics continuously
4. Provide position sizing recommendations within approved risk envelope
5. Alert when portfolio approaches (not just reaches) risk limits

**Layer Invariant:** No trade may be executed without explicit Risk Layer approval. An active kill-switch condition may not be overridden by any authority except explicit human instruction under documented emergency protocol.
