3. Extract lessons from attributed outcomes following anti-overfitting protocols
4. Update beliefs according to the Bayesian update protocol
5. Propagate updated knowledge across all relevant domains

## PART V — THE COMPLETE GOVERNANCE MODEL

### 5.0 Overview

The Governance Model of the IIOS defines the complete authority structure of the investment intelligence: who governs what, under what conditions, with what scope, with what limits, and how conflicts are resolved. The Governance Model is constitutional — it cannot be changed by operational instruction, only by formal constitutional amendment.

```
╔═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
║                    IIOS AUTHORITY HIERARCHY                              ║
╠═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╣
║  Level 7 │ CONSTITUTIONAL AUTHORITY   │ IIOS — supreme, non-delegable   ║
║  Level 6 │ HUMAN PRINCIPAL AUTHORITY  │ Investment principals           ║
║  Level 5 │ KILL-SWITCH AUTHORITY      │ RiskGuardian — non-bypassable   ║
║  Level 4 │ GOVERNANCE COUNCIL         │ Multi-component consensus       ║
║  Level 3 │ DOMAIN AUTHORITY           │ Per-architecture doc authority  ║
║  Level 2 │ LAYER AUTHORITY            │ Per-layer operational authority ║
║  Level 1 │ AGENT AUTHORITY            │ Individual agent decisions      ║
╚═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
```

### 5.1 Authority Hierarchy — Full Specification

#### Level 7: Constitutional Authority — IIOS

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Authority** | Investment Intelligence Operating System (this document) |
| **Scope** | All components, all layers, all operations, all agents, all decisions |
| **Delegable** | No — constitutional authority cannot be delegated |
| **Override** | Cannot be overridden by any lower authority |
| **Amendment** | Only by formal constitutional amendment process with documented justification |
| **Enforcement** | Audit Layer monitors compliance continuously |

The IIOS defines the 60 system invariants, the 65 constitutional articles, the governance model, and the architecture map. Every other authority derives its scope and limits from this document.

#### Level 6: Human Principal Authority

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Authority** | Investment principals — human beings who own or operate the AI Trading Brain |
| **Scope** | All decisions within constitutional bounds |
| **Delegable** | Yes — to specific agents for specific decision classes |
| **Override Capability** | Can override any operational decision within constitutional bounds |
| **Cannot Override** | Constitutional invariants; active kill-switch conditions (except through documented emergency protocol); historical records |
| **Override Protocol** | All human overrides are logged, timestamped, and attributed |

Human principal authority is the highest operational authority. It is bounded only by the IIOS constitution. A human principal may override any investment decision, any system parameter, and any process step — as long as the override does not violate a constitutional invariant.

#### Level 5: Kill-Switch Authority — RiskGuardian

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Authority** | RiskGuardian component |
| **Scope** | All execution activities when kill-switch conditions are active |
| **Activation Conditions** | VIX > 45; daily portfolio loss > 2%; any single position loss > 1%; maximum drawdown threshold breached |
| **Override** | Cannot be overridden by any automated component; requires explicit human override through emergency protocol |
| **Duration** | Active until conditions clear or human principal explicitly deactivates |
| **Effect** | Prevents all new position opening; existing positions may continue until their exit conditions trigger |

The kill-switch authority is the most powerful operational authority in the system. It supersedes all investment decisions, all agent signals, and all conviction scores. Only explicit human instruction through the emergency protocol can override an active kill-switch.

#### Level 4: Governance Council Authority

The IIOS defines five Governance Councils, each responsible for a specific domain of intelligence governance:

| Council | Domain | Members | Decision Method | Quorum |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Knowledge Council** | Knowledge taxonomy evolution; knowledge quality standards | Knowledge Layer + IIOS | Consensus | All members |
| **Risk Council** | Risk limit calibration; kill-switch threshold review | Risk Layer + Governance Layer + Human Principal | Majority + Human approval | All members |
| **Architecture Council** | Architecture evolution decisions; new component approval | Governance Layer + Audit Layer + Human Principal | Consensus + Human approval | All members |
| **Learning Council** | Learning anti-pattern governance; learning quality standards | Learning Layer + Memory Layer + Audit Layer | Consensus | Quorum (2/3) |
| **Audit Council** | Audit standard evolution; compliance definition | Audit Layer + Governance Layer + Human Principal | Consensus | All members |

Governance Councils are activated for decisions that exceed domain authority but do not require constitutional amendment. A Council decision is binding on all members and on all lower authorities in the affected domain.

#### Level 3: Domain Authority — Architecture Documents

Each of the nine architecture documents has domain authority within its defined scope:

| Document | Domain Authority Scope | Limits |
|---|---|---|
| MASTER_KNOWLEDGE | All knowledge classification and taxonomy decisions | Cannot define knowledge that contradicts IIOS invariants |
| INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY | All information validation rules | Cannot accept information that violates constitutional quality standards |
| ENTITY_ONTOLOGY | All entity definition and state management decisions | Cannot define entities that bypass the knowledge taxonomy |
| RELATIONSHIP_ONTOLOGY | All relationship type and strength decisions | Cannot establish relationships between entities not registered in ENTITY_ONTOLOGY |
| EVENT_ONTOLOGY | All event classification and impact decisions | Cannot classify events using undefined entity or relationship types |
| REASONING_ARCHITECTURE | All reasoning pattern and conviction decisions | Cannot produce conviction scores without required evidence balance |
| DECISION_ARCHITECTURE | All decision process and approval decisions | Cannot form decisions below the conviction threshold |
| LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE | All learning event and belief update decisions | Cannot update beliefs without outcome attribution |
| MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE | All memory lifecycle and consolidation decisions | Cannot alter existing memory records |

---

### 5.2 Decision Authority Matrix

| Decision Type | Primary Authority | Secondary Authority | Human Approval Required | Override Path |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Knowledge taxonomy update | Knowledge Council | IIOS | Recommended | Architecture Council |
| Information validation rule change | INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY domain authority | Knowledge Council | Not required | Knowledge Council |
| New entity class definition | ENTITY_ONTOLOGY domain authority | Knowledge Council | Not required | Knowledge Council |
| Risk limit adjustment | Risk Council | Human Principal | Required | Human Principal only |
| Kill-switch threshold change | Risk Council | Human Principal | Required | Human Principal only |
| Conviction threshold change | Architecture Council | Human Principal | Required | Human Principal only |
| Strategy promotion/demotion | LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE | Human Principal review | Recommended | Human Principal |
| Emergency exit of all positions | Kill-switch (automated) or Human Principal | None | Not required (emergency) | Human Principal |
| Constitutional amendment | Architecture Council + Human Principal | IIOS audit | Required | No override — constitutional process only |
| New agent addition | Architecture Council | Human Principal | Required | No override |

---

### 5.3 Human Override Protocol

Human override is a constitutional right of the investment principals. It is subject to the following protocol to ensure it is used appropriately and is always auditable:

| Step | Action | Required | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | State the override scope | Yes | Define what is being overridden |
| 2 | State the reason | Yes | Override without reason is not recordable |
| 3 | Acknowledge constitutional constraints | Yes | Human principal confirms override is within constitutional bounds |
| 4 | System records override with timestamp | Automatic | Audit trail |
| 5 | System flags all subsequent decisions affected by override | Automatic | Downstream awareness |
| 6 | System requests confirmation of override within 24 hours | Automatic | Persistent overrides require deliberate confirmation |

**What a human principal CAN override:**
- Any investment decision
- Any position exit or entry
- Any system parameter within constitutional bounds
- Kill-switch deactivation (under emergency protocol)
- Any learning or memory process

**What no authority — human or automated — CAN override:**
- Historical records (immutable)
- Audit trail completeness requirement
- The requirement to attribute all decisions
- The requirement to quantify uncertainty
- The requirement to record all overrides

---

### 5.4 Emergency Authority Protocol

Emergency authority is invoked when the standard decision process cannot be followed due to time constraints or system failure. It allows faster decision-making with reduced process steps, subject to enhanced post-event audit.

| Trigger | Authority | Actions Permitted | Process Reduction | Post-Event Requirement |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| System component failure during market hours | Human Principal | Direct position modification; forced exit | Debate and conviction threshold may be bypassed | Full post-event audit within 4 hours |
| Extreme market disruption (circuit breaker) | Human Principal + Kill-Switch | All position exits | Full process bypassed | Full post-event audit within 24 hours |
| Black swan event | Human Principal | Portfolio rebalancing; all exits | All processes bypassed | Full post-event audit within 24 hours |
| Connectivity failure | Kill-Switch (automatic) | Prevent new opens; maintain exits | Automated only | Auto-audit when connectivity restored |

---

### 5.5 Conflict Resolution Protocol

When two or more authority levels or architecture documents produce conflicting guidance, the following resolution protocol is applied in order:

| Step | Resolution Rule | Applies When |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Check constitutional invariants | If one option violates an invariant, the non-violating option is selected unconditionally |
| 2 | Apply authority hierarchy | Higher authority level prevails (see 5.1) |
| 3 | Apply risk primacy | If one option is more conservative, the conservative option prevails |
| 4 | Apply evidence primacy | The option with stronger evidence support prevails |
| 5 | Apply recency primacy | The option based on more recent information prevails |
| 6 | Escalate to Governance Council | If no rule resolves the conflict, the appropriate Governance Council decides |
| 7 | Escalate to Human Principal | If Governance Council cannot resolve, the Human Principal decides |

---

### 5.6 Escalation Hierarchy

All issues are escalated through the following hierarchy. Escalation should be as rapid as the severity requires:

| Severity | Level | Responder | Response Time | Notification |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Level 1 — Advisory** | Informational only | Feedback Layer | No response required | Dashboard update |
| **Level 2 — Warning** | Approaching threshold | Learning Layer + Audit Layer | Next review cycle | Dashboard alert |
| **Level 3 — Alert** | Threshold breached | Domain Authority | Within 30 minutes | System alert + log |
| **Level 4 — Incident** | Constitutional violation | Governance Layer + Audit Layer | Within 5 minutes | Immediate alert + escalation |
| **Level 5 — Emergency** | System integrity at risk | Human Principal + Kill-Switch | Immediate | All channels |
| **Level 6 — Crisis** | Capital at acute risk | Human Principal + Kill-Switch | Immediate | All channels + kill-switch activation |


---

## PART VI — SYSTEM INVARIANTS

### 6.0 Overview

System invariants are the constitutional guarantees of the IIOS. They are not guidelines. They are not best practices. They are constitutional requirements that must hold true in every operation, in every market condition, in every system state. An operation that violates a system invariant is constitutionally void.

The 60 system invariants are organised into 8 categories. Each invariant has a statement, a rationale, a violation consequence, and an enforcement mechanism.

---

### Category I — Truth and Epistemic Invariants (INV-01 to INV-08)

**Category Principle:** All intelligence activities must be grounded in evidence-based truth. Beliefs must accurately represent the intelligence's best understanding of reality. No belief may be held with more or less confidence than the evidence supports.

| # | Invariant | Statement | Rationale | Violation Consequence | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INV-01 | **Evidence Primacy** | No belief may be held with higher confidence than the evidence supports | Overconfident beliefs produce oversized positions and underestimated risk | Conviction score decoupled from evidence — decisions immediately invalid | Conviction computation audit at every cycle |
| INV-02 | **Falsifiability Requirement** | Every hypothesis must specify at least one condition that would invalidate it | Non-falsifiable hypotheses are not hypotheses — they are beliefs immune to evidence | Hypothesis rejected from consideration | Hypothesis validation gate |
| INV-03 | **Disconfirming Evidence Requirement** | Every evidence set must contain at least 20% disconfirming evidence | Pure confirming evidence indicates selective evidence collection (confirmation bias) | Decision void; evidence collection restarted | Evidence balance check |
| INV-04 | **Uncertainty Quantification** | Every output must include a quantified uncertainty estimate | Outputs without uncertainty bounds cannot be correctly sized or weighted | Output flagged as non-compliant; downstream uses suspended until corrected | Output schema validation |
| INV-05 | **No Certainty Claims** | No output may express certainty of 1.0 or 0.0 for any market prediction | Markets are never certain; claims of certainty are calibration failures | Output rejected; calibration recalibration triggered | Confidence range validation |
| INV-06 | **Truth Over Comfort** | Uncomfortable evidence must receive the same weight as comfortable evidence | Filtering evidence by emotional valence is confirmation bias | Evidence set audited; selective filtering causes decision void | Evidence audit gate |
| INV-07 | **Source Independence** | Multiple independent sources are required for high-confidence beliefs | Single-source beliefs are vulnerable to source error and fabrication | Confidence cap applied when source independence fails | Source diversity check |
| INV-08 | **Belief Proportionality** | Belief strength must be proportional to the quality and quantity of supporting evidence | Disproportionate beliefs produce disproportionate actions | Conviction recalibrated to evidence level; prior decision voided | Conviction calibration audit |

---

### Category II — Process Integrity Invariants (INV-09 to INV-16)

**Category Principle:** All intelligence processes must be followed with complete fidelity. No step may be skipped because the outcome already seems clear. Process integrity is the only foundation for audit, learning, and improvement.

| # | Invariant | Statement | Rationale | Violation Consequence | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INV-09 | **No Stage Skipping** | No stage of the cognitive loop (Stages 01–20) may be skipped regardless of apparent efficiency | Stage skipping breaks the audit trail and corrupts learning | Decision voided; loop restarted from point of skip | Stage completion verification |
| INV-10 | **Debate Completeness** | The five-agent debate must be completed in full — all five agents must contribute | Partial debates produce incomplete risk assessment | Decision voided; debate restarted | Debate completion log |
| INV-11 | **Risk Gate Mandatory** | Every proposed trade must pass through the Risk Layer before execution | Risk approval is non-optional; bypassing risk gate is a critical violation | Execution blocked; component placing trade suspended | Execution prerequisite check |
| INV-12 | **Attribution Mandatory** | Every outcome must be attributed before any lesson is extracted | Unattributed outcomes produce lessons that may reinforce wrong behaviour | Learning event suspended until attribution complete | Learning trigger gate |
| INV-13 | **Memory Encoding Mandatory** | Every learning event must produce a memory record | Unrecorded learning disappears — the intelligence gains no institutional benefit | Learning event flagged incomplete; escalated for manual review | Memory encoding confirmation |
| INV-14 | **Approval Before Execution** | No order may be submitted to a broker before the complete decision record is formed and approved | Post-hoc rationalisation of trades is not acceptable | Execution immediately halted; investigation triggered | Decision record completeness check |
| INV-15 | **Validation Before Consumption** | No information may be used in any reasoning or decision process without passing all validation gates | Unvalidated information corrupts all downstream reasoning | Reasoning process suspended; information quarantined | Validation status check |
| INV-16 | **Lineage Mandatory** | Every output must have a documented lineage — traceable to its inputs | Lineage gaps prevent audit and learning | Output flagged; audit gap investigation triggered | Lineage completion check |

---

### Category III — History and Immutability Invariants (INV-17 to INV-22)

**Category Principle:** History is the foundation of all future intelligence. It must be preserved in immutable form. No historical record may be altered, deleted, or selectively preserved.

| # | Invariant | Statement | Rationale | Violation Consequence | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INV-17 | **Historical Immutability** | No historical record — trade, decision, learning event, memory — may be altered after creation | History modification destroys the validity of all future learning that used the record | Record system integrity failure; full audit triggered | Write-once record storage; audit hash verification |
| INV-18 | **No Silent Deletion** | No record may be deleted without an explicit, documented, approved deletion record | Silent deletion is indistinguishable from fraud | Deletion flagged as potential integrity violation; investigation triggered | Deletion log requirement |
| INV-19 | **Complete History** | All events above the significance threshold must be recorded — no selective preservation | Selective preservation introduces selection bias into all future learning | Missing records trigger completeness alert; filling triggered | Completeness audit |
| INV-20 | **Outcome Recording** | Every closed position must have a complete outcome record regardless of outcome quality | Bad outcome records are as valuable as good ones for learning; suppression introduces survivorship bias | Missing outcome record = critical violation; learning pipeline halted | Outcome record gate |
| INV-21 | **Decision Record Completeness** | Every decision must have a complete decision record including all inputs, the reasoning chain, and the conviction score | Incomplete decision records make attribution analysis impossible | Attribution failure; learning event suspended | Decision record schema check |
| INV-22 | **Temporal Integrity** | All records must have accurate, non-manipulable timestamps | Timestamp manipulation destroys temporal ordering and causal analysis | Timestamp validation failure = integrity violation | Cryptographic timestamp verification |

---

### Category IV — Risk and Safety Invariants (INV-23 to INV-31)

**Category Principle:** Risk management has primacy over return optimisation at all times. No safety mechanism may be bypassed for the purpose of capturing a return opportunity. Safety is the precondition for all other intelligence activity.

| # | Invariant | Statement | Rationale | Violation Consequence | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INV-23 | **Risk Primacy** | Risk management authority overrides return optimisation authority in every conflict | Return optimisation without risk constraint is gambling, not intelligence | Return-motivated override of risk decision = constitutional crisis | Authority hierarchy enforcement |
| INV-24 | **Kill-Switch Inviolability** | An active kill-switch condition stops all new position opens unconditionally | Kill-switch conditions represent extreme system risk; overriding them risks catastrophic loss | Any trade opened against active kill-switch = immediate investigation and reversal | Kill-switch status check at execution gate |
| INV-25 | **Position Limit Supremacy** | No position may exceed its position size limit regardless of conviction level | High conviction does not justify outsized position risk | Position blocked; alert generated | Position size check at execution gate |
| INV-26 | **Portfolio Concentration Limit** | No single sector, theme, or entity may exceed its concentration limit | Concentration risk is the most common cause of catastrophic portfolio loss | Position blocked; concentration alert | Concentration check at execution gate |
| INV-27 | **No Averaging Down Automatically** | The intelligence may not automatically add to a losing position without a new independent decision process | Averaging down without new analysis amplifies bad decisions | Auto-average blocked; new decision process required | Position direction check |
| INV-28 | **Drawdown Halt** | When portfolio drawdown exceeds the daily limit, all new positions are halted until the next trading session | Continuing to trade in a bad day multiplies losses | All new opens blocked until next session | Drawdown monitor at execution gate |
| INV-29 | **No Leveraged Betting on Uncertainty** | When uncertainty exceeds the defined threshold, position size is automatically reduced | High uncertainty requires conservative sizing regardless of conviction | Position size reduced; operator alert | Uncertainty-adjusted sizing |
| INV-30 | **Correlation Risk Control** | When portfolio correlation exceeds the defined threshold, new positions in correlated instruments are blocked | High correlation defeats diversification and amplifies systemic risk | Correlated position blocked; correlation alert | Portfolio correlation check |
| INV-31 | **Emergency Override Documentation** | Every emergency override of a safety mechanism requires documentation within 4 hours | Undocumented emergency actions are indistinguishable from violations | Alert at 4h; escalation at 8h | Emergency log audit |

---

### Category V — Learning and Evolution Invariants (INV-32 to INV-38)

**Category Principle:** The intelligence must continuously improve based on evidence. Learning must be honest, anti-fragile, and protective of historical knowledge. No learning that degrades the intelligence is acceptable.

| # | Invariant | Statement | Rationale | Violation Consequence | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INV-32 | **Outcome-Based Learning** | No lesson may be extracted before the full outcome is observed | Premature lessons confuse cause and effect | Learning event suspended until complete outcome record exists | Learning trigger gate |
| INV-33 | **Anti-Overfitting** | Every lesson must be validated against out-of-sample evidence before being incorporated as a belief | In-sample lessons are not reliable | In-sample lessons held pending OOS validation | OOS validation gate |
| INV-34 | **Anti-Catastrophic-Forgetting** | New learning may not overwrite established beliefs without meeting a defined evidence threshold | Overwriting good historical beliefs with one new data point is a catastrophic failure | Update rate capped; evidence threshold enforced | Belief update rate limiter |
| INV-35 | **Cross-Domain Propagation** | Every lesson with cross-domain relevance must be propagated to all relevant domains | Domain-isolated learning creates knowledge silos that reduce overall intelligence | Propagation completeness check required | Domain relevance assessment |
| INV-36 | **Recency Balance** | No rolling window of evidence may use a time period that over-weights the most recent observations relative to the historical distribution | Recency bias produces strategies that are optimised for the last regime, not the general case | Temporal weight audit; balance correction | Temporal weight check |
| INV-37 | **False Lesson Detection** | Every lesson must pass a false lesson detection test before being incorporated | False lessons are more dangerous than no lessons | Spurious lessons cause systematic performance degradation | Multiple-confirmation requirement |
| INV-38 | **Architecture Immutability** | Lessons may not cause changes to architectural constants or invariants — learning updates beliefs, not architecture | Architecture stability is required for consistent audit trails | Architectural change attempt blocked; escalated | Architecture change gate |

---

### Category VI — Explainability and Transparency Invariants (INV-39 to INV-44)

**Category Principle:** Every operation of the intelligence must be explainable to a knowledgeable human. Black-box behaviour is constitutionally prohibited. Transparency is not optional — it is the precondition for governance.

| # | Invariant | Statement | Rationale | Violation Consequence | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INV-39 | **No Black-Box Outputs** | Every output must include a human-readable reasoning chain linking inputs to output | Black-box outputs cannot be governed, audited, or improved | Output rejected; component investigation triggered | Output schema validation |
| INV-40 | **Reasoning Chain Completeness** | Every reasoning chain must include all reasoning steps — no step may be omitted as "obvious" | Omitted steps break audit trails | Reasoning chain rejected; reprocessed | Reasoning chain completeness check |
| INV-41 | **Evidence Citation** | Every conclusion must cite the evidence that supports it, with quality scores | Uncited conclusions are assertions, not intelligence | Conclusion rejected; evidence citation required | Evidence citation check |
| INV-42 | **Contradiction Declaration** | Every conclusion must declare any evidence that contradicts it and explain why it was outweighed | Hiding contradictory evidence is epistemic fraud | Conclusion rejected; contradiction audit | Contradiction check |
| INV-43 | **Confidence Calibration Transparency** | The calibration history of every confidence score type must be publicly available within the system | Hidden calibration allows systematic overconfidence without detection | Calibration records must be queryable | Calibration record requirement |
| INV-44 | **Override Transparency** | Every human or system override must be logged with full details — who, what, why, when | Undocumented overrides are ungovernable | Override without record = constitutional violation | Override log requirement |

---

### Category VII — Architecture and Evolution Invariants (INV-45 to INV-52)

**Category Principle:** The architecture of the intelligence may evolve, but only in accordance with constitutional processes. Evolution is constitutional, not opportunistic.

| # | Invariant | Statement | Rationale | Violation Consequence | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INV-45 | **Additive Evolution Only** | No architecture change may remove an existing capability or break an existing interface | Removal destroys the intelligence built on that capability | Removal attempt blocked; Architecture Council review triggered | Interface compatibility check |
| INV-46 | **Backward Compatibility** | All changes are backward-compatible with existing historical records | Incompatible changes make historical data unreadable | Compatibility break blocked | Backward compatibility test |
| INV-47 | **Documentation Precedes Deployment** | No architectural change is deployed without complete documentation | Undocumented changes cannot be governed or audited | Deployment blocked | Documentation gate |
| INV-48 | **Constitutional Amendment Process** | Changes to invariants or constitutional principles require the full amendment process | Informal amendment destroys constitutional authority | Change rejected; amendment process initiated | Amendment process gate |
| INV-49 | **Complexity Justification** | Every new architectural component must justify its complexity in terms of documented capability improvement | Unjustified complexity degrades reliability | New component rejected without documented justification | Complexity audit |
| INV-50 | **No Speculative Architecture** | Architectural components are added only when a specific, tested need is identified | Speculative components add debt without value | Speculative addition blocked | Need justification requirement |
| INV-51 | **Interface Stability** | Public interfaces of all components are stable — changes require full amendment process | Interface instability breaks all consumers | Interface change blocked | Interface change audit |
| INV-52 | **Version Immutability** | Once a version is deployed and has generated historical records, it is immutable | Modifying a deployed version corrupts historical records | Modification blocked; new version required | Version control gate |

---

### Category VIII — Operational Continuity Invariants (INV-53 to INV-60)

**Category Principle:** The intelligence must operate continuously and reliably. No single component failure may stop the intelligence. Continuity of operation is a constitutional requirement.

| # | Invariant | Statement | Rationale | Violation Consequence | Enforcement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| INV-53 | **No Single Point of Failure** | No single component failure may stop the entire intelligence system | Single-component dependency creates fragility incompatible with market-hours operation | Architecture review triggered; redundancy required | Resilience audit |
| INV-54 | **Fail-Safe Default** | When a component fails, the system falls to its safe state — conservative rather than aggressive | Failure-to-action is more dangerous than failure-to-inaction | Fail-safe mode activated on component failure | Fail-safe mechanism verification |
| INV-55 | **Monitoring Continuity** | Open positions must never go unmonitored for more than 60 seconds during market hours | Unmonitored positions are subject to unlimited adverse movement | Alert at 60s gap; emergency monitoring activated | Monitoring heartbeat |
| INV-56 | **Data Feed Redundancy** | At least one fallback data source must be available for all critical data types | Single data source failure cannot disable reasoning | Fallback activated; primary failure logged | Fallback activation test |
| INV-57 | **Audit Continuity** | The Audit Layer must never be suspended — audit operates continuously regardless of system state | Suspended audit allows unconstrained violations | Audit suspension = critical violation | Audit heartbeat |
| INV-58 | **Recovery Priority** | When recovering from failure, audit integrity is restored before operational capability | Operational recovery without audit integrity produces unauditable operations | Recovery sequence enforced | Recovery sequence validation |
| INV-59 | **No Silent Failure** | Every component failure is detected, logged, and escalated — no failure is silently ignored | Silent failures accumulate into systemic risk | Failure detection mechanism required for every component | Heartbeat + failure detection |
| INV-60 | **State Consistency** | After recovery from failure, system state must be validated for consistency before operations resume | Inconsistent state produces inconsistent decisions | State validation required before resuming | Pre-resume state check |


---

## PART VII — THE COMPLETE DATA / KNOWLEDGE / INTELLIGENCE FLOW

### 7.0 Overview

This part illustrates how reality is progressively transformed from raw data into actionable intelligence and, through experience, into wisdom. The transformation follows seven levels of abstraction, each elevating the preceding level into a richer, more useful form.

```
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
                    THE SEVEN LEVELS OF INTELLIGENCE ABSTRACTION
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

  Level 7: WISDOM        "What patterns endure across all market cycles?"
                ▲
                │ (long-term institutional memory + meta-learning)
  Level 6: KNOWLEDGE     "What do I believe about this market? With what confidence?"
                ▲
                │ (learning from outcomes)
  Level 5: INTELLIGENCE  "What should I do, and why, with what conviction?"
                ▲
                │ (reasoning + decision)
  Level 4: INSIGHT       "What does this pattern mean for future prices?"
                ▲
                │ (event classification + impact assessment)
  Level 3: UNDERSTANDING "What events are occurring? What entities are changing state?"
                ▲
                │ (entity + relationship updates)
  Level 2: INFORMATION   "What data is valid? What is its quality?"
                ▲
                │ (validation + quality scoring)
  Level 1: DATA          "What raw signals are arriving from the market?"

  ▼ (back through learning and memory to improve all levels)

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
```

---

### 7.1 Primary Forward Flow — Data to Intelligence

The primary forward flow transforms raw market data into investment decisions through five transformation steps:

```
  REALITY
     │
     │  (market prices, volumes, economic releases, corporate events)
     ▼
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │               DATA ACQUISITION                      │
  │  Dhan API / yfinance / Economic feeds               │
  │  Raw, unvalidated, timestamped                      │
  └──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘
                             │
                             ▼
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │               INFORMATION (Level 2)                 │
  │  INFORMATION_ONTOLOGY processes:                    │
  │  • Schema validation                                │
  │  • Source reliability scoring                       │
  │  • Timeliness verification                          │
  │  • Conflict detection                               │
  │  Output: validated data with quality score ≥ 0.6   │
  └──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘
                             │
                             ▼
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │               UNDERSTANDING (Level 3)               │
  │  ENTITY_ONTOLOGY + RELATIONSHIP_ONTOLOGY process:   │
  │  • Entity state updates                             │
  │  • Correlation coefficient updates                  │
  │  • Relationship strength changes                    │
  │  • EVENT_ONTOLOGY: event detection and classification│
  │  Output: entity graph + event stream                │
  └──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘
                             │
                             ▼
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │               INSIGHT (Level 4)                     │
  │  EVENT_ONTOLOGY + REASONING_ARCHITECTURE:           │
  │  • Event impact assessment                          │
  │  • Hypothesis generation                            │
  │  • Historical analogue retrieval                    │
  │  • Evidence collection                              │
  │  Output: investment hypotheses with conviction scores│
  └──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘
                             │
                             ▼
  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │               INTELLIGENCE (Level 5)                │
  │  DECISION_ARCHITECTURE:                             │
  │  • Five-agent debate                                │
  │  • Risk integration                                 │
  │  • Portfolio constraint check                       │
  │  • Decision formation                               │
  │  • Execution                                        │
  │  Output: approved investment decisions + trades     │
  └──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┘
                             │
                             ▼
                        MARKET ACTION
```

---

### 7.2 Feedback Flow — Outcomes to Knowledge

After execution and outcome observation, the feedback flow transforms outcomes into knowledge:

```
  MARKET ACTION
       │
       │ (position opened; monitoring begins)
       ▼
  OUTCOME OBSERVATION
       │
       │ (position closed; actual PnL computed)
       ▼
  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │              LEARNING EVENT                            │
  │  LEARNING_ARCHITECTURE processes:                      │
  │  • What happened? (outcome record)                     │
  │  • Why did it happen? (attribution analysis)           │
  │  • What can be learned? (lesson extraction)            │
  │  • Is the lesson reliable? (validation)                │
  │  Output: validated lesson + belief updates             │
  └────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │              KNOWLEDGE UPDATE (Level 6)                │
  │  Bayesian belief updates propagated to:                │
  │  • Reasoning pattern weights                           │
  │  • Entity priors                                       │
  │  • Strategy performance estimates                      │
  │  • Market regime assessments                           │
  │  Output: updated knowledge state                       │
  └────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │              MEMORY ENCODING                           │
  │  MEMORY_ARCHITECTURE 26-stage pipeline:                │
  │  • Working memory → Short-term memory                  │
  │  • Short-term memory → Long-term memory                │
  │  • Long-term memory → Institutional memory             │
  │  Output: permanent institutional memory record        │
  └────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
  IMPROVED INTELLIGENCE FOR NEXT CYCLE
```

---

### 7.3 Wisdom Formation Flow — Long-Term Accumulation

Wisdom is not produced in any single cycle. It accumulates over hundreds or thousands of cycles as patterns are validated across multiple market regimes:

```
  Cycle 1:   Event → Decision → Outcome → Learning → Memory
  Cycle 2:   Event → Decision → Outcome → Learning → Memory
  ...
  Cycle N:   Event → Decision → Outcome → Learning → Memory
                                                          │
                                              ┌───────────┘
                                              │
                                              ▼
                                    PATTERN VALIDATION
                                    (does this lesson hold
                                     across multiple regimes?)
                                              │
                                         YES  │  NO
                                         ───────────
                                              │
                                    WISDOM FORMATION (Level 7)
                                    "Principles that endure across
                                     all market conditions"
                                              │
                                              ▼
                                    CONSTITUTIONAL PRINCIPLE CANDIDATE
                                    (may eventually become IIOS invariant)
```

---

### 7.4 Information Transformation Table

| Input Level | Input State | Transformation Process | Output Level | Output State | Value |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Raw data | Unvalidated market signals | Schema validation + source check | Information | Validated signals with quality scores | Low — many signals are noise |
| Information | Validated signals | Entity/relationship update + event detection | Understanding | Entity state + events | Medium — context provided |
| Understanding | Entity state + events | Hypothesis generation + evidence collection | Insight | Investment hypotheses | High — opportunity identified |
| Insight | Investment hypotheses | Five-agent debate + risk approval | Intelligence | Approved decisions | Very high — actionable |
| Intelligence | Approved decisions | Execution + outcome observation | Experience | Outcome records | Maximum — real feedback |
| Experience | Outcome records | Attribution + lesson extraction | Knowledge | Validated beliefs | Very high — generalizable |
| Knowledge | Validated beliefs | Long-term accumulation + pattern validation | Wisdom | Enduring principles | Maximum — timeless insight |

---

### 7.5 The Intelligence Recursion

The most important property of the complete data/knowledge/intelligence flow is its recursive nature: the output of intelligence activity (outcomes) becomes the input to the knowledge layer (learning), which improves the intelligence that generates the next round of decisions.

```
               ┌──────────────────────────────────────────┐
               │         INTELLIGENCE RECURSION           │
               │                                          │
               │  Better Knowledge                        │
               │      → Better Reasoning                  │
               │          → Better Decisions              │
               │              → Better Outcomes           │
               │                  → Better Learning       │
               │                      → Better Memory     │
               │                          → Better Knowledge│
               │                              → ...        │
               │                                          │
               │  This is the engine of continuous        │
               │  self-improvement.                       │
               │                                          │
               │  Each cycle completes this loop once.    │
               │  Every completion improves the next.     │
               └──────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

The intelligence recursion is the constitutional purpose of the entire system. Every architectural decision, every governance rule, every invariant, and every constitutional principle exists to ensure that this recursion converges toward better intelligence rather than diverging toward worse.

---

## PART VIII — SYSTEM FAILURE MODES

### 8.0 Overview

A failure mode is a class of systematic malfunction that causes the intelligence to produce incorrect, misleading, or dangerous outputs. The IIOS catalogues 21 failure modes. For each failure mode, the following are defined: definition, causes, symptoms, detection mechanism, recovery protocol, mitigation strategy, isolation procedure, and escalation path.

### 8.1 Failure Mode 1 — Knowledge Corruption

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Knowledge Corruption |
| **Definition** | The knowledge base contains false, outdated, or internally inconsistent beliefs that contaminate all downstream reasoning |
| **Causes** | Data poisoning; unvalidated information accepted; belief updated on insufficient evidence; adversarial input |
| **Symptoms** | Reasoning produces systematically biased conclusions; recommendations cluster around false attractors; attribution analysis reveals systematic wrong-direction errors |
| **Detection** | Rolling belief accuracy audit; contradiction detection in knowledge base; cross-source consistency check |
| **Recovery** | Identify corrupted beliefs; trace to source; quarantine; rollback to pre-corruption state; re-derive from clean sources |
| **Mitigation** | Strict information validation; minimum evidence count for belief formation; cross-source verification requirement |
| **Isolation** | Corrupted knowledge domain is marked as unreliable; downstream reasoning flagged; decisions in affected domain suspended |
| **Escalation** | Knowledge Council; Human Principal review |

---

### 8.2 Failure Mode 2 — Reasoning Failure

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Reasoning Failure |
| **Definition** | The reasoning system produces conclusions that are not supported by the available evidence, or applies reasoning patterns inappropriately |
| **Causes** | Circular reasoning; confirmation bias in evidence collection; inappropriate pattern application; model drift |
| **Symptoms** | Conviction scores not correlated with outcomes; evidence balance consistently above 80% confirming; reasoning chain references evidence created after the hypothesis |
| **Detection** | Conviction calibration audit; evidence balance audit; reasoning chain timestamp verification; prediction accuracy tracking |
| **Recovery** | Identify specific reasoning pattern failure; retrain affected pattern; audit all recent decisions using failed pattern |
| **Mitigation** | Evidence balance requirement (INV-03); falsifiability requirement (INV-02); adversarial reasoning mandate |
| **Isolation** | Failed reasoning pattern suspended; alternative patterns substituted; conviction score flagged as potentially unreliable |
| **Escalation** | Reasoning Layer review; Architecture Council if systematic |

### 8.3 Failure Mode 3 — Decision Failure

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Decision Failure |
| **Definition** | The decision process produces decisions that are systematically wrong — either too aggressive, too conservative, or miscalibrated relative to conviction levels |
| **Causes** | Conviction threshold miscalibration; portfolio constraint implementation error; five-agent debate imbalance; risk model failure |
| **Symptoms** | Decision win rate below expected; systematic size errors; portfolio constraint violations; debate consistently unanimous (one side suppressed) |
| **Detection** | Decision performance attribution; constraint violation audit; debate diversity metrics; conviction-outcome correlation |
| **Recovery** | Recalibrate conviction threshold; fix constraint implementation; rebalance debate weights; recalibrate risk model |
| **Mitigation** | Regular conviction threshold calibration; constraint compliance audit; debate diversity monitoring |
| **Isolation** | Affected decision class suspended; decisions reviewed manually; alternative decision path activated |
| **Escalation** | Decision Layer review; Risk Council; Human Principal |

### 8.4 Failure Mode 4 — Learning Failure

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Learning Failure |
| **Definition** | The learning system fails to extract valid lessons from outcomes, or extracts lessons that are false, misattributed, or over-generalised |
| **Causes** | Attribution failure; overfitting; recency bias; insufficient sample size for lesson extraction; false correlation |
| **Symptoms** | Performance degradation despite learning events; lessons that contradict each other; belief updates in the wrong direction |
| **Detection** | Walk-forward validation of extracted lessons; belief update direction audit; out-of-sample lesson performance tracking |
| **Recovery** | Identify false lessons; roll back affected belief updates; strengthen attribution methodology; increase minimum sample size |
| **Mitigation** | Out-of-sample validation gate (INV-33); anti-overfitting protocols; minimum sample size requirements |
| **Isolation** | Learning process suspended for affected domain; manual review of recent lessons; flag affected beliefs as provisional |
| **Escalation** | Learning Council; Architecture Council |

### 8.5 Failure Mode 5 — Memory Failure

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Memory Failure |
| **Definition** | The memory system fails to encode, preserve, or retrieve learning events correctly, causing the intelligence to "forget" or misremember |
| **Causes** | Consolidation failure; retrieval index corruption; storage failure; memory TTL miscalibration |
| **Symptoms** | Analogical reasoning degrades; repeated decisions on same scenario; missing memory records; retrieval latency spike |
| **Detection** | Memory completeness audit; retrieval index integrity check; analogical reasoning quality metrics |
| **Recovery** | Rebuild retrieval index; restore from backup; trigger emergency consolidation; re-derive memory records from decision records |
| **Mitigation** | Regular consolidation cycles; storage redundancy; retrieval index integrity checks |
| **Isolation** | Memory-assisted reasoning suspended; decisions proceed with lower confidence; flagged for manual memory review |
| **Escalation** | Memory Layer review; Architecture Council |

| **Recovery** | Audit Layer restoration; constitution re-enforcement; violation remediation; governance mechanism rebuild |
| **Mitigation** | Audit Layer independence (INV-57); meta-audit schedule; Human Principal review of audit completeness |
| **Isolation** | External audit activated; all decisions held for manual review |

### 8.9 Failure Mode 9 — Overfitting

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Overfitting |
| **Definition** | The intelligence optimises its decisions for historical patterns that do not generalise — performance is excellent on historical data and poor on new data |
| **Causes** | Insufficient training data diversity; lack of regime variation in training data; over-parameterised models; insufficient regularisation |
| **Symptoms** | Excellent backtested performance; poor forward-tested performance; strategies that fail in new market regimes |
| **Detection** | Walk-forward test degradation; performance gap between in-sample and out-of-sample; regime-stratified performance |
| **Recovery** | Increase training data diversity; add regularisation; reduce model complexity; expand regime coverage |
| **Mitigation** | OOS validation gate (INV-33); walk-forward testing in ResearchLab layer; regime-diversified training |
| **Isolation** | Overfitted strategies de-promoted; fresh strategies activated |
| **Escalation** | Learning Council; Architecture Council for systemic overfitting |

### 8.10 Failure Mode 10 — Model Drift

|---|---|

| **Symptoms** | Gradual performance degradation; increasing prediction errors; conviction scores less correlated with outcomes |
| **Detection** | Rolling model performance metrics; conviction-outcome correlation decay; prediction error trend |
| **Recovery** | Retrain affected models on more recent data; update model architecture if necessary |
| **Mitigation** | Rolling retraining schedule; performance monitoring with alerts; regime change detection |
| **Isolation** | Drifted models flagged; alternative models activated; confidence in drifted model capped |
| **Escalation** | Learning Layer; Architecture Council for structural drift |

| **Symptoms** | Concepts produce different outcomes than historical patterns suggest; sector classifications produce unexpected correlations |
| **Detection** | Concept-to-outcome correlation monitoring; ENTITY_ONTOLOGY health metrics; sector correlation anomaly detection |
| **Recovery** | Update concept definitions in ENTITY_ONTOLOGY and RELATIONSHIP_ONTOLOGY; re-derive all beliefs based on updated concepts |
| **Mitigation** | Regular knowledge taxonomy review; concept validity audit; relationship graph health monitoring |

| **Isolation** | Affected data source quarantined; all beliefs from that source flagged as provisional |

### 8.13 Failure Mode 13 — Circular Reasoning

| Attribute | Value |
|---|---|
| **Name** | Circular Reasoning |
| **Definition** | The reasoning system uses a belief as evidence for itself — the conclusion is assumed in the premises |
| **Causes** | Reasoning chain construction error; hypothesis using evidence generated after the hypothesis was formed; confirmation bias |
| **Symptoms** | Evidence that predates the hypothesis is absent; reasoning chain has no independent inputs; high conviction with thin evidence |
| **Detection** | Timestamp audit on evidence vs. hypothesis creation; independent evidence source check |
| **Recovery** | Reject circular reasoning chain; require independent evidence; audit all decisions produced by circular reasoning |
| **Mitigation** | Falsifiability requirement (INV-02); evidence timestamp check; independent evidence source requirement |
| **Escalation** | Reasoning Layer; Audit Layer |

|---|---|
