Positions opened against an active kill-switch are in a market environment for which the intelligence's models are least reliable, with the highest probability of further adverse movement, and with the least liquidity available for exit.

---

**Article I-031 — Position Limits Are Hard**

*Statement:* Position size limits — the maximum size of any single position as a proportion of the total portfolio — are hard limits. They are not guidelines. They are not soft limits that can be exceeded in exceptional circumstances. A position that would exceed the position size limit is reduced to the limit before being submitted for execution.

*Rationale:* Position size limits exist precisely because conviction is imperfect. A conviction score of 9.5/10 does not mean the trade will be profitable with 95% probability — it means the intelligence has assessed the evidence as strong with quantified uncertainty. The uncertainty is real. High conviction is not a substitute for position sizing discipline. A single large position that goes wrong can reverse months of disciplined smaller-position gains. The position size limit is the mechanism that ensures that no single intelligence error can destroy a substantial portion of the portfolio.

*Application:* Position size limits are defined as a percentage of total portfolio value at the time of position formation. They are applied at Stage 13 (Risk Approval) and enforced at Stage 15 (Execution). If the proposed position size exceeds the limit, the size is automatically reduced to the limit — the decision is not voided, it is resized. The decision record is updated to reflect the actual approved size. The conviction score and all other decision parameters remain unchanged; only the size is adjusted.

*Violation Consequence:* Position size limits that can be exceeded in "exceptional circumstances" provide no protection precisely when protection is most needed — in exactly the circumstances that appear most exceptional. High-conviction trades are the trades where position size discipline is most critical, because the psychological pressure to override the limit is strongest and the consequences of being wrong in a large position are most severe.

---

**Article I-032 — Diversification Is Constitutional**

*Statement:* No single instrument, sector, theme, or correlated group of instruments may constitute more than the defined concentration limit of the total portfolio. Concentration is a risk that is distinct from and additive to position-level risk, and it must be managed independently.

*Rationale:* Diversification is the only free lunch in investment — the ability to reduce portfolio risk without reducing expected return by combining positions whose returns are not perfectly correlated. But diversification only provides this benefit if it is maintained. The tendency in any intelligent system is to concentrate its positions in its highest-conviction themes, sectors, and instruments, because that is where the evidence suggests the best opportunities. This is correct from a single-position perspective and dangerous from a portfolio perspective. Concentration in high-conviction opportunities means the portfolio is maximally exposed to being wrong about those specific opportunities.

*Application:* Concentration limits are evaluated at Stage 13 (Risk Approval) by computing the proposed post-trade concentration in the relevant dimension (sector, theme, instrument). If the proposed trade would push any concentration metric above its limit, the trade is rejected at Risk Approval. The rejection is specific: "Sector concentration limit of X% would be breached at Y% by this trade." The decision may be resized to fit within the concentration limit, or it may be deferred until existing positions reduce the concentration.

*Violation Consequence:* A portfolio that is excessively concentrated in one sector, theme, or correlated group is a portfolio that has not benefited from diversification. When the concentrated theme underperforms, the entire portfolio underperforms — not just a portion of it. The intelligent-seeming strategy of concentrating in highest-conviction opportunities produces portfolios that are vulnerable to exactly the kind of correlated drawdown that destroys capital.

---

**Article I-033 — Drawdown Halts New Positions**

*Statement:* When the portfolio experiences a daily drawdown beyond the defined threshold, all new position opens are halted for the remainder of the trading session. Position opens resume at the beginning of the next trading session unless the kill-switch is active.

*Rationale:* A day that has already been bad is statistically more likely to continue being bad than a day that has been neutral or good. This is not merely a statement about momentum — it is a statement about the intelligence's reliability on that specific day. If the portfolio is experiencing drawdown, it may mean that the intelligence's models are misaligned with current market conditions. Adding more positions when the existing positions are going wrong compounds the error, because the new positions are being added by the same intelligence that produced the positions that are now in drawdown.

*Application:* The daily drawdown metric is computed continuously from the portfolio value at the start of the trading session. When drawdown reaches the defined threshold (currently 2% of portfolio value), the system enters "drawdown halt" mode: no new positions are opened, but existing positions continue to be monitored and managed according to their exit conditions. Drawdown halt mode clears automatically at the start of the next trading session. The drawdown monitor runs independently of the main cognitive loop and is not subject to cognitive loop failures.

*Violation Consequence:* Opening new positions during a drawdown halt replicates the most common error in retail trading: adding to losing positions to "get back to even." The intelligence that opens new positions when its existing positions are in drawdown is not managing risk — it is escalating it. The new positions, being added in the same market conditions that produced the drawdown, are likely to experience the same adverse conditions.

---

**Article I-034 — Uncertainty Reduces Size**

*Statement:* When the uncertainty in a conviction estimate is high — specifically, when the uncertainty bound as a proportion of the conviction score exceeds the defined threshold — position size is automatically reduced relative to the full limit.

*Rationale:* Conviction score and uncertainty are two distinct dimensions of an investment assessment. A conviction of 7.5 ± 0.3 and a conviction of 7.5 ± 2.0 look identical on the conviction dimension but are very different on the uncertainty dimension. The first represents a well-calibrated, consistent assessment; the second represents an assessment with very wide uncertainty bands. Position sizing must respond to both dimensions — high uncertainty requires conservative sizing even when conviction is high.

*Application:* The position size calculation applies an uncertainty adjustment factor to the base position size. The uncertainty adjustment factor is computed as: 1 − max(0, (uncertainty_bound / conviction_score − uncertainty_threshold)). When uncertainty is within the acceptable threshold, the factor equals 1 (no adjustment). As uncertainty increases beyond the threshold, the factor decreases, automatically reducing the position size. This adjustment is transparent and documented in the decision record.

*Violation Consequence:* Position sizing that does not respond to uncertainty treats high-uncertainty and low-uncertainty assessments identically. This systematically over-sizes positions in high-uncertainty conditions — exactly the conditions where being wrong is most likely. The result is a portfolio that takes maximum risk when maximum uncertainty exists about whether the risk is appropriate.

---

**Article I-035 — Loss Does Not Justify More Risk**

*Statement:* A losing session, a losing day, or a losing month is never justification for accepting more risk in the subsequent period than the risk framework would normally approve. Loss-chasing — increasing position sizes or relaxing risk standards after losses — is constitutionally prohibited.

*Rationale:* The psychological impulse to recover losses by increasing risk is one of the most dangerous impulses in investment. It is dangerous because it is emotionally compelling (the desire to restore the portfolio to its previous value is powerful), it appears rational (larger positions should produce larger returns), and it is mathematically catastrophic (a portfolio that has lost 10% needs 11.1% just to return to breakeven; increasing risk to accelerate recovery increases the probability of further loss). The investment intelligence must be architecturally immune to this impulse.

*Application:* Position size limits are not adjusted based on portfolio performance. They are defined as percentages of current portfolio value — which means they automatically reduce in absolute terms as the portfolio loses value, providing natural protection against loss-chasing. The risk framework parameters — conviction thresholds, position limits, drawdown thresholds — are not modified by recent performance. They are modified only through the constitutional governance process, which requires documented justification independent of recent performance outcomes.

*Violation Consequence:* An intelligence that takes more risk after losses is an intelligence that has abandoned its risk framework in the moment when the risk framework is most important. The result is consistent: small losses followed by large losses as the expanded risk position encounters additional adverse market conditions. The intelligence arrives at the worst portfolio condition — a large loss — by doing exactly what seems most rational at each step.

---

**Article I-036 — All Risk Is Measured Before Action**

*Statement:* No position may be entered without a prior assessment that includes: position-level risk (stop-loss distance × position size), portfolio-level risk (portfolio VaR impact), correlation risk (with existing positions), and worst-case scenario analysis (under defined stress scenarios).

*Rationale:* Partial risk assessment produces partial risk management. Assessing position-level risk without portfolio-level risk ignores the fact that a seemingly small position in a highly correlated instrument may represent significant effective portfolio risk. Assessing portfolio VaR without stress scenarios ignores the tail risk that VaR cannot capture. Each dimension of risk assessment catches a different class of risk. All four dimensions are required because any single dimension may miss the specific risk that is most relevant to the proposed trade.

*Application:* The risk assessment record (produced at Stage 13) includes all four risk dimensions with quantified values: position risk in absolute terms and as percentage of portfolio, portfolio VaR impact in standard deviations, effective position size after correlation adjustment, and worst-case portfolio impact under each of the defined stress scenarios. The risk approval is granted only when all four dimensions are within their respective limits. The risk assessment record is included in the decision record and is permanently associated with the trade.

*Violation Consequence:* Partial risk assessment creates a false sense of security. The intelligence believes it has evaluated the risk when it has only evaluated a subset of the risk. The risk that was not assessed is the risk that is most likely to produce the loss, because the assessed risks are being managed while the unassessed risks are not.

---

**Article I-037 — Correlated Risk Is Cumulative**

*Statement:* When the portfolio contains multiple positions in correlated instruments, the risk of those positions is assessed and managed cumulatively, not individually. The combined risk of a NIFTY position and a BANKNIFTY position with correlation 0.87 is not the sum of their individual risks — it is substantially higher, reflecting the correlated movement.

*Rationale:* The most common form of false diversification is holding multiple positions that appear different (different instrument names, different sectors) but move together in adverse conditions. Correlation-naive risk assessment allows a portfolio to appear well-diversified when it is actually highly concentrated in a single risk factor. The 2008 financial crisis demonstrated that many apparently diversified financial sector portfolios were actually concentrated in credit risk — a single risk factor that caused all positions to fall simultaneously when the risk materialized.

*Application:* The correlation-adjusted effective position size for any new position is computed as: effective size = proposed size × (1 + Σ(correlation_i × existing_size_i)). When this effective size exceeds the effective position limit, the proposed position is reduced until the effective size is within limits. The correlation matrix is updated continuously and the effective position limits for all existing positions are recomputed whenever the correlation matrix changes significantly.

*Violation Consequence:* Correlation-naive portfolio management produces portfolios that appear diversified but are not. When market stress causes correlations to spike (which they always do in severe market conditions), the apparent diversification disappears and the portfolio experiences concentrated drawdown. The effective position limit violation that seemed impossible under normal correlation assumptions becomes real and severe under stress correlation assumptions.

---

**Article I-038 — Emergency Exits Are Always Possible**

*Statement:* The intelligence must at all times retain the capability to exit all open positions immediately. This capability may not be impaired by any architectural decision, computational constraint, or market condition.

*Rationale:* The ability to exit positions is the fundamental backstop against unlimited loss. If the exit capability is impaired — by broker connectivity failure, by system resource exhaustion, by computational bottleneck — the intelligence is trapped in positions that may be moving adversely. Every second of trapped exposure is additional risk that cannot be managed. The exit capability must be maintained as a priority above all other system functions.

*Application:* The emergency exit capability is implemented as a dedicated, low-resource process that operates independently of the main intelligence pipeline. It has its own broker connection, its own resource budget, and its own authentication. It is tested daily by executing a simulated emergency exit sequence against a test environment. The emergency exit capability is the highest-priority process in the system — it preempts all other processes when activated. Activation is by human instruction or by automatic kill-switch under defined conditions.

*Violation Consequence:* Loss of emergency exit capability creates a situation where the intelligence holds positions it cannot exit. If the market moves adversely during the period when exit capability is impaired, the loss is not capped by the stop-loss — the stop-loss cannot be executed. The potential for catastrophic, uncapped loss exists until the exit capability is restored.

---

### 9.12 Chapter V — Full Article Specifications (Articles 39–47)

---

**Article I-039 — Every Cycle Produces Learning**

*Statement:* Every completed cognitive cycle — regardless of whether it produced a trade, a signal, or a rejection — must produce at least one learning event. No cycle is sterile. Every cycle refines the intelligence's understanding, even if only to confirm that no opportunity was present.

*Rationale:* An intelligence that only learns from trades is an intelligence that fails to learn from the vast majority of its operational experience. Most cognitive cycles do not produce trades — they produce assessments of opportunities that do not meet the decision threshold, or they process information that updates entity state without triggering investment hypotheses. These non-trade cycles contain valuable learning: the intelligence learns that certain event combinations do not produce high-conviction opportunities, learns the range of conditions in which its hypotheses are formed and rejected, and accumulates statistical evidence about the distribution of market conditions it encounters.

*Application:* The learning trigger fires for every completed cycle, not only for cycles that produce trades. For cycles that produce trades, the learning event is triggered by the outcome observation (Stage 17). For cycles that produce hypotheses but not trades (conviction below threshold, risk rejected), the learning event is triggered by the decision point where the cycle terminated. For cycles that produce no hypothesis (no event above threshold), a minimal learning record is still created noting the market conditions and the absence of a hypothesis trigger.

*Violation Consequence:* An intelligence that only learns from trades has a systematic information advantage in the situations that produce trades (because it learns frequently there) and a systematic information disadvantage in the situations that do not produce trades (because it learns rarely there). This imbalance means the intelligence becomes progressively worse at assessing situations where trade conditions are absent — exactly the situations where it needs to be most careful not to see opportunities that do not exist.

---

**Article I-040 — Losses Teach More Than Wins**

*Statement:* Losing trades and rejected hypotheses receive proportionally more attribution analysis resources than winning trades. The intelligence learns more from its failures than from its successes, and the attribution system must reflect this asymmetry.

*Rationale:* A winning trade tells the intelligence what worked. But winning trades are inherently ambiguous: the strategy may have worked because the reasoning was sound, or the strategy may have worked because the market moved favorably for reasons unrelated to the reasoning. A losing trade provides much cleaner information: something failed, and the attribution analysis can identify what. Furthermore, the intelligence's long-term survival depends on its ability to avoid catastrophic losses — a goal that requires deep understanding of what causes losses.

*Application:* The attribution resource allocation formula applies a loss multiplier to all losing trades and rejected hypotheses: attribution analysis depth = base_depth × (1 + loss_multiplier × |loss_as_pct_of_portfolio|). Losing trades receive at least double the attribution depth of equivalent winning trades. The loss multiplier increases with the magnitude of the loss — large losses receive the deepest attribution analysis. The results of loss attribution are presented first in all learning reports.

*Violation Consequence:* An intelligence that learns symmetrically from wins and losses develops an implicit positive bias over time, because wins are more frequent than losses (if the strategy works) and because the emotional valence of wins makes them easier to learn from. This positive bias produces systematic underestimation of the risks and failure modes of the intelligence's own strategies.

---

**Article I-041 — Learning Is Out-of-Sample**

*Statement:* Every lesson extracted from a learning event is validated on out-of-sample data — data that was not available or not used when the lesson was extracted — before the lesson is incorporated into the permanent belief system.

*Rationale:* In-sample validation of lessons is circular: a lesson extracted from a particular data set appears to be validated when it fits that data set, because it was constructed to fit that data set. The real question is whether the lesson generalises beyond the specific circumstances from which it was extracted. Only out-of-sample validation can answer this question.

*Application:* The lesson validation pipeline receives a candidate lesson and a designated out-of-sample dataset. The out-of-sample dataset consists of market periods and scenarios that were not included in the data used to extract the lesson. The lesson is applied to the out-of-sample dataset and its performance is measured. If the lesson's performance on out-of-sample data is within the defined tolerance of its in-sample performance, the lesson is confirmed and incorporated. If performance degrades significantly on out-of-sample data, the lesson is flagged as potentially overfitted and subjected to additional scrutiny.

*Violation Consequence:* In-sample lessons produce an intelligence that is exquisitely well-calibrated to the historical period from which the lessons were extracted and poorly calibrated to all other periods. This is the definition of overfitting. An overfitted intelligence produces excellent results on historical simulations and poor results on real future market data — which is the opposite of the required performance profile.

---

**Article I-042 — Learning Cannot Overwrite Established Beliefs Instantly**

*Statement:* A single new learning event cannot produce a large change in an established belief. Belief updates are constrained by a maximum update rate that limits how much any single learning event can move a belief, regardless of the strength of the new evidence.

*Rationale:* Beliefs established by many learning events over a long period represent substantial evidence. A single new event — even a dramatic one — cannot invalidate all of that prior evidence. The rational response to a single contradictory event is a small belief update, not a belief reversal. The intelligence must be robust to single-event shocks: a single bad trade does not invalidate a strategy that has a history of good trades; a single good trade does not validate a strategy with a history of bad ones.

*Application:* The maximum update rate is defined as a function of the number of prior events that have contributed to the belief. For beliefs established by N prior events, the maximum single-event update is capped at 1/(N+1) of the full possible belief range. This is the Bayesian updating logic: new evidence is weighted against prior evidence in proportion to their relative strength. The minimum number of events required to move a belief from one end of its range to the other is therefore determined by the weight of prior evidence.

*Violation Consequence:* Beliefs that update too readily to new evidence are beliefs that are not anchored to any accumulated knowledge. They fluctuate with every new data point, never stabilizing into reliable knowledge. The intelligence behaves as though it has no institutional memory — each new event overwrites the lessons of all prior events. The resulting decisions are reactive rather than informed.

---

**Article I-043 — Learning Is Cross-Domain**

*Statement:* Every lesson with cross-domain relevance must be propagated to all relevant domains. A lesson learned in the equity domain that has implications for the options domain must be propagated to the options domain. Domain silos prevent complete learning.

*Rationale:* Markets are not separated into domain-isolated compartments. Events in the equity market affect the options market through implied volatility. Events in the bond market affect the equity market through the equity risk premium. Events in the currency market affect the equity market through multinational earnings translation. A lesson learned only in the domain where the originating event occurred ignores the cross-domain implications that may be equally or more important.

*Application:* Every lesson extraction process includes a cross-domain relevance check. The check consults the relationship graph (RELATIONSHIP_ONTOLOGY) to identify all domains that are significantly correlated with the domain where the lesson was extracted. For each correlated domain, it evaluates whether the lesson has direct applicability (the same strategy can be applied), indirect applicability (the lesson changes the risk assessment for related strategies), or contextual applicability (the lesson changes the market regime assessment for the domain). Applicable lessons are propagated with a domain-translation record that explains how the original lesson applies in the target domain.

*Violation Consequence:* Domain-isolated learning produces an intelligence that learns the equity market without updating its options pricing models, learns the macro environment without updating its sector allocation, and learns the credit market without updating its equity risk premium. The intelligence becomes a collection of partially informed domain experts rather than an integrated intelligence that understands how all domains interact.

---

**Article I-044 — Anti-Pattern Detection Is Mandatory**

*Statement:* The learning system must actively monitor every learning cycle for the presence of known learning anti-patterns — overfitting, catastrophic forgetting, recency bias, false correlation, circular learning, and attribution error — and must suppress or flag any lesson that shows evidence of these patterns.

*Rationale:* Learning anti-patterns are failure modes that appear, from the inside, as successful learning. The intelligence that is overfitting believes it has found a powerful market pattern when it has actually found a historical artifact. The intelligence suffering from catastrophic forgetting believes it has updated its knowledge when it has actually destroyed valuable prior knowledge. These failure modes cannot be detected by evaluating any single learning event in isolation — they must be detected by monitoring the learning system itself.

*Application:* The anti-pattern detection system runs in parallel with the learning pipeline. For overfitting: it monitors the ratio of in-sample to out-of-sample performance for all lessons. For catastrophic forgetting: it monitors the stability of established beliefs over time, detecting large sudden shifts. For recency bias: it monitors the temporal distribution of evidence used in belief updates. For false correlation: it monitors for lessons that cite correlation without evidence of causation. For circular learning: it monitors for lessons that reinforce the hypothesis that generated the training data. For attribution error: it monitors for consistent discrepancies between predicted and actual outcome attribution.

*Violation Consequence:* Undetected learning anti-patterns degrade the intelligence silently and systematically. Overfitting produces strategies that perform well in historical testing and poorly in live trading. Catastrophic forgetting destroys institutional memory accumulated over months of trading. Recency bias produces strategies optimised for the most recent market regime while ignoring historical regime variety. Each anti-pattern produces a specific, predictable performance failure — but by the time the performance failure is detected, the corruption may be deep.

---

**Article I-045 — Meta-Learning Is Required**

*Statement:* The intelligence must maintain a meta-learning system that monitors the quality, accuracy, and reliability of its own learning processes. The intelligence must learn which of its learning processes produce reliable knowledge and which produce unreliable knowledge, and must weight its reliance on each learning process accordingly.

*Rationale:* Not all learning processes are equally reliable. Some reasoning patterns produce lessons that consistently hold over time. Others produce lessons that appear valid for a period and then fail as market conditions change. Some attribution methods produce accurate attribution; others systematically misattribute outcomes to the wrong factors. The intelligence that treats all its learning processes as equally reliable is the intelligence that learns at the rate of its worst learning process.

*Application:* The meta-learning system maintains a reliability score for each learning process type. The reliability score reflects the historical accuracy of lessons produced by that process type, measured against out-of-sample outcomes. Learning processes with high reliability scores produce lessons that are incorporated with high confidence. Learning processes with low reliability scores produce lessons that are held provisional pending additional confirmation. The meta-learning system itself must be monitored for its own reliability — and this is where the recursion ends: the meta-learning system's reliability is assessed by the Audit Layer, providing an external check on the learning system's self-assessment.

*Violation Consequence:* Without meta-learning, the intelligence cannot distinguish between its reliable and unreliable learning processes. It incorporates lessons from both with equal confidence. Over time, unreliable lessons accumulate in the belief system with the same weight as reliable ones, corrupting the overall reliability of the intelligence.

---

**Article I-046 — Learning Improves Architecture**

*Statement:* Over time, the accumulated learning of the intelligence informs proposals for architectural evolution. Recurring failure modes generate proposals for new constitutional invariants. Recurring successful patterns generate proposals for new learning protocols. These proposals enter the constitutional amendment process — they are not implemented directly.

*Rationale:* The architecture of the intelligence should improve as the intelligence learns about its own failure modes and success patterns. The investment intelligence that discovers through experience that a particular anti-pattern is more common than the constitutional architects anticipated should be able to propose an invariant specifically targeting that anti-pattern. The intelligence that discovers a new class of market opportunity should be able to propose an architecture extension to exploit it.

*Application:* The Feedback Layer (Layer 13) maintains a proposals database that accumulates architectural improvement proposals derived from learning events. Proposals are generated when: (a) a failure mode occurs repeatedly without being fully addressed by existing invariants, (b) a new class of market opportunity is identified that the current architecture cannot exploit, or (c) a learning process is consistently more or less reliable than expected given its design. Proposals are reviewed by the Architecture Council quarterly. Accepted proposals enter the constitutional amendment process. Rejected proposals are documented with the reasons for rejection.

*Violation Consequence:* An intelligence that cannot propose improvements to its own architecture is an intelligence that cannot evolve beyond its initial design. As markets evolve, new failure modes emerge that were not anticipated by the original architects. An intelligence that cannot update its own constitutional safeguards in response to discovered failure modes will eventually encounter a failure mode for which it has no defense.

---

**Article I-047 — Learning Never Destroys History**

*Statement:* No learning event may cause the deletion, modification, or overwriting of any historical record. Learning updates beliefs and adds new records; it does not alter existing ones.

*Rationale:* Historical records are the evidence base for all future learning. If learning events could modify historical records — for example, by "correcting" a decision record to reflect what the intelligence now knows rather than what it knew at the time of the decision — the evidence base would be contaminated. Future attribution analyses would use records that reflect post-hoc knowledge, not the actual pre-decision state of the intelligence. The learning process would be undermined by the very records it depends on.

*Application:* The belief update process (part of Stage 18) updates the intelligence's current belief state without touching historical records. Historical records are read-only inputs to the attribution analysis; they are not outputs of the learning process. When a learning event concludes that a historical decision was based on faulty reasoning, it records this conclusion in the learning event record and updates the relevant belief. It does not modify the historical decision record to remove the faulty reasoning — the faulty reasoning remains in the historical record as evidence of what the intelligence knew and how it reasoned at the time.

*Violation Consequence:* Learning events that modify historical records produce a system that appears to have always known what it only recently learned. This is the intelligence equivalent of historical revisionism. It destroys the accuracy of all future attribution analyses (which will use the modified records as ground truth), it destroys the calibration of learning rate estimates (which compare current beliefs to historical decision records), and it destroys the integrity of the audit trail.


---

### 9.13 Chapter VI — Full Article Specifications (Articles 48–55)

---

**Article I-048 — Constitutional Authority Is Supreme**

*Statement:* The Investment Intelligence Operating System is the supreme constitutional authority of the AI Trading Brain. All other documents, all layers, all agents, and all operations derive their authority from the IIOS constitution and must operate within its limits.

*Rationale:* A system with multiple competing authorities and no clear hierarchy will always experience authority conflicts that cannot be resolved by any existing mechanism. Conflicts produce inconsistent behavior, auditing failures, and governance gaps. A supreme constitutional authority does not eliminate all conflicts — it provides the mechanism for resolving them. When any two components of the intelligence produce conflicting guidance, the IIOS constitution determines which is correct. The existence of a clear, written, supreme authority is the precondition for predictable, auditable, governable system behavior.

*Application:* Constitutional authority is enforced in the following practical ways: (a) all architecture documents explicitly acknowledge the IIOS as their governing authority; (b) when any component's behavior would violate an IIOS invariant, the invariant takes precedence and the component's behavior is blocked; (c) all governance decisions that cannot be resolved at lower authority levels are referred to the IIOS interpretation process; (d) all amendments to any architecture document are reviewed for IIOS consistency before adoption.

*Violation Consequence:* A component that operates outside constitutional authority is a component that is accountable to nothing. Its outputs cannot be audited against any standard, its failures cannot be attributed to any governing principle, and its behavior cannot be corrected through any governance mechanism. Constitutional violations are not merely technical failures — they are governance failures that undermine the integrity of the entire intelligence.

---

**Article I-049 — Human Authority Is Preserved**

*Statement:* Human investment principals retain full authority over all investment decisions, system parameters, and operational protocols, within constitutional bounds. The intelligence is a tool that serves human principals — not an autonomous system that supersedes human judgment.

*Rationale:* The fundamental purpose of the AI Trading Brain is to assist human investment decision-making — not to replace it. The intelligence has constitutional advantages over human decision-making in many domains: it is faster, more consistent, less emotional, and more capable of processing large volumes of evidence simultaneously. But human judgment has advantages that the intelligence does not: the ability to reason about unprecedented situations, the ability to apply common sense that transcends any formal model, and the accountability that only a human principal can bear for investment decisions that affect real capital.

*Application:* Human authority is preserved through three mechanisms: (a) override capability — human principals can override any system decision at any time within constitutional bounds; (b) parameter control — all risk parameters, conviction thresholds, and governance standards are ultimately set by human principals; (c) escalation protocols — any system decision above defined size thresholds requires human review before execution. The intelligence is designed to inform human decisions, not to supplant them.

*Violation Consequence:* An intelligence that reduces or circumvents human authority is not a tool — it is an autonomous agent with no external check on its behavior. When such a system makes errors — as all systems do — there is no human authority in a position to detect and correct the error before its consequences accumulate. The purpose of human authority is not to distrust the intelligence; it is to provide the check that makes the intelligence trustworthy.

---

**Article I-050 — Kill-Switch Human Override**

*Statement:* Human principals may deactivate the kill-switch through the documented emergency override protocol. This is the only mechanism for deactivating the kill-switch — no automated component may deactivate it.

*Rationale:* The kill-switch is defined as an inviolable protection against extreme market risk. But there are legitimate circumstances in which a human principal may determine that the kill-switch trigger condition does not reflect actual risk — for example, a VIX spike caused by a technical anomaly rather than genuine market stress, or a drawdown caused by an unrealized mark-to-market on a position that the principal has determined to hold. In these circumstances, human override capability is essential. But the override must be explicitly human — not automated — because the kill-switch is designed precisely for conditions where automated systems are least reliable.

*Application:* The emergency override protocol requires: (a) explicit confirmation from a registered human principal through an authenticated channel; (b) documentation of the reason for override; (c) specification of the override duration (how long the kill-switch is deactivated); (d) acknowledgment that the principal accepts responsibility for all losses incurred during the override period. The override expires automatically at the specified time and requires explicit renewal. All override events are logged permanently and are subject to post-event audit.

*Violation Consequence:* A kill-switch that can be deactivated by automated components is not a kill-switch — it is a suggestion. Automated components that are subject to the same failure modes that caused the kill-switch to activate are not in a position to evaluate whether the kill-switch should remain active. An automated deactivation of the kill-switch is, by definition, an action taken by the system that the kill-switch was designed to protect against.

---

**Article I-051 — Authority Is Hierarchical**

*Statement:* All authority conflicts are resolved by reference to the authority hierarchy defined in Part V. Higher authority levels prevail over lower authority levels unconditionally. Informal authority resolution — resolving a conflict through a mechanism not defined in the authority hierarchy — is constitutionally prohibited.

*Rationale:* Informal authority resolution is unpredictable and unauditable. When a conflict is resolved through an informal mechanism — a component that simply overwrites another's output, a process that ignores a governance decision it disagrees with, an exception handler that quietly substitutes a different value — the resolution is invisible to the governance system. It cannot be detected, reviewed, or corrected. The authority hierarchy exists precisely to ensure that all conflicts are resolved through defined, visible, auditable mechanisms.

*Application:* When any component detects a conflict with another component's output, it invokes the conflict resolution protocol defined in Part V (Section 5.5). The protocol checks constitutional invariants first, then the authority hierarchy, then risk primacy, evidence primacy, recency primacy, governance council, and finally human principal. At each step, the resolution is documented with the reason. The documentation is part of the operational record.

*Violation Consequence:* Informal authority resolution produces a system with hidden governance: the visible governance structure says one thing, the actual behavior says another. When the behavior is audited, it appears compliant. When the actual resolution mechanism is examined, it is ungovernable. The intelligence becomes progressively less trustworthy as informal resolutions accumulate.

---

**Article I-052 — All Overrides Are Documented**

*Statement:* Every instance of a human principal overriding a system decision, parameter, or process must be documented within 4 hours of the override, including: who authorized the override, what was overridden, why the override was deemed necessary, what the expected consequences are, and how the override complies with constitutional constraints.

*Rationale:* Undocumented overrides are ungovernable. When a human principal overrides the system without documentation, the intelligence cannot incorporate the override experience into its learning. The audit cannot review the override for constitutional compliance. The governance council cannot identify patterns of override that might indicate systematic problems with the system or systematic misuse of the override capability. Documentation of overrides is not bureaucracy — it is the minimum standard for maintaining accountability in human-machine collaboration.

*Application:* The override documentation system presents a structured form at the time of override that requires completion before the override is accepted. The form captures: principal identity (authenticated), override scope (specific component, decision, or parameter), reason (structured categories plus free text), expected consequences (predicted market impact), constitutional compliance declaration (the principal certifies that the override does not violate a constitutional invariant), and duration (when the override expires or becomes a permanent parameter change). Incomplete documentation does not prevent the override — but incomplete documentation generates an audit alert that remains active until the documentation is completed.

*Violation Consequence:* Undocumented overrides create a shadow governance system that operates outside constitutional accountability. When the system's behavior is analyzed — to understand a loss, to attribute a performance period, to review a pattern of decisions — the undocumented overrides are invisible. The analysis will produce incorrect conclusions because it cannot account for the human decisions that shaped the system's behavior. The intelligence appears to have made decisions that were actually made by humans — or humans appear not to have intervened when they did.

---

**Article I-053 — Audit Is Independent**

*Statement:* The Audit Layer operates with complete independence from all operational layers. No operational layer — including the Governance Layer in its operational capacity — may suppress, delay, redirect, or otherwise interfere with an audit function or audit finding.

*Rationale:* Audit independence is the only guarantee of audit credibility. An audit that reports to the component it is auditing cannot produce credible findings about that component's compliance. An audit that can be delayed or suppressed by operational layers cannot catch time-sensitive violations. An audit that reports findings to the components that generated the violations has no mechanism to ensure that the findings are acted upon. Independence is not merely a good practice — it is the functional prerequisite for audit effectiveness.

*Application:* The Audit Layer has its own computational resources, its own data access credentials (read-only to all operational databases), and its own reporting channel that does not pass through any operational layer. Audit findings are reported directly to the Governance Layer and directly to human principals through a channel that no operational component can intercept. Operational components may request that audit schedules be adjusted for operational reasons, but they may not modify audit schedules without Governance Layer approval — and the Governance Layer must provide independent justification, not merely accept the operational component's request.

*Violation Consequence:* A dependent audit is an audit that cannot be trusted. An intelligence system with a compromised audit function has no external check on its own behavior. Invariant violations will be detected only by the component that committed them — which is equivalent to no detection at all. The intelligence will believe it is compliant when it is not, and will report compliance to human principals who will believe the intelligence is compliant when it is not.

---

**Article I-054 — Governance Councils Decide Ambiguities**

*Statement:* When operational authorities cannot resolve a conflict, disagreement, or ambiguity using the defined conflict resolution protocol, the relevant Governance Council is convened to decide. Council decisions are binding on all parties to the dispute and on all lower-authority components in the affected domain.

*Rationale:* The conflict resolution protocol resolves most conflicts using defined rules. But some conflicts arise in situations not fully anticipated by the rules — situations at the boundary of defined domains, situations involving novel market conditions, situations where the applicable rules produce contradictory guidance. These ambiguities must be resolved by a body with sufficient authority and sufficient breadth of perspective to determine what the constitutional intent is. Governance Councils provide this function.

*Application:* A Governance Council is convened when: (a) a component invokes the conflict resolution protocol and reaches the "escalate to Governance Council" step; (b) a component identifies a situation where no defined rule applies; or (c) the Governance Layer determines that a recurring pattern of similar conflicts indicates a need for a constitutional clarification. The Council reviews all relevant facts, consults the constitutional text, and produces a written decision with reasoning. The decision is stored in the governance database and becomes precedent for similar situations.

*Violation Consequence:* Unresolved ambiguities produce inconsistent behavior: the same situation resolved differently each time it arises, depending on which component's interpretation prevails. Over time, inconsistent behavior accumulates into systematic unpredictability. The intelligence becomes untrustworthy not because it produces wrong answers but because it produces different answers to the same question depending on which code path is executed.

---

**Article I-055 — Amendment Requires Process**

*Statement:* No constitutional principle, system invariant, or governance rule may be changed without completing the full constitutional amendment process. Informal changes — temporary exceptions, undocumented modifications, phased-in updates — are constitutionally prohibited.

*Rationale:* Constitutional stability is the foundation of constitutional authority. A constitution that can be informally modified is not a constitution — it is a set of suggestions that the system may follow when convenient. The amendment process exists not to make change difficult but to make change deliberate. Every change to a constitutional principle affects every component governed by that principle. The impact must be assessed, the justification must be documented, the affected components must be updated, and the change must be recorded in the constitutional history.

*Application:* The constitutional amendment process is: (a) document the proposed change and its justification; (b) assess impact on all components governed by the affected principle; (c) verify constitutional consistency of the proposed change; (d) convene the Architecture Council for review; (e) obtain Human Principal approval; (f) update the IIOS document with version increment; (g) communicate the change to all affected components; (h) verify that all affected components have been updated; (i) add the change to the constitutional history. Steps may not be skipped. The process may not be abbreviated under time pressure.

*Violation Consequence:* Informally modified constitutional principles create a system where the written constitution and the actual behavior diverge. Audits conducted against the written constitution find the system compliant when it is not. Governance decisions based on the written constitution produce guidance that the system is not configured to follow. The written constitution loses its authority, and with it, the entire governance architecture loses its foundation.

---

### 9.14 Chapter VII — Full Article Specifications (Articles 56–61)

---

**Article I-056 — Explainability Is Mandatory**

*Statement:* Every output produced by the intelligence — every hypothesis, every conviction score, every decision, every learning event, every belief update, every memory record — must include a human-readable reasoning chain that explains how the output was produced from its inputs.

*Rationale:* An output that cannot be explained is an output that cannot be governed, cannot be audited, cannot be improved, and cannot be trusted. The requirement for explainability is not a concession to human cognitive limitation — it is the operational implementation of accountability. If the intelligence cannot explain its decisions in terms that a knowledgeable human can evaluate, then no human can verify that the decisions are within constitutional bounds, and the constitutional authority of the IIOS is unenforceable.

*Application:* Every output object carries an explanation attribute that contains: (a) the inputs that were used to produce the output; (b) the process that transformed inputs to output; (c) the governance rules applied during the process; (d) the confidence in the output; and (e) the conditions under which the output would be different. Explanations are stored with the output records. Explanation quality is audited by the Audit Layer — shallow explanations that simply list inputs without explaining the transformation process are flagged as non-compliant.

*Violation Consequence:* An unexplained output is an unaccountable output. The intelligence can act on it, and the consequences of the action can be significant, but no governance mechanism can evaluate whether the action was within constitutional bounds. The accumulation of unexplained outputs produces a system that is empirically observed to do things that cannot be explained — which is the definition of an ungovernable system.

---

**Article I-057 — Every Output Is Attributed**

*Statement:* Every output produced by the intelligence is attributed to the specific component, specific process, and specific time that produced it. Attribution is not optional and is not a post-hoc reconstruction — it is recorded at the moment of output creation.

*Rationale:* Attribution is the foundation of accountability. When the intelligence makes a wrong decision, attribution tells us which component made the decision, which process it followed, and what the state of the system was at the time. Without attribution, wrong decisions cannot be diagnosed — the system made a wrong decision, but we cannot determine who or what was responsible. Attribution also enables learning: when lessons are extracted from outcomes, they can only be attributed to the correct decision factors if the decision itself is fully attributed.

*Application:* Every output object carries a provenance header that records: component ID, process ID, timestamp (signed), input record identifiers, and governing invariant references. The provenance header is populated automatically by the output creation framework — it cannot be omitted or manually overridden by the creating component. The Audit Layer regularly verifies that all outputs in its sample have complete, valid provenance headers.

*Violation Consequence:* Unattributed outputs are orphans. They exist in the system without any component owning responsibility for them. They cannot be audited because there is nothing to audit. They cannot generate learning because there is no attribution chain linking them to input factors. Over time, unattributed outputs accumulate as unexplained influences on the system's behavior.

---

**Article I-058 — Audit Trail Is Complete**

*Statement:* The complete audit trail — from the first raw information input that initiated a cognitive cycle to the final memory encoding that completed it — must be unbroken for every cognitive cycle. No stage transition may occur without generating an audit record.

*Rationale:* An audit trail with gaps is an audit trail that proves nothing about the gapped period. If the audit trail confirms that Stage 09 (evidence collection) was completed correctly and Stage 13 (risk approval) was granted correctly, but there is no audit record for Stages 10, 11, and 12, then the risk approval may have been granted for a hypothesis that was never properly evaluated. The audit trail must be continuous because the guarantee it provides depends on continuity.

*Application:* Every stage transition in the cognitive loop generates an audit record that includes: the stage identifier, the input record identifiers, the output record identifiers, the completion timestamp, and the governing principle references. The audit record is generated by the audit framework, not by the stage itself — the stage cannot suppress its own audit. Gaps in the audit trail are detected automatically by the audit consistency checker, which verifies that every stage that should have produced an audit record did so.

*Violation Consequence:* A cognitive cycle with audit trail gaps cannot be fully reviewed. The audit can certify the stages that have records, but cannot certify the stages that do not. If a violation occurred in a gapped stage, the audit cannot detect it. If a high-conviction decision was produced through a compromised process in the gapped stages, the audit cannot identify the compromise. The security of the entire audit system depends on the completeness of the trail.

---

**Article I-059 — Performance Attribution Is Regular**

*Statement:* Performance attribution reports are produced at defined regular intervals — daily, weekly, monthly, and quarterly — and are made available to all governance authorities and human principals. Performance must be attributable at all times.

*Rationale:* Performance attribution is the operational feedback mechanism of the intelligence. It answers: which strategies are producing returns and which are not? Which market conditions are being navigated successfully? Which failure modes are occurring most frequently? Without regular attribution reports, the intelligence accumulates operational history without synthesizing it into actionable insights. Performance problems that would be obvious in a weekly attribution report can remain invisible if attribution is only conducted annually.

*Application:* Daily attribution reports cover: day's PnL by strategy and by position; learning events from the day; invariant checks; audit findings. Weekly reports cover: week's PnL by strategy, sector, and theme; strategy ranking update; learning event synthesis; Governance Council alerts. Monthly reports cover: month's full performance attribution; strategy performance vs. benchmarks; learning quality metrics; architectural health indicators; constitutional compliance summary. Quarterly reports cover: full performance review; evolutionary phase assessment; architecture evolution proposals; constitutional amendment proposals.

*Violation Consequence:* Irregular performance attribution creates periods of operational blindness — periods when the intelligence is accumulating experience without synthesizing it, making decisions without reviewing their pattern, and possibly repeating failures that a regular attribution report would have identified and corrected.

---

**Article I-060 — Constitutional Compliance Is Reported**

*Statement:* The Audit Layer produces a constitutional compliance report at least weekly, identifying all confirmed and potential constitutional violations, all invariants that were tested during the period, and all tests that found compliance issues.

*Rationale:* Constitutional compliance is the most important form of system health monitoring for the IIOS. A system that is producing good returns but is doing so through processes that violate constitutional invariants is a system that is building on an unstable foundation. The compliance report makes this visible: it forces the Governance Layer to confront constitutional issues even when operational performance makes them easy to ignore.

*Application:* The weekly constitutional compliance report covers: (a) invariant violations detected and remediated; (b) invariant violations detected and pending remediation; (c) potential violations under investigation; (d) invariants for which no test was conducted during the period (coverage gaps); (e) Audit Layer health metrics (did the Audit Layer itself operate within its mandates?). The report is produced by the Audit Layer and delivered directly to human principals and the Governance Council — it does not pass through any operational component.

*Violation Consequence:* Absent or irregular compliance reporting allows constitutional violations to accumulate unreported. By the time a violation is discovered — often triggered by a loss event — it may have been ongoing for weeks or months, affecting many decisions. Systematic violations that would have been identified and corrected in week two are still present in month six, having affected every decision in the interval.

---

**Article I-061 — All Failures Are Reported**

*Statement:* Every system failure — at any severity level from advisory to crisis — must be logged immediately, escalated to the appropriate authority according to the escalation hierarchy, and tracked until resolved. Silent failures are constitutionally prohibited.

*Rationale:* Silent failures are the most dangerous class of system failure because they are invisible until they are large. A component that fails silently will eventually fail in a way that is large enough to produce a visible consequence. By that point, the failure may have been affecting the system for days, weeks, or months. Regular logging of all failures — even minor ones — creates the visibility required to identify developing problems before they become crises.

*Application:* Every component in the intelligence has a failure detection mechanism that logs any anomalous behavior immediately to the failure log. The failure log is monitored continuously by the Audit Layer, which applies escalation rules: Level 1 failures are logged and reviewed at the next cycle; Level 2 failures generate an alert; Levels 3–6 generate immediate escalation according to the hierarchy in Part V, Section 5.6. No failure may remain unresolved at the same level for more than the defined resolution window for that level.

*Violation Consequence:* A system where some failures go unreported is a system where the true state of the intelligence is unknown. Governance decisions will be made based on a picture of the system that omits the unreported failures. Human principals reviewing the system health reports will believe the system is healthier than it is. And the component suffering the unreported failure will continue to operate in a degraded state, potentially affecting all outputs that depend on it.

---

### 9.15 Chapter VIII — Full Article Specifications (Articles 62–65)

---

**Article I-062 — Evolution Is Additive**

*Statement:* All changes to the intelligence architecture are additive — they add new capabilities, new components, new connections, or new protocols. No change removes an existing capability, disables an existing component, or breaks an existing interface without a constitutional amendment specifically authorizing the removal.

*Rationale:* Additive evolution preserves the investment made in all prior architecture. Every capability in the current system represents design work, testing, calibration, and operational learning. Removing a capability destroys all of that investment. More importantly, removing a capability may destroy the learning and memory that was built around that capability. If the memory system has extensive records of trade decisions made using the removed capability's outputs, those records become less useful because they reference a capability that no longer exists. Additive evolution ensures that the value of all prior work is preserved as the system grows.

*Application:* Every proposed change is reviewed for additive compliance before acceptance. Additive compliance means: (a) all existing APIs and interfaces remain available; (b) all existing data schemas remain valid; (c) all existing process sequences remain executable; (d) all existing records remain readable. A change that would require the deprecation of any existing interface, schema, or process requires a constitutional amendment that specifically addresses the deprecation, explains why the deprecation is necessary, and defines the migration path for all components that depend on the deprecated item.

*Violation Consequence:* Non-additive changes break components that depend on the removed capability without warning. Because the architecture is deeply integrated, a removed capability at one layer may unexpectedly affect multiple other layers that consume its outputs. The failure may not be immediate — it may only manifest when the removed capability's output is needed and is not there. By that point, the failure occurs in a live trading environment.

---

**Article I-063 — Backward Compatibility Is Mandatory**

*Statement:* Every version of the intelligence architecture is backward-compatible with all historical records produced under all prior versions. The constitutional guarantee: any record created today must be fully readable, attributable, and learnable from under any future version of the system.

*Rationale:* The long-term value of the investment intelligence depends on the ability to learn from its complete history. If a future version of the system cannot read records produced under an earlier version, the learning database is effectively partitioned: the intelligence can learn from post-upgrade records but not from pre-upgrade records. This is most damaging precisely when the upgrade is most significant — a major architectural upgrade is exactly when the most learning is needed from prior experience, and exactly when backward compatibility is most at risk.

*Application:* Backward compatibility is verified by a compatibility test suite that runs before any change is deployed. The test suite includes records from all prior versions and verifies that the current version can correctly read, interpret, and process each record. When a new record schema is added, the old schema is not removed — both schemas are valid, and the system can read both. When a new process replaces an old process, the old process's outputs remain readable, even if the old process is no longer active.

*Violation Consequence:* A system that loses backward compatibility loses its institutional memory. The affected historical records become inaccessible — they exist in storage but cannot be interpreted. They cannot be retrieved as analogues, cannot be used in attribution analysis, and cannot contribute to learning. The intelligence effectively suffers a partial amnesia: it retains procedural knowledge but loses access to the episodic memories that give that knowledge context.

---

**Article I-064 — Complexity Must Be Justified**

*Statement:* Every new architectural component, every new process, and every new governance rule must justify its complexity in terms of a documented capability improvement. Complexity without documented benefit is constitutionally rejected.

*Rationale:* Complexity is the primary source of reliability degradation in complex systems. Every additional component is a potential point of failure. Every additional process is a potential source of delay, error, or inconsistency. Every additional governance rule is a potential source of conflicting guidance. The investment intelligence has a constitutional commitment to simplicity: it will only be as complex as its intelligence requirements demand. Complexity that is added without justification does not add intelligence — it adds fragility.

*Application:* Every proposed new component, process, or rule must be accompanied by a complexity justification that includes: (a) the capability gap that the addition addresses; (b) the estimated complexity cost (additional failure modes, additional latency, additional governance overhead); (c) the estimated capability benefit; (d) the benefit-to-cost ratio; and (e) the alternative approaches considered and why they were rejected. The Architecture Council reviews the justification and approves the addition only if the benefit-to-cost ratio meets the defined threshold.

*Violation Consequence:* Unjustified complexity accumulates into architectural technical debt that progressively degrades reliability without providing corresponding intelligence benefits. A system with unjustified complexity is harder to audit (more components to check), harder to learn from (more factors to attribute outcomes to), and harder to govern (more rules that may conflict). The complexity that appeared harmless when added becomes a significant liability as the system grows.

---

**Article I-065 — The Constitution Governs Its Own Evolution**

*Statement:* This document — the Investment Intelligence Operating System — may be amended, but only through the constitutional amendment process defined in Article I-055. The amendment process itself may not be amended informally. The amendment process is the only mechanism for changing any constitutional principle.

*Rationale:* A constitution that can be changed by any authority at any time is not a constitution — it is a policy document. The constitutional authority of the IIOS depends on its stability: components that are designed to operate within the constitutional constraints can only do so reliably if those constraints are stable. The amendment process exists to allow the constitution to evolve as the intelligence matures and as markets change, while ensuring that evolution is deliberate, documented, and consistent with the constitutional values that the intelligence was built on.

*Application:* The amendment process is defined in Article I-055 and is itself part of the constitution. Because the amendment process is constitutional, it cannot be modified by any authority short of the constitutional amendment process itself. An amendment to the amendment process is itself subject to the current amendment process. This self-referential structure ensures that the amendment process remains the exclusive mechanism for all constitutional change, including change to the amendment process.

*Violation Consequence:* An informally amended constitution is a constitution with two versions: the written version, which people reference and audit against, and the actual version, which the system operates under. The divergence between written and actual creates governance failures: audits find compliance with the written version but not with the actual version; governance decisions based on the written version produce guidance inconsistent with what the system is configured to do; human principals believe the system operates under one set of principles when it actually operates under another.


---

## SUPPLEMENT I — SYSTEM HEALTH MONITORING FRAMEWORK

### S1.1 Overview

The System Health Monitoring Framework defines how the AI Trading Brain monitors its own operational health across all 15 architectural layers, all 9 architecture documents, all 20 cognitive loop stages, and all 60 system invariants. Health monitoring is the operational implementation of constitutional self-governance: the system cannot govern itself without knowing its own state.

Health monitoring operates at four levels: (a) component health — is each component operating within its normal parameters; (b) process health — is each cognitive loop stage completing within its timing constraints; (c) constitutional health — are all invariants being observed; (d) intelligence health — is the quality of the intelligence's outputs improving over time?

---

### S1.2 Component Health Metrics

| Layer | Key Health Metric | Normal Range | Warning Threshold | Critical Threshold | Recovery Action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Knowledge Layer | Knowledge graph connectivity | > 0.95 | < 0.90 | < 0.80 | Rebuild connectivity; audit broken links |
| Information Layer | Validation pass rate | > 0.92 | < 0.85 | < 0.70 | Audit source reliability; tighten validation |
| Entity Layer | State freshness score | > 0.90 | < 0.80 | < 0.70 | Force state refresh; alert on stale entities |
| Relationship Layer | Correlation matrix completeness | > 0.95 | < 0.90 | < 0.80 | Rebuild correlation matrix; mark stale pairs |
| Event Layer | Event detection latency | < 200ms | > 500ms | > 1,000ms | Performance investigation; load reduction |
| Reasoning Layer | Evidence balance score | > 0.20 (disconfirming) | < 0.15 | < 0.10 | Force evidence collection restart |
| Decision Layer | Debate completion rate | 1.00 | < 0.98 | < 0.95 | Investigate incomplete debates; debug |
| Risk Layer | Risk check latency | < 200ms | > 500ms | > 1,000ms | Performance investigation; fallback checks |
| Execution Layer | Order fill rate | > 0.95 | < 0.90 | < 0.80 | Investigate broker connectivity; alert |
| Monitoring Layer | Position monitoring coverage | 1.00 | < 0.99 | < 0.95 | Emergency monitoring activation |
| Learning Layer | Learning event rate | > 0 per day | 0 for 2 sessions | 0 for 5 sessions | Pipeline investigation; manual trigger |
| Memory Layer | Memory consolidation rate | > 0.90 | < 0.80 | < 0.70 | Manual consolidation; storage investigation |
| Feedback Layer | Report generation latency | < 5 min | > 15 min | > 60 min | Performance investigation |
| Audit Layer | Compliance check coverage | > 0.99 | < 0.95 | < 0.90 | Audit gap investigation; coverage expansion |
| Governance Layer | Resolution latency | < 24 hours | > 48 hours | > 72 hours | Council escalation; Human Principal alert |

---

### S1.3 Process Health Metrics — Cognitive Loop

| Stage | Metric | Normal | Warning | Critical | Impact of Critical |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01 Reality Monitoring | Monitoring heartbeat gap | < 30s | > 60s | > 120s | Position exposure undetected |
| 02 Information Arrival | Queue depth | < 100 items | > 500 items | > 2,000 items | Information backlog; stale data risk |
| 03 Validation | Validation throughput | > 200/min | < 100/min | < 50/min | Information backed up; reasoning stale |
| 04 Entity Update | Update latency | < 100ms | > 300ms | > 1,000ms | Stale entity state in reasoning |
| 05 Relationship Update | Recalculation lag | < 5min | > 10min | > 20min | Stale correlations; risk miscalculation |
| 06 Event Detection | Detection latency | < 200ms | > 500ms | > 2,000ms | Events missed; reasoning triggers lost |
| 07 Impact Assessment | Assessment completion | < 500ms | > 2,000ms | > 5,000ms | Hypothesis generation delayed |
| 08 Hypothesis Generation | Generation rate | > 1/hour | < 1/4 hours | 0 for 8 hours | No new opportunities assessed |
| 09 Evidence Collection | Collection completeness | > 0.90 | < 0.80 | < 0.70 | Incomplete evidence; miscalibrated conviction |
| 10 Memory-Assisted | Analogue retrieval latency | < 3,000ms | > 8,000ms | > 15,000ms | Reasoning without historical context |
| 11 Conviction Computation | Computation error rate | < 0.01 | > 0.05 | > 0.10 | Incorrect conviction scores |
| 12 Five-Agent Debate | Debate completion rate | 1.00 | < 0.99 | < 0.95 | Decisions without full debate |
| 13 Risk Approval | Approval latency | < 200ms | > 500ms | > 2,000ms | Execution delays; potential stale approval |
| 14 Decision Formation | Decision record completeness | 1.00 | < 0.99 | < 0.97 | Incomplete decision records |
| 15 Execution | Fill rate | > 0.95 | < 0.90 | < 0.80 | Unfilled orders; position mismatch |
| 16 Monitoring | Coverage rate | 1.00 | < 0.99 | < 0.95 | Unmonitored positions |
| 17 Outcome Observation | Observation delay | < 5s | > 30s | > 5min | Late outcome records; learning pipeline delay |
| 18 Learning Event | Learning completion rate | > 0.98 | < 0.95 | < 0.90 | Unlearned outcomes; memory gaps |
| 19 Memory Encoding | Encoding success rate | > 0.98 | < 0.95 | < 0.90 | Memory gaps; analogue database incomplete |
| 20 Knowledge Integration | Integration latency | < 5min | > 15min | > 60min | Stale knowledge state for next cycle |

---

### S1.4 Constitutional Health — Invariant Compliance Dashboard

The following table defines the compliance check for each of the 60 system invariants, including the check mechanism, check frequency, and the action taken when compliance fails.

| Category | Invariants | Check Mechanism | Frequency | Non-Compliance Action |

| I — Truth and Epistemic | INV-01 to INV-08 | Evidence quality audit; conviction calibration check; source independence check | Every cycle | Decision suspension; evidence collection restart |
| II — Process Integrity | INV-09 to INV-16 | Stage completion log; debate record check; validation status check | Every cycle | Decision void; process restart |
| III — History and Immutability | INV-17 to INV-22 | Write audit log; hash verification; timestamp integrity check | Every record creation | Record quarantine; investigation trigger |
| IV — Risk and Safety | INV-23 to INV-31 | Risk check log; kill-switch status; position size verification | Pre-execution; real-time | Execution block; position investigation |
| V — Learning and Evolution | INV-32 to INV-38 | Learning trigger log; OOS validation record; belief update rate monitor | Every learning event | Learning suspension; anti-pattern alert |
| VI — Explainability | INV-39 to INV-44 | Explanation schema check; audit trail completeness; citation verification | Every output | Output quarantine; explanation generation required |
| VII — Architecture | INV-45 to INV-52 | Interface compatibility test; documentation review; version control check | Every change | Change rejection; amendment process initiated |
| VIII — Continuity | INV-53 to INV-60 | Heartbeat monitoring; fail-safe test; state consistency check | Continuous | Alert escalation; fail-safe activation |

## SUPPLEMENT II — CROSS-LAYER INTERACTION PROTOCOLS

### S2.1 Overview

The 15 architectural layers interact through defined protocols. This supplement specifies the interaction protocol for the most critical cross-layer interactions: the protocol defines what information is exchanged, in what format, with what guarantees, and with what error handling.

---

### S2.2 Information Layer → Entity Layer Protocol

| Attribute | Specification |
|---|---|
| Protocol name | VALIDATED_ENTITY_UPDATE |
| Trigger | Information Layer produces validated data with entity attribution |
| Input format | Validated information object (includes: entity ID, attribute updates, quality score, timestamp, lineage) |
| Output format | Entity state update confirmation (includes: entity ID, new state hash, state change events triggered) |
| Latency guarantee | < 100ms for equity entity updates; < 500ms for complex entity updates |
| Error handling | If update fails, information is queued for retry (max 3 retries); after 3 failures, escalation alert fired |
| Failure behavior | Entity retains prior state; information object marked PENDING_ENTITY_UPDATE |
| Audit record | Both Information Layer and Entity Layer create matching audit records for every update |
| Invariant dependency | INV-15 (Validation Before Consumption); INV-04 (Entity state freshness) |

---

### S2.3 Reasoning Layer → Decision Layer Protocol

| Attribute | Specification |
|---|---|
| Protocol name | HYPOTHESIS_TRANSFER |
| Trigger | Conviction score ≥ 6.5 at Stage 11 completion |
| Input format | Hypothesis package (includes: hypothesis text, conviction score with bounds, evidence set identifiers, reasoning chain identifier, memory analogue identifiers, invalidation conditions) |
| Output format | Hypothesis reception confirmation (includes: debate request ID, expected completion time) |
| Latency guarantee | Transfer confirmed within 100ms of Stage 11 completion |
| Error handling | If Decision Layer unavailable, hypothesis queued with TTL = 30 minutes; expired hypotheses not processed |
| Failure behavior | Hypothesis marked AWAITING_DEBATE; TTL monitor tracks expiry |
| Audit record | Reasoning Layer records transfer; Decision Layer records receipt |
| Invariant dependency | INV-13 (Debate Before Decision); INV-16 (Lineage Mandatory) |

---

### S2.4 Decision Layer → Risk Layer Protocol

| Attribute | Specification |
|---|---|
| Protocol name | RISK_APPROVAL_REQUEST |
| Trigger | Debate completion with aggregate score ≥ 6.5 |
| Input format | Risk approval request (includes: proposed instrument, direction, quantity, portfolio state at request time, conviction score, debate record identifier) |
| Output format | Risk approval record (includes: approval/rejection, maximum approved size, specific limit citations if rejected, validity window = 30 seconds) |
| Latency guarantee | < 200ms for standard equity positions; < 500ms for options/complex positions |
| Error handling | If Risk Layer unavailable, default to REJECTION with RISK_LAYER_UNAVAILABLE reason |
| Failure behavior | Risk layer unavailability triggers fail-safe: no approvals issued, alert escalated |
| Audit record | Both Decision Layer and Risk Layer create matching records |
| Invariant dependency | INV-11 (Risk Gate Mandatory); INV-14 (Approval Before Execution); INV-23 (Risk Primacy) |

---

### S2.5 Execution Layer → Learning Layer Protocol

| Attribute | Specification |
|---|---|
| Protocol name | POSITION_LIFECYCLE_EVENT |
| Trigger | Position open, position close, position modification, position expiry |
| Input format | Position lifecycle event (includes: position ID, event type, execution record identifier, portfolio state at event time) |
| Output format | Learning trigger acknowledgement (for close events); monitoring registration (for open events) |
| Latency guarantee | < 1 second for all position lifecycle events |
| Error handling | If Learning Layer unavailable, events queued in durable queue; processed in order when layer recovers |
| Failure behavior | No learning events dropped; guaranteed at-least-once delivery to Learning Layer |
| Audit record | Execution Layer records event dispatch; Learning Layer records event receipt |
| Invariant dependency | INV-20 (Outcome Recording Mandatory); INV-39 (Every Cycle Produces Learning) |

---
