| Attribute | Specification |
|---|---|
| Protocol name | LEARNING_EVENT_ENCODING |
| Trigger | Learning event completion (attribution + lesson extraction + belief update all complete) |
| Input format | Learning event package (includes: learning event ID, attribution record, lesson records, belief update records, outcome record reference) |
| Output format | Memory encoding confirmation (includes: memory record ID, tier assigned, consolidation schedule) |
| Latency guarantee | Immediate record creation < 5 seconds; consolidation scheduled within 30 minutes |
| Error handling | If Memory Layer storage fails, learning event queued; retry with exponential backoff; alert after 3 failures |
| Failure behavior | Learning events preserved in durable queue; never lost |
| Audit record | Learning Layer records encoding request; Memory Layer records creation confirmation |
| Invariant dependency | INV-13 (Encoding Before Completion); INV-17 (Historical Immutability); INV-25 (Memory Is Perpetual) |

|---|---|
| Protocol name | ANALOGUE_RETRIEVAL |
| Trigger | Reasoning Layer Stage 10 (Memory-Assisted Reasoning) |
| Input format | Retrieval request (includes: situation description, key features, time horizon, market regime, maximum number of analogues) |
| Output format | Analogue set (includes: ranked list of analogues with match quality scores, outcome summaries, confidence ratings) |
| Latency guarantee | < 3,000ms for active tier; < 10,000ms for warm tier; < 30,000ms for cold tier |
| Error handling | If retrieval times out, reasoning proceeds with empty analogue set; output flagged as "no historical context" |
| Failure behavior | Reasoning operates at reduced confidence; memory-assisted reasoning stage marked incomplete |
| Audit record | Reasoning Layer records retrieval request; Memory Layer records retrieval event (read-only — does not modify records) |
| Invariant dependency | INV-26 (Recall Does Not Alter Memory); INV-10 (Memory-Assisted Reasoning documentation) |

## SUPPLEMENT III — FAILURE CASCADE ANALYSIS

### S3.1 Overview

A failure cascade occurs when a failure in one layer propagates to one or more other layers, amplifying the initial failure into a system-wide incident. The following section maps the most significant failure cascades and defines the containment protocols for each.

---

### S3.2 Data Poisoning Cascade

```
TRIGGER: Invalid data enters through Information Layer validation gap

Information Layer                 Data accepted with incorrect quality score
       │
       ▼
Entity Layer                      Entity state updated with false attributes
       │
       ▼
Relationship Layer                Correlation calculations using false entity state
       │
       ▼
Reasoning Layer                   Hypotheses generated on false premises
       │
       ▼
Decision Layer                    High-conviction decision on corrupted reasoning
       │
       ▼
Execution Layer                   Trade executed in wrong direction
       │
       ▼
LOSS EVENT
       │
       ▼
Learning Layer                    False lesson extracted from false-premise trade
       │
       ▼
Memory Layer                      False memory encoded
       │
       ▼
INSTITUTIONAL MEMORY CORRUPTION
```

**Containment Protocol:**
1. Quarantine the poisoned information source immediately
2. Identify all entity state updates that used poisoned data (Entity Layer audit)
3. Roll back entity state to pre-poisoning snapshot
4. Identify all hypotheses, decisions, and executions that used poisoned entity state
5. Audit affected trades; close positions if appropriate
6. Extract "false lesson" learning events and mark as INVALIDATED
7. Mark corresponding memory records as SUSPECT pending re-derivation
8. Rebuild entity state from clean sources
9. Full cascade impact report within 24 hours

---

### S3.3 Risk Layer Failure Cascade

```
TRIGGER: Risk Layer becomes unavailable during active decision process

Risk Layer                        Unavailable — no risk approvals possible
       │
Fail-safe protocol                Default to REJECTION for all pending approvals
       │
       ▼
Decision Layer                    All pending decisions blocked (correct fail-safe behavior)
       │
       ▼
Execution Layer                   No new executions (correct fail-safe behavior)
       │
       ▼
Monitoring Layer                  Existing positions continue to be monitored (unaffected)
       │
       ▼
Alert escalation                  Level 4 incident (constitutional violation risk)
       │
       ▼
Human Principal notification      IMMEDIATE
```

**Containment Protocol:**
1. Risk Layer failure triggers automatic fail-safe: all approvals default to REJECTED
2. No new positions opened during Risk Layer outage
3. Existing positions continue under Monitoring Layer
4. Human Principal notified immediately
5. Risk Layer restoration is Priority 1
6. After restoration, validate Risk Layer state against Monitoring Layer position records
7. All decisions pending during outage are re-evaluated from scratch (not approved retroactively)

---

### S3.4 Memory Layer Failure Cascade

```
TRIGGER: Memory Layer consolidation failure during high-volume period

Memory Layer                      Working memory full; consolidation blocked
       │
       ▼
Reasoning Layer                   Analogue retrieval returns stale results (old index)
       │
       ▼
Conviction Computation            Historical context missing; conviction uncertainty wider
       │
       ▼
Decision Layer                    Higher proportion of decisions at conviction borderline
       │
       ▼
Learning Layer                    New learning events cannot be encoded (encoding queue fills)
       │
       ▼
Learning Loop                     BREAKS — new outcomes cannot feed back into knowledge
```

**Containment Protocol:**
1. Memory Layer congestion alert at working memory > 80%
2. Trigger emergency consolidation batch at 85% utilization
3. If consolidation fails, suspend new memory encoding and alert
4. Reasoning Layer notified: operate in "reduced historical context" mode with explicit confidence reduction
5. Learning events queued in durable queue — not lost, but not yet encoded
6. Human Principal notified at Level 3 (Alert)
7. After Memory Layer recovery, process queued learning events in order
8. Verify no learning events lost; rebuild analogue index

---

### S3.5 Governance Cascade

```
TRIGGER: Constitutional violation detected by Audit Layer

Audit Layer                       Constitutional violation detected (e.g., evidence balance < 20%)
       │
       ▼
Immediate Alert                   Level 4 Incident (Constitutional Violation)
       │
       ▼
Governance Layer                  Convened immediately
       │
       ▼
Decision Layer investigation      All decisions made during violation period reviewed
       │
       ▼
Void decision determination       Decisions made in violation are constitutionally void
       │
       ▼
Execution Layer                   Voided decisions' positions reviewed
       │
       ▼
Human Principal review            Review and approve/reverse each voided position
       │
       ▼
Root cause investigation          What caused the evidence balance failure?
       │
       ▼
Constitutional remediation        Fix and document
       │
       ▼
Post-incident report              Within 24 hours
```

---

| **Decision Quality** | Decision audit completeness | Decisions with complete records / total | > 0.99 | < 0.97 | < 0.95 |
| **Learning Quality** | Learning event rate | Learning events per day | > 3 | < 1 | 0 |

| **Learning Quality** | Belief update accuracy | Belief updates in correct direction / total | > 0.70 | < 0.60 | < 0.50 |

| **Memory Quality** | Analogue retrieval quality | Relevant analogues / total retrieved | > 0.80 | < 0.70 | < 0.60 |
| **Memory Quality** | Institutional memory depth | Total retrievable memory records | > 100 | < 50 | < 20 |
| **Constitutional Quality** | Invariant compliance | Non-violated invariants / 60 | 60/60 | 58/60 | 55/60 |
| **Constitutional Quality** | Audit trail completeness | Audited cycles / total cycles | > 0.99 | < 0.97 | < 0.95 |
| **Constitutional Quality** | Override documentation rate | Documented overrides / total overrides | 1.00 | < 0.98 | < 0.95 |

### S4.3 Intelligence Maturity Model

The following maturity model assesses the overall maturity of the investment intelligence. Maturity is not the same as performance — a mature intelligence may underperform in a specific market regime while still exhibiting high-quality reasoning, well-calibrated beliefs, and effective learning.

| Maturity Level | Name | Belief Quality | Reasoning Quality | Decision Quality | Learning Quality | Memory Depth | Constitutional Compliance |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Level 1** | Initial | Uncalibrated | Incomplete chains | No risk integration | No attribution | < 20 records | > 50% invariants |
| **Level 2** | Structured | Low calibration | Basic chains | Risk gates active | Basic attribution | > 50 records | > 70% invariants |
| **Level 3** | Managed | Moderate calibration | Full chains | Five-agent debate | Full attribution | > 200 records | > 85% invariants |
| **Level 4** | Adaptive | Good calibration | Multi-pattern | Adaptive sizing | Cross-domain learning | > 500 records | > 95% invariants |
| **Level 5** | Optimising | High calibration | Memory-assisted | Regime-aware | Meta-learning active | > 1,000 records | > 99% invariants |
| **Level 6** | Institutionally Wise | Excellent calibration | Archetypical | Regime-optimised | Full meta-learning | > 5,000 records | 100% invariants |

Current AI Trading Brain maturity target: **Level 3 by end of Phase I, Level 4 by end of Phase II.**

## SUPPLEMENT V — ARCHITECTURE EVOLUTION GOVERNANCE

### S5.1 Evolution Principles

The AI Trading Brain is a living architecture — designed to evolve as market knowledge accumulates, as the learning system produces insights, and as the investment landscape changes. But evolution without governance produces architectural entropy: the accumulation of changes that individually seem reasonable but collectively degrade the coherence and reliability of the system.

The five evolution principles constrain how the architecture grows:

**Principle 1 — Evidence-Driven Evolution**
Every architectural change must be justified by evidence. The evidence may come from the learning system (a systematic pattern of suboptimal behavior that a new capability would address), from the market environment (a structural change that the current architecture is not designed to navigate), or from governance review (an identified constitutional gap that requires architectural addition). Aesthetic preferences, feature envy, and speculation about future value are not sufficient justification.

**Principle 2 — Minimum Effective Change**
The smallest architectural change that achieves the justified goal is the correct change. A change that achieves the goal plus additional improvements is a larger change than necessary — it introduces more disruption, more risk, and more governance overhead than the goal requires. Architecture evolution is not an opportunity for broad improvement; it is a targeted response to a documented need.

**Principle 3 — Interface Preservation**
All existing interfaces are preserved through every architectural change. A change that modifies an existing interface is not an evolution — it is a breaking change that requires a constitutional amendment process, an impact assessment across all consuming components, and a migration path for all affected consumers.

**Principle 4 — Constitutional Consistency**
Every architectural addition is reviewed for consistency with all 65 constitutional articles and all 60 system invariants before acceptance. An addition that is inconsistent with a constitutional principle may only proceed by amending the principle — it may not proceed by simply ignoring the principle.

**Principle 5 — Verified Rollback**
Every architectural addition is paired with a verified rollback plan. Before the addition is deployed, the rollback plan is tested in isolation to confirm that it restores the prior state correctly. If the rollback plan cannot be verified, the addition cannot be deployed.

---

### S5.2 Capability Addition Protocol

The capability addition protocol governs all new capabilities added to the AI Trading Brain. It applies to: new architecture documents, new architectural layers, new components within existing layers, new data sources, new agent types, new reasoning patterns, new memory tiers, and new governance mechanisms.

**Stage 1 — Capability Proposal**
The proposing authority (Human Principal, Architecture Council, or Governance Layer) submits a capability proposal that includes: (a) the capability description; (b) the documented need it addresses; (c) the layers and components affected; (d) the interfaces it adds or modifies; (e) the constitutional articles and invariants it implicates; (f) the estimated complexity cost; (g) the estimated capability benefit; (h) the rollback plan.

**Stage 2 — Constitutional Review**
The Architecture Council reviews the proposal for constitutional consistency. Each of the 65 constitutional articles and each of the 60 system invariants is checked against the proposed capability. Any inconsistency is documented with the specific article or invariant at issue. Inconsistencies that cannot be resolved through proposal modification require an amendment process before the capability can proceed.

**Stage 3 — Complexity Assessment**
The Architecture Council assesses the complexity cost of the proposed capability. The complexity cost is evaluated across: additional failure modes introduced, additional latency contributed, additional governance overhead created, additional learning burden imposed, and additional memory requirements. The complexity assessment produces a complexity score that is compared against the capability benefit score.

**Stage 4 — Prototype Construction**
A prototype of the proposed capability is constructed in an isolated environment. The prototype must: (a) implement the core capability; (b) preserve all existing interfaces; (c) generate the required audit records; (d) comply with the constitutional articles cited in Stage 2; (e) pass all existing test suites.

**Stage 5 — Constitutional Compliance Testing**
The prototype is subjected to constitutional compliance testing that specifically validates compliance with each constitutional article and invariant cited in Stage 2. Compliance testing is conducted by the Audit Layer, which is independent of the development team that built the prototype.

**Stage 6 — Capability Validation**
The prototype is tested against the documented need it was built to address. Does the capability actually solve the problem it was proposed to solve? Is the solution reliable under stress? Does it perform within the latency requirements of the layer it operates in?

**Stage 7 — Integration Testing**
The prototype is integrated with the full architecture in a test environment and subjected to full integration testing. Integration testing covers: all cross-layer protocols that interact with the new capability; all governance mechanisms that apply to the new capability; all audit mechanisms that must cover the new capability; all failure scenarios identified in the complexity assessment.

**Stage 8 — Human Principal Approval**
The Architecture Council presents the validated prototype to the Human Principal with: the constitutional review findings, the complexity assessment, the capability validation results, and the integration test results. The Human Principal approves or rejects the addition. Approval must be explicit — default is rejection.

**Stage 9 — Deployment**
The approved capability is deployed to the production environment following the deployment protocol in the IIOS. The deployment includes: the capability itself, updated documentation in the relevant architecture document, the rollback plan (in executable form), and updated test suites.

**Stage 10 — Post-Deployment Monitoring**
The new capability is monitored for 30 days after deployment with heightened audit coverage. The health metrics for the new capability are tracked daily. Any deviation from expected behavior triggers investigation. At the 30-day mark, a post-deployment review is submitted to the Governance Layer.

---

### S5.3 Architecture Versioning Policy

The AI Trading Brain architecture uses semantic versioning: **MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH**

| Version Component | Meaning | Change Trigger | Amendment Required? |
|---|---|---|---|
| MAJOR | Constitutional change | New constitutional chapter; removal of existing invariant; fundamental governance restructure | Yes — full amendment process |
| MINOR | Significant capability addition | New architecture document; new layer; new agent class | Yes — constitutional review + Human Principal approval |
| PATCH | Minor enhancement | New component within existing layer; new data source; parameter tuning; bug fix | No — Architecture Council review sufficient |

**Version History:**

| Version | Description | Date | Constitutional Change? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.0.0 | Foundation: 17 layers, basic cognitive loop, paper trading | Initial deployment | N/A |
| 1.1.0 | MetaLearning integration: regime-strategy mapping, kNN predictor | After 30 days | No |
| 1.2.0 | Continuous market scanning: MarketMonitor 30s cycle | After 45 days | No |
| 1.3.0 | Strategy performance tracker: auto-disable underperformers | After 60 days | No |
| 2.0.0 | IIOS constitution ratified: all 65 articles, 60 invariants, formal governance | Constitution completion | Yes |

**Planned future versions** (not yet approved — subject to full protocol):

| Version | Proposed Addition | Constitutional Impact |
|---|---|---|
| 2.1.0 | Institutional Memory Tier: decade-scale pattern storage | Part IX Article I-062 compliance review |
| 2.2.0 | Cross-Market Intelligence: global market regime integration | Part II §2.1 expansion; new entity class |
| 3.0.0 | Real-money execution: broker integration with live capital | MAJOR — risk invariants expanded; governance restructure |

---

### S5.4 Architecture Documentation Maintenance

Every architectural addition requires documentation maintenance. The documentation system is versioned alongside the architecture itself. No code change may be deployed without corresponding documentation change.

**Documentation maintenance requirements for each change type:**

| Change Type | IIOS Update | Affected Doc Update | Architecture Index Update | Invariant Index Update | Constitutional Article Update |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PATCH | Optional if relevant | Always | If new component added | If new invariant | If article text changed |
| MINOR | Always | Always | Always | If new invariants | If articles added |
| MAJOR | Always (amendment) | Always | Always | Always | Always |

## SUPPLEMENT VI — REGIME-AWARE OPERATING MODES

### S6.1 Overview

The AI Trading Brain does not operate identically across all market conditions. The MetaLearning layer (Layer 3) maintains a regime-strategy map that adapts the system's behavior based on the current market regime. This supplement specifies the operating mode for each of the six major market regimes.

---

### S6.2 Regime Operating Mode Specifications

**Regime 1 — Bull Trend (Strong Directional Upward)**

| Parameter | Normal Mode | Bull Trend Mode | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conviction threshold | 6.5 | 6.0 | Reduced friction in clear directional markets |
| Maximum position size | 5% capital | 7% capital | Higher confidence in directional signal |
| Stop-loss multiplier | 1.0x ATR | 0.8x ATR | Tighter stops; trend provides cushion |
| Profit-target multiplier | 1.0x | 1.3x | Extend winners in trending markets |
| Momentum strategy weight | Base | +30% | Momentum performs in strong trends |
| Mean-reversion weight | Base | −30% | Mean-reversion underperforms in trends |
| Options strategy bias | Neutral | Long calls / bull spreads | Trend provides clear direction |
| Holding period | Base | 1.3x base | Hold trends longer |

**Regime 2 — Bear Trend (Strong Directional Downward)**

| Parameter | Normal Mode | Bear Trend Mode | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conviction threshold | 6.5 | 6.5 | No reduction; bear markets are more volatile |
| Maximum position size | 5% capital | 4% capital | Reduced size; bear markets reverse sharply |
| Stop-loss multiplier | 1.0x ATR | 1.2x ATR | Wider stops; false recoveries common |
| Profit-target multiplier | 1.0x | 0.8x | Take profits faster; short covering reversals |
| Short strategy weight | Base | +40% | Bears are short-side markets |
| Long strategy weight | Base | −40% | Long strategies underperform in bear markets |
| Options strategy bias | Neutral | Long puts / bear spreads | Trend provides clear direction |
| Hedging weight | Base | +50% | Hedge against sharp reversals |

**Regime 3 — High Volatility Ranging**

| Parameter | Normal Mode | High Vol Ranging | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conviction threshold | 6.5 | 7.0 | Higher threshold; noisy signals |
| Maximum position size | 5% capital | 2.5% capital | Half size; high volatility amplifies losses |
| Stop-loss multiplier | 1.0x ATR | 2.0x ATR | Much wider stops; high volatility reaches normal stops |
| Kill-switch VIX level | 45 | 35 | Lower trigger; high vol regime near dangerous levels |
| Momentum strategy weight | Base | −40% | Momentum fails in ranging markets |
| Mean-reversion weight | Base | +40% | Mean-reversion excels in ranging markets |
| Options strategy bias | Neutral | Short premium / condors | High IV premiums make short premium attractive |
| Minimum evidence threshold | 3 sources | 5 sources | More evidence required in noisy markets |

**Regime 4 — Low Volatility Ranging**

| Parameter | Normal Mode | Low Vol Ranging | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conviction threshold | 6.5 | 7.0 | Higher threshold; ranging markets have lower signal quality |
| Maximum position size | 5% capital | 4% capital | Slightly reduced; ranging reduces conviction |
| Stop-loss multiplier | 1.0x ATR | 0.7x ATR | Tighter stops; low vol makes tight stops viable |
| Momentum strategy weight | Base | −30% | Low momentum in ranging markets |
| Carry strategy weight | Base | +30% | Carry / income strategies preferred |
| Options strategy bias | Neutral | Short straddles / strangles | Low IV makes short premium less attractive but theta positive |
| Minimum evidence threshold | 3 sources | 3 sources | No change; ranging but low noise |

**Regime 5 — Crisis (Black Swan / Systemic Risk)**

| Parameter | Normal Mode | Crisis Mode | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| System activation | Full | Restricted | Only defensive operations permitted |
| Kill-switch threshold | VIX > 45 OR DD > 2% | VIX > 30 OR DD > 1% | Lowered threshold; crisis requires faster response |
| New position opening | Permitted | Prohibited | No new risk in crisis |
| Existing position management | Standard | Aggressive exit | Reduce exposure immediately |
| Maximum portfolio exposure | 100% | 30% | Maximum 30% portfolio at risk in crisis |
| Human Principal alerts | Standard | Immediate | Human oversight required in crisis |
| Options strategy | All types | Long puts only | Crisis hedging only |
| Holding period | Base | 0.25x base | Exit positions 75% faster |

**Regime 6 — Event-Driven (Major Corporate / Macro Events)**

| Parameter | Normal Mode | Event-Driven Mode | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conviction threshold | 6.5 | 7.5 | Much higher threshold; events create false signals |
| Maximum position size | 5% capital | 3% capital | Reduced size; event outcomes are binary |
| Event window pre-trade ban | None | 24 hours before | No trading in 24-hour window before events |
| Event window post-ban | None | 1 hour after | Volatility too high immediately after events |
| Momentum strategy weight | Base | −50% | Pre-event momentum is often driven by speculation |
| Event-arbitrage weight | Base | +50% | Event-arbitrage strategies specifically designed for this regime |
| Options strategy bias | Neutral | Straddles / long premium | Event creates volatility regardless of direction |

---

### S6.3 Regime Transition Protocol

When the MetaLearning Layer detects a regime transition (regime change confidence > 0.80), the following protocol executes:

**Step 1 — Regime Change Alert**
MetaLearning Layer alerts the Governance Layer, Decision Layer, and Risk Layer simultaneously with the new regime classification and confidence score.

**Step 2 — Position Review**
The Monitoring Layer conducts an immediate review of all open positions against the new regime's position parameters. Any position that exceeds the new regime's maximum size or violates the new regime's strategy constraints is flagged for review.

**Step 3 — Parameter Update**
The Decision Layer updates its operating parameters to the new regime's specification. All subsequent hypotheses are evaluated under the new parameters.

**Step 4 — Risk Layer Update**
The Risk Layer updates its approval parameters to the new regime's specification. All subsequent risk approval requests are evaluated under the new parameters.

**Step 5 — Audit Record**
The regime transition is recorded as a constitutional event in the audit log, including: prior regime, new regime, confidence score, triggering conditions, parameter changes enacted.

**Step 6 — Human Principal Notification**
Human Principal is notified of the regime transition with a brief summary of the triggering conditions and the parameter changes enacted.

The AI Trading Brain comprises approximately 62 agents distributed across its 17 operational layers. This supplement provides the constitutional specification for each major agent class: its role, its governing constitutional articles and invariants, its inputs and outputs, its failure behavior, and its relationship to other agents.

---

### S8.2 Agent Class Specifications

**Agent Class 1 — GlobalIntelligenceAgent**

| Attribute | Specification |
|---|---|
| Instance count | 1 (singleton) |
| Layer | GlobalIntelligence (Layer 1) |
| Governing articles | I-001, I-002, I-003, I-015, I-053, I-056 |

## SUPPLEMENT IX — KNOWLEDGE QUALITY FRAMEWORK

### S9.1 Overview

The knowledge quality framework defines how the AI Trading Brain assesses, monitors, and improves the quality of the knowledge it holds. Knowledge quality is distinct from intelligence performance: a system can be producing good trading returns while holding knowledge of poor quality, but over time poor-quality knowledge will degrade the quality of the intelligence.

---

### S9.2 Knowledge Quality Dimensions

| Dimension | Description | Measurement | Target | Warning | Critical |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Accuracy** | Does the knowledge correctly represent the world? | Prediction accuracy of knowledge-derived beliefs | > 0.70 | < 0.60 | < 0.50 |
| **Completeness** | Does the knowledge cover all relevant domains? | Proportion of relevant domains with substantive coverage | > 0.85 | < 0.75 | < 0.60 |
| **Consistency** | Are different knowledge elements consistent with each other? | Detected contradiction rate | < 0.02 | > 0.05 | > 0.10 |
| **Currency** | Is the knowledge up to date? | Proportion of knowledge elements updated within target window | > 0.90 | < 0.80 | < 0.70 |
| **Precision** | Are beliefs stated with appropriate confidence bounds? | Proportion of beliefs with explicit uncertainty estimates | > 0.95 | < 0.85 | < 0.75 |
| **Traceability** | Can every knowledge element be traced to its evidence? | Proportion of knowledge with complete lineage | > 0.99 | < 0.97 | < 0.95 |
| **Coherence** | Do the knowledge elements form a coherent model? | Cross-domain consistency tests passing | > 0.95 | < 0.90 | < 0.80 |
| **Depth** | Is the knowledge detailed enough to support high-quality reasoning? | Average evidence count per major hypothesis domain | > 5 | < 3 | < 2 |

---

### S9.3 Knowledge Freshness Requirements

Different knowledge domains have different freshness requirements based on how rapidly the underlying reality changes.

| Knowledge Domain | Maximum Staleness | Refresh Trigger | Staleness Consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Market prices (intraday) | 30 seconds | Real-time feed | Positions may be incorrectly valued |
| Market regime | 5 minutes | 30-second scan | Strategy weights may be misaligned |
| Global context (S&P, FX, bonds) | 5 minutes | 5-minute cache | Global risk assessment may be stale |
| Entity state (corporate fundamentals) | 24 hours | Daily refresh | Entity valuations may be stale |
| Correlation matrix | 7 days | Weekly rebuild | Risk correlations may be misestimated |
| Strategy performance metrics | 24 hours | Daily EOD update | kNN predictor may use stale weights |
| Analogue database | 5 minutes (pointer) | Continuous | Analogue retrieval may miss recent events |
| Market event calendar | 24 hours | Daily refresh | Event windows may be missed |
| Regime history | Real-time | Continuous update | Regime learning may lag |
| Constitutional knowledge | Permanent | Amendment only | The constitution does not expire |

---

### S9.4 Knowledge Integrity Verification

The Knowledge Integrity Verifier runs daily and checks the following integrity conditions across all stored knowledge:

**Check 1 — Hash Integrity**
Every knowledge record has a hash of its content recorded at creation. The verifier recalculates the hash and compares it to the stored hash. Any mismatch indicates that the record has been modified since creation — a constitutional violation of INV-17 (Historical Immutability).

**Check 2 — Lineage Integrity**
Every knowledge record that cites source records must be able to resolve each citation to an existing source record. Broken citations indicate that source records have been deleted — a constitutional violation of INV-17.

**Check 3 — Cross-Consistency**
The verifier checks that related knowledge elements are mutually consistent. For example: if the regime knowledge says "current regime = Bull Trend" and the correlation knowledge says "average inter-asset correlation = 0.65", these must be consistent with the known correlation patterns of bull trend regimes.

**Check 4 — Temporal Ordering**
All knowledge records are chronologically ordered. The verifier checks that no knowledge record is temporally dependent on information that was not available at its creation time. Forward-looking knowledge (knowledge that could only have been created using future information) is a data integrity violation.

**Check 5 — Attribution Completeness**
Every knowledge record that was produced by the intelligence (rather than received from external sources) must have a complete attribution chain: which agent produced it, from which inputs, at which time, under which constitutional principles. Incomplete attribution is a violation of Article I-057.

---

### S9.5 Knowledge Base Health Report Format

The weekly knowledge base health report contains the following sections:

**Section 1 — Summary Metrics**
- Total knowledge records: active count by tier (working / episodic / semantic / procedural)
- Quality score by dimension: accuracy, completeness, consistency, currency, precision, traceability, coherence, depth
- Integrity check results: checks passed, checks flagged, checks failed
- Net knowledge change: records added, records updated, records deprecated

**Section 2 — Quality Trends**
Seven-day trend for each quality dimension. Improving trends are positive indicators; deteriorating trends require investigation. Consistently low quality in a dimension indicates a systematic problem with that dimension's measurement or maintenance process.

**Section 3 — Domain Coverage Map**
A map of which knowledge domains have strong coverage and which have gaps. Gaps are ranked by priority: high-priority gaps (in domains that the intelligence frequently reasons about) require a coverage improvement plan within 14 days.

**Section 4 — Integrity Violations**
Any integrity violations identified during the week, with: the violation type, the affected records, the investigation status, and the remediation plan. All violations must be resolved within 7 days; unresolved violations escalate to Level 3 (Alert).

**Section 5 — Recommendations**
The Knowledge Base Health Report ends with 3–5 specific recommendations for improving knowledge quality in the coming week. Recommendations are ranked by expected impact. Each recommendation includes the specific action, the expected improvement, and the component responsible for implementing it.

## SUPPLEMENT X — COMPLETE METRICS DICTIONARY

### S10.1 Performance Metrics

| Metric | Definition | Formula | Ideal Value | Acceptable Range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Win Rate | Proportion of trades with positive outcome | Winning trades / Total trades | > 0.58 | 0.50 – 1.00 |
| Risk-Adjusted Return | Return per unit of risk taken | Net PnL / Total risk deployed | > 0.6 | > 0.3 |
| Sharpe Ratio | Annualised risk-adjusted return | (Return – Risk-free) / Volatility | > 1.2 | > 0.8 |
| Maximum Drawdown | Largest peak-to-trough loss | Max(Peak – Trough) / Peak | < 8% | < 15% |
| Calmar Ratio | Return relative to max drawdown | Annualised return / Max drawdown | > 2.0 | > 1.0 |
| Average Win | Average profit per winning trade | Sum(wins) / Count(wins) | > 1.5× avg loss | > 1.0× avg loss |
| Average Loss | Average loss per losing trade | Sum(losses) / Count(losses) | Reference value | Minimize |
| Profit Factor | Ratio of gross profit to gross loss | Gross profit / Gross loss | > 1.5 | > 1.2 |
| Expectancy | Average expected profit per trade | (Win rate × avg win) – (Loss rate × avg loss) | > 0 | > 0 |
| Recovery Factor | Return relative to max drawdown period | Total return / Max drawdown | > 3.0 | > 1.5 |

### S10.2 Intelligence Quality Metrics

| Metric | Definition | Formula | Ideal Value | Acceptable Range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Conviction Calibration | Accuracy of conviction scores as probability estimates | Correlation(conviction/10, win rate) | > 0.85 | > 0.70 |
| Evidence Quality Score | Average quality of evidence underlying hypotheses | Sum(evidence quality) / Count(evidence) | > 0.80 | > 0.65 |
| Reasoning Chain Depth | Average number of inference steps per hypothesis | Sum(chain lengths) / Count(hypotheses) | > 10 | > 6 |
| Debate Diversity Index | Variance of debate agent votes (high = diverse views) | Std(debate votes) / Mean(debate votes) | > 0.25 | > 0.15 |
| Belief Update Rate | Rate at which new evidence updates existing beliefs | Belief updates / Learning events | > 0.80 | > 0.60 |
| Learning Validity Rate | Proportion of learned lessons confirmed OOS | OOS-confirmed lessons / Total lessons | > 0.85 | > 0.70 |
| Memory Utilisation | Proportion of memory retrievals that improved decisions | Memory-assisted wins / Total memory-assisted | > 0.60 | > 0.45 |
| Anti-Pattern Detection | Rate at which anti-patterns are detected and suppressed | Anti-patterns detected / Estimated anti-patterns | > 0.90 | > 0.75 |

### S10.3 Operational Metrics

| Metric | Definition | Ideal Value | Warning | Critical |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cycle Latency | End-to-end cognitive loop time | < 150ms | > 300ms | > 500ms |
| Layer Latency (standard) | Time per architectural layer | < 20ms | > 50ms | > 100ms |
| Kill-Switch Response | Time from trigger to activation | < 100ms | > 250ms | > 500ms |
| Decision Throughput | Decisions evaluated per hour | > 120 | < 60 | < 20 |
| Audit Coverage | Proportion of cycles fully audited | > 0.99 | < 0.97 | < 0.95 |
| System Uptime | Proportion of market hours operational | > 0.999 | < 0.99 | < 0.95 |
| Error Rate | Exceptions per 1,000 cycles | < 0.5 | > 2.0 | > 5.0 |
| Override Frequency | Human overrides per week | < 2 | > 5 | > 10 |
| Invariant Compliance | Invariants passing / 60 | 60/60 | < 58/60 | < 55/60 |

## CLOSING DECLARATION

This document — the **Investment Intelligence Operating System** — is the supreme constitutional authority of the AI Trading Brain.

It governs all 17 operational layers, all 62 agents, all 9 subordinate architecture documents, and all operations of the investment intelligence. Every component of the intelligence derives its purpose, its authority, and its accountability from the principles, invariants, and articles defined here.

The architecture described in this document is not merely a technical specification. It is a statement of values: that investment intelligence should reason with integrity, decide with accountability, learn with discipline, remember with fidelity, and evolve with deliberation.

The AI Trading Brain is not an autonomous system. It is a tool — a sophisticated, self-improving, constitutionally-governed tool — built to serve human investment principals. Its intelligence is in service of human judgment, not a substitute for it.

This constitution will evolve. Markets change. Intelligence improves. New capabilities become possible. But every evolution will be deliberate, constitutional, and in service of the same fundamental purpose: to help human principals make better investment decisions, with more evidence, more discipline, and more accountability than they could achieve alone.

The architecture series is complete. The constitution is ratified.

## SUPPLEMENT XI — DECISION QUALITY AUDIT FRAMEWORK

### S11.1 Purpose

Every decision the intelligence makes is auditable after the fact. The Decision Quality Audit Framework defines the standard for post-decision review: what is examined, what constitutes a high-quality decision, and what constitutes a poor-quality decision regardless of outcome.

**The central principle:** A good process with a bad outcome is still a good process. A bad process with a good outcome is still a bad process. The audit evaluates process quality, not outcome quality. Outcome quality is evaluated by the Learning System; process quality is evaluated by the Audit Layer.

---

### S11.2 Decision Quality Scorecard

| Dimension | Score 1–3 (Poor) | Score 4–6 (Acceptable) | Score 7–9 (Excellent) | Weight |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Evidence Quality** | < 3 sources; no disconfirming evidence | 3–5 sources; some disconfirming | 5+ sources; ≥20% disconfirming evidence | 25% |
| **Reasoning Chain** | Shallow; < 5 steps; no pattern labels | 5–8 steps; some patterns | 8+ steps; multiple named patterns; explicit uncertainty | 20% |
| **Debate Quality** | Any agent skipped; all agents agree | All agents present; some disagreement | Full debate; substantive dissent; minority opinion documented | 20% |
| **Risk Compliance** | Risk approval absent or incomplete | Risk approval present; minor gaps | Risk approval complete; all limits explicitly cited | 20% |
| **Explainability** | No reasoning chain for output | Partial chain; some gaps | Complete chain; every inference step justified | 15% |

**Total weighted score ≥ 7.0** = High-quality decision (eligible for learning from outcome)
**Total weighted score 5.0–6.9** = Acceptable decision (learning flagged with quality caveat)
**Total weighted score < 5.0** = Poor-quality decision (void; learning suppressed; investigation required)

---

### S11.3 Post-Decision Audit Protocol

**Step 1 — Evidence Reconstruction**
The audit retrieves the evidence set that was used to form the hypothesis underlying the decision. It verifies: (a) all cited evidence records exist; (b) evidence quality scores match the scores cited in the hypothesis; (c) the evidence was available at the time the hypothesis was formed (no forward-looking evidence).

**Step 2 — Reasoning Chain Verification**
The audit traces the reasoning chain from raw evidence to conviction score. It verifies: (a) each inference step is documented; (b) each pattern label matches a registered pattern in the Reasoning Architecture; (c) the confidence propagation through the chain is correctly calculated; (d) uncertainty is correctly widened at each step with added uncertainty.

**Step 3 — Debate Record Review**
The audit reviews the debate record. It verifies: (a) all five debate agents participated; (b) each agent's vote is accompanied by a reasoning justification; (c) the aggregate score was correctly calculated; (d) any minority dissent is recorded; (e) the debate completed within the time limit.

**Step 4 — Risk Approval Verification**
The audit reviews the risk approval record. It verifies: (a) the approval was issued by the RiskManagerAI (not self-approved by any other component); (b) the position size executed did not exceed the approved size; (c) the approval was used within its validity window; (d) all applicable limits were explicitly checked.

**Step 5 — Outcome Cross-Reference**
The audit links the decision to its outcome record. It verifies: (a) the outcome record exists; (b) the outcome was recorded within 5 minutes of position close; (c) the learning event was triggered from the correct outcome record. This step does not evaluate whether the outcome was profitable — only whether the outcome was correctly recorded and attributed.

**Step 6 — Quality Score Assignment**
Based on Steps 1–5, the audit assigns a decision quality score using the scorecard in Section S11.2. The score is stored in the decision record. The score influences how the Learning System weights the lessons extracted from this decision's outcome.

---

### S11.4 Quality-Adjusted Learning

The Learning System uses decision quality scores to weight lessons:

| Decision Quality Score | Lesson Weight | Learning Action |
|---|---|---|
| 8.0 – 9.0 | 1.3× | High-quality signal — amplify learning |
| 6.5 – 7.9 | 1.0× | Standard learning weight |
| 5.0 – 6.4 | 0.5× | Reduced weight — process uncertainty |
| 3.0 – 4.9 | 0.1× | Near-suppressed — serious process doubt |
| < 3.0 | 0.0× | Suppressed — poor process; do not learn |

This weighting ensures that the most valuable learning signal comes from the highest-quality decisions. A profitable trade made through a poor process should not teach the intelligence to replicate the poor process.

---

## SUPPLEMENT XII — TELEGRAM REPORTING FRAMEWORK

### S12.1 Overview

The AI Trading Brain communicates with the Human Principal via Telegram, providing real-time alerts, daily reports, and on-demand status queries. The Telegram interface is governed by the same constitutional principles as all other intelligence outputs: all messages must be accurate, attributed, and explainable.

---

### S12.2 Alert Message Taxonomy

| Alert Type | Trigger | Message Content | Priority |
|---|---|---|---|
| TRADE_OPENED | Position opened | Instrument, direction, size, entry price, stop, target, strategy | High |
| TRADE_CLOSED | Position closed | Instrument, direction, exit price, outcome, PnL, holding period | High |
| STOP_HIT | Stop-loss triggered | Instrument, trigger price, loss amount, portfolio impact | High |
| TARGET_HIT | Profit target triggered | Instrument, trigger price, gain amount, portfolio impact | High |
| KILL_SWITCH | Kill-switch activated | Trigger condition, VIX/loss level, positions affected, action taken | Critical |
| OVERRIDE_ACTIVATED | Human override received | Override scope, duration, acknowledging principal | Critical |
| REGIME_CHANGE | Market regime transition | Prior regime, new regime, confidence, strategy changes enacted | Medium |
| INVARIANT_VIOLATION | Constitutional violation detected | Invariant violated, component, severity, action taken | High |
| EOD_SUMMARY | Market close | Day PnL, trades count, win rate, strategy performance, learning events | Medium |
| WEEKLY_REPORT | Sunday EOD | Week PnL, win rate, strategy ranking, top learning events, upcoming events | Medium |
| HEALTH_ALERT | System health degradation | Component, metric, current value, threshold, action | High |

---

### S12.3 Command Interface

The Telegram bot accepts the following commands from authenticated Human Principals:

| Command | Response | Layer Queried |
|---|---|---|
| `/status` | System health summary: all 15 layers, latency, compliance | System Monitor |
| `/positions` | Current open positions with unrealised PnL | ExecutionEngine |
| `/pnl` | Today's and this week's PnL summary | PerformanceAnalytics |
| `/strategies` | Active strategy list with win rates and weights | MetaStrategyController |
| `/regime` | Current market regime with confidence and age | RegimeDetectorAgent |
| `/learn` | Recent learning events summary | LearningEngine |
| `/perf` | Performance metrics table (Sharpe, drawdown, win rate) | PerformanceAnalytics |
| `/risk` | Current risk state: exposure, limits, kill-switch status | RiskManagerAI |
| `/override <scope> <duration>` | Initiate human override (requires confirmation) | RiskGuardianAgent |
| `/pause` | Pause new position opening (monitoring continues) | ExecutionEngine |
| `/resume` | Resume from pause state | ExecutionEngine |
| `/kill` | Emergency kill-switch activation | RiskGuardianAgent |
| `/audit` | Latest audit summary | Audit Layer |

---

*All Telegram messages are logged to the audit trail. Commands that modify system state require explicit confirmation. The `/kill` command is the only command that executes without a confirmation step — it is designed for emergency use when every second counts.*


---

## SUPPLEMENT XIII — CONSTITUTIONAL AMENDMENT REGISTER

### S13.1 Overview

The constitutional amendment register is the official record of all amendments to the Investment Intelligence Operating System. It is maintained by the Architecture Council and is part of the permanent constitutional record of the AI Trading Brain.

---

### S13.2 Amendment Register Format

Each entry in the amendment register records:

| Field | Description |
|---|---|
| Amendment ID | Sequential identifier (AMEND-001, AMEND-002, …) |
| Date | Date the amendment was ratified |
| Ratified By | Human Principal and Architecture Council members present |
| Articles Affected | List of constitutional articles modified, added, or removed |
| Invariants Affected | List of system invariants modified, added, or removed |
| Summary | One-sentence summary of the amendment |
| Justification | Documented need that the amendment addresses |
| Impact Assessment | Layers and components affected; interfaces changed |
| Effective Date | When the amendment takes operational effect |
| Rollback Plan | How to revert to prior state if amendment causes issues |

---

## SUPPLEMENT XIV — SYSTEM DEPLOYMENT CHECKLIST

### S14.1 Pre-Deployment Checklist

Before any code change is deployed to the production environment, the following checklist must be completed in order. No item may be skipped.

**Code Quality**
- [ ] All modified files pass linting (no syntax errors)
- [ ] All modified files pass type checks (where applicable)
- [ ] No new `self.X` vs `X` constant scope bugs (see user memory: patterns.md)
- [ ] No forward references to undefined symbols
- [ ] All new class-level constants accessed as `self.CONSTANT` within methods

**Interface Preservation**
- [ ] All existing public method signatures unchanged
- [ ] All existing class names unchanged
- [ ] All existing return types preserved
- [ ] No existing import paths broken

**Constitutional Compliance**
- [ ] Change reviewed against all 60 system invariants
- [ ] Change reviewed against all 65 constitutional articles
- [ ] Change is additive (does not remove existing capability)
- [ ] Amendment process initiated if any constitutional principle is changed

**Testing**
- [ ] Modified components pass existing test suites
- [ ] Cross-layer integration tests pass
- [ ] Constitutional compliance tests pass
- [ ] Backward compatibility tests pass

**Documentation**
- [ ] Architecture document updated (if new capability added)
- [ ] IIOS architecture index updated (if new component, invariant, or article)
- [ ] Files Modified table in copilot-instructions.md updated

**Deployment**
- [ ] `git add <files>` — all modified files staged
- [ ] `git commit -m "<message>"` — descriptive commit message
- [ ] `git push origin main` — pushed to remote
- [ ] VPS deploy: `ssh -i ~/.ssh/trading_vps root@178.18.252.24 "cd /root/ai-trading-brain && git pull origin main && docker compose build --no-cache && docker compose down && docker compose up -d && sleep 8 && docker compose ps"`
- [ ] Both containers show `Up … (healthy)` in `docker compose ps` output
- [ ] Post-deploy: one full cycle verified in logs

---

### S14.2 Post-Deployment Monitoring Protocol

**Immediately after deploy (first 30 minutes):**
- Monitor cycle latency — should be < 200ms; alert if > 300ms
- Monitor error rate — should be 0; alert if any exceptions in modified path
- Monitor invariant compliance — should be 60/60; alert if any drop
- Monitor kill-switch state — should be INACTIVE; alert if triggered

**First 24 hours:**
- Daily performance metrics within expected range
- No constitutional violations detected
- All audit records complete and valid
- Learning events triggered correctly

**First 7 days:**
- Performance metrics trend stable or improving
- No new failure modes observed
- Post-deployment review submitted to Governance Layer

## SUPPLEMENT XVI — FIRST PRINCIPLES SUMMARY

The following 20 first principles summarise the essential commitments of the AI Trading Brain. They are derived from the 65 constitutional articles and 60 system invariants. They are written here not as governance rules but as plain-language reminders of what the system fundamentally is.

1. **Truth first.** The intelligence does not act on what it wants to be true. It acts only on what the evidence supports. When evidence is absent or weak, the intelligence acknowledges uncertainty rather than inventing confidence.

2. **Evidence before belief.** Every belief is grounded in independently verifiable evidence. A belief not grounded in evidence is not a belief — it is a guess. The intelligence does not guess.

3. **Disconfirm actively.** The intelligence actively seeks evidence that its hypotheses are wrong. At least 20% of the evidence considered for any hypothesis must be disconfirming. An intelligence that only seeks confirming evidence will systematically overestimate its own accuracy.

4. **Process before outcome.** The intelligence evaluates the quality of its decisions by the quality of the process, not the quality of the outcome. Good processes sometimes produce bad outcomes. Bad processes sometimes produce good outcomes. The intelligence learns from process quality.

5. **Debate before decision.** Every high-conviction hypothesis is debated by five agents before it becomes a decision. No agent may make a decision alone. Diversity of perspective is built into the decision architecture.

6. **Risk is always primary.** Capital preservation comes before capital growth. The intelligence will always choose a smaller, safer action over a larger, riskier action when uncertain. Uncertainty is the default state.

7. **Kill-switch is inviolable.** When the kill-switch is triggered, all new positions stop, no exceptions. The kill-switch cannot be bypassed by any automated component. Only a human principal may deactivate it through a documented override.

8. **Learn from every outcome.** Every position close generates a learning event. The intelligence never leaves an outcome unlearned. Learning is not optional — it is constitutional.

9. **History is permanent.** Once recorded, a historical fact is never modified. If new evidence contradicts an old fact, the new evidence is added alongside the old fact. Both records exist permanently.

10. **Memory is institutional.** The intelligence's memory is not a cache — it is an institution. It holds the accumulated experience of every market cycle, every trade, every lesson. It is the source of the intelligence's wisdom.

11. **Explainability is mandatory.** Every output — every conviction score, every decision, every learning event — comes with a complete explanation of how it was produced. The intelligence is never a black box to itself or to its principals.

12. **Authority is hierarchical.** All conflicts are resolved through the authority hierarchy. No informal resolution. No quiet exception. The hierarchy exists precisely for the conflicts that seem hardest to resolve.

13. **Humans remain sovereign.** The intelligence is a tool that serves human judgment. It augments human decision-making; it does not replace it. The human principal always has the final word.

14. **Evolution is additive.** The intelligence only grows — it never removes. Every new capability is added alongside existing capabilities. Existing interfaces, schemas, and processes are never broken.

15. **Complexity must justify itself.** Every new component must justify its complexity. The intelligence is as simple as it can be while doing what it needs to do. Complexity for its own sake is not permitted.

16. **Audit is independent.** The audit function cannot be influenced by the components it audits. Independence is the precondition for audit credibility.

17. **Governance is constitutional.** All governance decisions are made within the constitutional framework. The constitution governs the governance. There is no authority above the constitution except the amendment process.

18. **Failure is reported.** Every failure, at every severity level, is reported immediately. Silent failures are the most dangerous kind. The intelligence is designed to surface its own problems.

19. **Backward compatibility is permanent.** Any record created today can be read by any future version of the system. The intelligence never loses access to its own history.

20. **The purpose is service.** The intelligence exists to serve human investment principals — to give them better evidence, better reasoning, more disciplined decisions, and a clearer view of the market than they could achieve alone. This purpose governs every other principle.

---

*These twenty principles are not rules to be followed mechanically. They are commitments to be honoured with judgment. When a situation arises that no rule perfectly addresses, these principles are the guide: what would an intelligence that embodies these commitments do?*

*The answer to that question is always available. It requires only the discipline to ask it.*


---

> *"The investment intelligence of the AI Trading Brain is not measured by the returns it produces in any single quarter. It is measured by the quality of its reasoning, the discipline of its process, the fidelity of its memory, and the integrity of its evolution — sustained across all market conditions, through all regimes, over the long run that only patient, principled intelligence can navigate."*
